# SKILL ORIGINAL: cartoon-audio-short

# Cartoon short: idea → script → voiceover → finished MP4

The look: flat 2D bean-head cartoon characters (canvas-drawn "cards" with plum outlines) in a colourful 3D world with black ink outlines, painted textures, depth-of-field and gliding cameras. The output is 1080x1920, 30 fps, exactly as long as the voiceover. The user's voice is unchanged. The music bed and SFX are synthesized and sit at **50 % of the voice level**. Proven on "The Soldier Who Never Left" (19.7 s, 8 shots) and "The Man Who Moved a Mountain" (41.8 s, 12 shots, rural Bihar cast, map-view route lines).

## Rules
- Stories, characters and shots are always original. Never recreate another creator's video shot for shot. Offer an original take on the same idea instead.
- No real, named living people as characters, and no brand mascots or logos.
- No captions unless the user asks. Diegetic text (a carved message, a sign) is fine, and it must read correctly (not mirrored).
- Never edit or re-time the voice. The picture is timed to it, and every visual reaction lands 0-3 frames before its word.
- **Audio levels:** the music bed sits at 50 % of the voice level (−6 dB, gated RMS). SFX sit at 50 % or slightly less, with peaks capped so no hit pokes out over the narration. Always mix with `mix.py`, never by hand. Use no ducking or compression that would change this ratio; only one linear gain plus a peak limiter goes on the whole mix.
- Keep the user informed with a task list. Send a stills contact sheet before the long render.

## Stage 1: Intake (always first)
If an audio file is already attached, skip to Stage 4. If a script was also given, use it for the timing.
Otherwise ask one AskUserQuestion (or plain text if that tool is missing): **"Where are you starting from?"**
- **Voiceover ready**: ask them to upload it, plus the script text if they have it (the script makes the timing exact). Go to Stage 4 when it arrives.
- **I have a script, need the voiceover**: tighten the script per Stage 3 if needed, give it back with recording tips, ask for the upload, then go to Stage 4.
- **Need video ideas and a script**: go to Stage 2.
Also ask the target length if they haven't said. The default is 20-30 s; 45-60 s is allowed.

## Stage 2: 10 video ideas
Give exactly 10 ideas in one reply, numbered, and ask the user to reply with a number (or "more"). Each idea gets:
- **Title** (5-8 words, curiosity-driven, e.g. "The Soldier Who Never Left").
- **Hook line**: the first spoken sentence, a paradox stated as fact ("A man sold the Eiffel Tower. Twice.").
- **The twist** in one line (the ironic payoff that ends the video).
- **Why it animates well**: the 2-3 big visual moments (a collapse, a reveal, a chase).
- **True or legend**: say plainly whether it is documented history, a disputed account or folklore.

What makes an idea "perfect" for this format:
- One surprising story with a physical cause and effect you can show.
- A clear everyman character, stakes that escalate, and a twist in the last line.
- A setting that can be built from simple 3D shapes plus cartoon cards (towns, bridges, ships, castles, deserts, labs, palaces).
- Mix the 10 across eras and themes: ancient, medieval, 1800s, modern, science, animals, inventions, disasters, weird laws, lucky accidents.
- For anything presented as true, verify the key facts with a web search before offering it. Don't present a viral legend as fact. If the user wants it anyway, frame it as "legend says".
- No real living people as the butt of a joke, nothing graphic and nothing political.

## Stage 3: Script (after they pick)
Write the narration only (no stage directions in the spoken text), sized at about 3.7 words/s: 20 s ≈ 75 words, 30 s ≈ 110, 60 s ≈ 220. Deep or "soothing" ElevenLabs narrator voices run much slower, near 2.5 words/s with their pauses (103 words came out at 41.8 s with "Henry"). If the user may pick one of those, size at ~2.6 words/s or tell them the length will grow, and suggest speed 1.1. Formula:
1. **Hook** (sentence 1, no setup): the absurd fact.
2. **Setting** in 1-2 lines: when, where, who, a concrete detail.
3. **Escalation**, 2-4 beats, each bigger. Others doubt or mock. Use one exaggeration joke.
4. **Mystery or pivot**: "But when..." / "You see," introduces the real mechanism, often with an everyday analogy.
5. **Twist / payoff**: short, ironic, and the last words of the video.
Use plain spoken English, contractions, sentences around 8-15 words, and numbers written as words. Never describe what the viewer already sees.

Then reply with:
- The script in a copyable block, with the word count and estimated length.
- A one-paragraph visual plan (the shots you'll animate).
- Recording tips: one steady, warm narrator voice; ElevenLabs works well (a deep narrator voice, speed about 1.05-1.1, stability about 45-55 %). Mp3 or wav is fine. Leave natural pauses at sentence ends. Export without music.
- The ask: **"Upload the voiceover when it's ready and I'll make the full video."**
Keep the final script text. It drives the timing in Stage 4, so no speech recognition is needed. If they recorded a changed version, ask for the final text.

## Stage 4: Build the video (after the voiceover is uploaded)
Do everything from here without stopping. Ask only if the timing cannot be resolved.

### 4.0 Get the kit
The code kit ships **inside this skill folder** at `kit/` (engine, tools, the Mountain Man worked example, and `engine/web/toonbase.js`, the `toon.ts` Pen library from github.com/mromidev/claude-animated-shorts with its types already stripped, MIT). Locate the folder with `find / -name SKILL.md -path "*cartoon-audio-short*" 2>/dev/null` and treat its `kit/` as read-only.

Then run `bash <skill>/kit/tools/setup.sh ~/shorts/<slug>`. It copies the engine and tools into the project, installs three.js r160 with npm, builds `web/toon.js` (`toonbase.js` + `cast.js`), and links the global Playwright. Only the three.js install needs the network.
- Requirements: Node 22.6+, Python 3 with numpy + Pillow, ffmpeg, and Playwright with Chromium (`npm i -g playwright && npx playwright install chromium` if missing).
- If package registries are blocked, git over GitHub usually works. Never disable TLS or route around the proxy.
- Chromium: use the pre-installed Playwright browser with `--use-angle=swiftshader --enable-unsafe-swiftshader --ignore-gpu-blocklist`. That gives CPU WebGL at about 3-10 s per frame (≈3.5 s on 2 cores, so a 42 s short is ~75 min of rendering).
- **If `kit/` is missing** (a synced copy of the skill can carry only this SKILL.md), tell the user in one line and rebuild it: `git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/mromidev/claude-animated-shorts` for `templates/bridge/toon.ts` (strip types with `module.stripTypeScriptTypes` → `toonbase.js`) and `templates/audio/{dsp,instruments,sfx_gen}.py`, `npm i three@0.160.0`, then write the engine files listed under Architecture, keeping every Engine gotcha. Keep the rebuilt kit and offer it to the user as a zip so it can go back into the skill folder.

### 4.1 Timing
Run `python3 timing.py voice.mp3 script.txt --out work`.
- With whisper or faster-whisper installed, it uses word timestamps.
- Otherwise it aligns script phrases (split at punctuation) to silence-detected speech segments, with a DP that matches syllables to duration and penalises sentence ends inside a segment and long gaps inside a phrase.
- With no ASR and no script, ask for the script. Claude cannot hear audio.
- Check `work/phrases.json`: pauses should fall at sentence ends. Total frames = round(duration × 30).

### 4.2 Shot plan (`work/PLAN.md` before code)
Make one row per shot: frames, words, setting, light, camera move, characters/poses/expressions, effects and SFX cue.
- Frame 0 is already the hook image.
- Usually one shot per phrase. Merge tiny phrases into the next shot.
- Illustrate every sentence literally. Props carry the jokes.
- Vary shot sizes: hook MCU → wide reveal → two-shot → insert close-up on the key object → reaction close-up → environment move for the payoff.
- Use light for beats: day for normal, golden for decline or drama, night-blue for explanation. A time jump gets a 6-frame white flash.
- The twist line gets a sting (music stops, low hit, push-in with roll). The last shot's physical payoff lands on the final word.
- Distances, routes and "before vs after" numbers: use an aerial map view (camera high, pitched ~75° down, the whole route inside the frame width) with a dashed route ribbon that reveals along its length. Red for the long or old way, green for the new way, and repeat the same map angle for the callback.
- Sad beats are never played for laughs: on a fall or an accident, follow the dropped prop (pot, hat, letter) and keep the person out of frame within ~6 frames.
- Keep characters, dress and setting true to the story's place and period (e.g. rural India: dhoti, kurta or vest, gamchha pagri, sari with a head pallu, bare feet, mud houses with khapra tile roofs, palms), and add the outfit fields to the cast file instead of forcing an existing set.

### 4.3 World (`web/main.js`)
Keep the engine sections: toon materials, ink contract, `add`/`tbox`, sky, lights, cards, render targets/post, `applyLight`, `drawActors`, `swapForNd`, `renderFrame`. Replace the example world between `// ---------- world ----------` and `// ---------- cards ----------`, plus the story parts of `worldState()`.
- Build from primitives with painted canvas textures (`paint.js`: planks, beams, rotten wood, stone, moss/ivy, grass, dirt, plaster, roof tiles, marble, banners, carved text). Write new painters in the same flat-with-dark-seams style.
- Use `tbox` for constant texel density, a 2-step toon gradient, and fog = horizon colour.
- Background: hills (half-buried spheres), mountains (cones + snow caps), cypress/pine trees, distant houses, painted cloud billboards.
- Set pieces (collapses, falls, doors) are pivoted groups driven by 0..1 progress from `worldAt(f)`, with eased and staggered parts.
- Curved diegetic text uses planar UVs. Check it isn't mirrored.

### 4.4 Cast (`web/cast.js` + `web/castdefs.js` → `web/toon.js`)
- `bodyFrontX(p, outfit, opts)` (and `bodySideX` for profiles) draws on a 600x1000 design canvas with soles at y=975. The card is 2.25 m tall, so 1 unit = 0.00225 m.
- Outfits are plain objects in `castdefs.js` (fields such as top, kurta, short, dhoti, shorts, sari, border, pagri, hair, beard, beardSize, moustache, skin, barefoot, build). Add new ones there, and new garments, headgear and props to `cast.js` in the same style.
- Arm poses: hang, spear, point, up, wave, hip, belly, temple (+swirl), lift, reach (+lift), cheek, shield, cross, thumb.
- Expressions: the toon.ts FACE set plus snore, stubborn, squint. Extend FACE for more.
- Rebuild after edits with `cat web/toonbase.js web/cast.js > web/toon.js`. Render a character test sheet before using new designs.
- Billboards turn on yaw only, get a contact blob and an alpha-tested shadow, and are never inked by the world pass.
- **Card depth pull**: a yaw billboard next to a wall slices into it and the swing or prop disappears inside the rock. Move each card's vertices toward the camera in view space (`mv.xyz *= (d - pull) / d`, which keeps its screen size) in both the colour material and the normal/depth card material: pull ≈ 0.9 m for action cards against walls, 0.3 m otherwise. Pull FX hit points toward the camera by the same amount.
- **Side-view cards need a side camera**: keep |dot(facing, camera right)| ≥ ~0.4, or a profile walking "into" the screen reads wrong. In corridors or cuts, shoot diagonally (~40° off the corridor axis), with the hammer, tool or gaze pointing at screen-right where the target is.
- Wide action poses (a two-handed swing) need a wider card (1000x1150 design units, body centred, soles at DH-25) so the prop never leaves the canvas.
- Build card keys from `q()`-rounded values (so the canvas redraws only on visible change).
- A detached part (e.g. a lifted helmet) is its own card, aligned with `headCenter(seed)`: y = DECK + (partY − hc) × 0.00225.

### 4.5 Motion: smooth and alive (`web/motion.js`)
- **Cameras**: `camFromKeys(keys, f, {kicks})`. Use smootherstep glides (18-45 f), `easeOut` for reveals and whips, and `path` via-points (Catmull-Rom) for curved moves. Every hold creeps and nothing is static for 4+ frames. Use 3-5° rolls on drama and 7-frame flip-shakes on impacts.
- **Continuity**: prefer continuous moves (pull back from a close-up into the wide, rack focus, crane through the deck, push into an object). Hard cut only on place or time jumps, with a flash.
- **Characters never freeze**: apply `idle(f, seed)` to every card, `blinkAt` blinks, and head bobs on stressed words.
- **Actions**: anticipation → `back` overshoot → `spring` settle. A stamp is a 2-frame dip + dust puff + kick. Laughs are an alternating tilt + bob.
- **Secondary motion**: banner cloth waves, leaves/dust/drips/Zzz effect cards, flowing water. Something always moves.
- **Walks**: `walk()` gives distance + step phase. Feet planted, bob 3-7 %.
- **Lens**: DOF on close-ups and mediums (cInf 8-14 px), focus on the eyes. Wides are sharp. Racks take ~14 f.

### 4.6 Look check (before the full render)
Render stills at each shot's middle plus the transitions and key actions: `node render.mjs stills/a "5,40,..."` then `python3 sheet.py stills/a stills/sheet_a.jpg`. Look at the sheet and fix these traps:
- **Narrow vertical frame**: horizontal half-width = d·tan(hfov/2), with hfov = 2·atan(tan(vfov/2)·0.5625). At 4 m and fov 40 that is ±0.8 m, so stage characters in depth.
- Railings or posts across faces (camera inside the rail or ~2.5 m up). The camera path through houses, roofs or bushes. Foreground props under ~2 m away. The hero hidden behind a pillar.
- Mirrored text. A detached-part card offset the wrong way.
- Extrude material groups: 0 = caps, 1 = sides+top.
- Missing ink: debug the edge-only output (`window.INKDBG`) and read back the normal/depth target with `readRenderTargetPixels`. Ink width ~5 px near → ~3.2 px far at 1080 (2.5 px is too thin and DOF washes it out). The sky counts as far depth, not a card.
- Camera inside or behind terrain: clamp the camera above `groundAt(x, z) + 1.2`, and check that the line of sight to the hero clears the near slope (a low camera outside a cut or notch is blocked by its lip).
- Groups of 3-4 characters don't fit side by side in a vertical frame: stagger them in depth (front ~5 m, back ~10 m), each within the frame's half-width at its distance, and keep the gag gesture on a fully visible character.
- Repeating ground tiles in wides: fade texture detail to its mip average beyond ~70-320 m. A two-stop blue→peach sky turns purple, so use a three-stop sky (top / mid / horizon).
- Before trusting a framing, project key points through the camera with three.js in Node (`Vector3.project`) to check that everything is inside 0..1.
Send the sheet to the user with a one-line shot summary, then start the full render.

### 4.7 Sound (`audio.py` → stems → `mix.py`)
Rewrite the cue table for the story; the synths stay. `audio.py` writes only stems (`music.wav`, `sfx.wav`).
- Music: a short march-like loop (kick/snare, plucked bass, brass pad). It stops at the twist line, then a low drone continues.
- SFX on events (in frames/30): whooshes on big moves, thunk on stamps, rumble+crash on collapses, scrape on metal, ting on reveals, sting on the twist, creaks, snap, splash, snore, leaves, water ambience.
- Run both inside the project's `audio/` folder: `python3 audio.py ../voice.mp3 .`, then `python3 mix.py ../voice.mp3 music.wav sfx.wav mix.wav` (`finish.sh` reads `audio/mix.wav`). It sets music to exactly 50 % of the voice's gated RMS (−6 dB) and SFX to 50 % with peaks capped at 0.7× the voice peak. It prints the measured levels: confirm music shows −6.0 dB vs voice and SFX is at or below that.
- Lock picture timing before making sound. Any timing change means re-running audio.py and mix.py.
- Continuous beds (wind, ambience) dominate the SFX stem's gated loudness. Keep them low so the hits carry; after the peak cap, SFX landing at −6 to −8 dB vs voice is right. Big hits (crashes, breakthroughs) are layered: steel tink + rock crack + low thump + debris rattle.

### 4.8 Render + finish
- Run `node render.mjs frames "0-<N-1>"` with Bash `run_in_background`, inside a retry loop (`for i in 1 2 3 4 5 6; do RESUME=1 node render.mjs ... && break; done`), because the environment can restart mid-render. Stay in the turn until it finishes with foreground waits of ≤10 minutes that print the frame count and any errors. `RESUME=1` continues after an interruption. Don't run heavy Python at the same time on small machines. After fixing one shot, delete or overwrite only that frame range and re-render it.
- Story edits made while the render runs only reach frames rendered after a restart, so re-render the edited shot's range afterwards.
- Run `bash finish.sh "Title"`. It applies one linear gain to about −14 LUFS plus a −1 dBFS peak limiter, encodes H.264 CRF 17 + AAC (`-preset veryfast`), runs freeze/black and loudness QA, and writes `out/qa_sheet.jpg`. Look at the sheet. Frozen spans ≥0.4 s or black frames need a fix: the usual cause is a camera that settles after an event (a prop lands, the shot holds), so add a creeping push to the end of that shot and re-render only its frames. Cap white flashes at ~0.9 so no frame is pure white.
- Deliver the MP4 with SendUserFile and a few lines on what each shot shows. Offer captions, tweaks, or the next idea from the list.

## Engine gotchas (kept in the kit; keep them if rebuilding)
- `#include <fog_vertex>` / `<fog_fragment>` go on their own lines.
- The final pass writes `linearToOutputTexel(...)` (render targets are linear HalfFloat).
- The normal+depth pass swaps materials per mesh: world → normal/depth, cards → alpha-tested mask (rgb 0, a = depth), blobs/clouds/sky hidden, water → flat normal. Fog and shadows are off during it.
- Ink goes on the near side of depth jumps only. Creases come from the normal dot. Card neighbours are skipped. Ink fades at ~140-520 m.
- DOF: 48 taps with a per-sample CoC test (no foreground bleed).
- The sun comes from az/el per light preset. The shadow camera is centred on each shot's action.
- The normal/depth target is FloatType RGBA, cleared to alpha 0, and every shader reads alpha ≤ 0 as far (1e5): clear values above 1 are not reliable.
- Near-side depth ink uses the second difference of 1/z along 4 directions (exactly 0 on any plane, so no false lines on grazing ground), with diagonal steps counted at √2 distance.
- Card pull is applied in both the colour pass and the normal/depth card pass, so DOF and ink agree with what is drawn.
- MeshToonMaterial ignores `flatShading`; use non-indexed polyhedra (Dodecahedron/Icosahedron detail 0) for faceted rocks.
- Triplanar toon: inject world position and normal at `worldpos_vertex`, replace `map_fragment`, and give the material its own `customProgramCacheKey`.

## Architecture (reference for editing the kit)
- `web/index.html` loads `main.js` (ES module) over a local HTTP server.
- `main.js`: renderer, toon materials, world, cards (1200x2000 CanvasTexture planes, origin at soles), passes colour (RT samples 4) → normal/depth → ink+grade (saturation, gain/lift, vignette, flash) → DOF to screen. It exposes `window.renderFrame(f)` and `window.ready`.
- `story.js` exports SHOTS, `shotAt`, `camAt`, `dofAt`, LIGHTS/`lightFor`, `worldAt`, `actorsAt(f, T)`, `fxAt(f, T)`. All are pure functions of the frame.
- `render.mjs`: Playwright → `renderFrame(f)` → `toDataURL('image/jpeg', .95)` → `frames/f0000.jpg`.
- `mix.py`: gated-RMS level lock (music and SFX at 50 % of the voice). `finish.sh`: linear loudness gain, limiter, encode, QA.
- Characters: the Pen API from mromidev/claude-animated-shorts `templates/bridge/toon.ts`, extended in the same module scope by the cast file.

---

# SKILL ORIGINAL: claude-animated-shorts

# Cartoon 3D story short (three.js + Remotion)

Proven example: **The Bridge That Fell for a Song**. It is an original 67 s short about the 1831 Broughton bridge and 74 soldiers marching in step.
- Code: `src/videos/bridge/` of your Remotion project (composition `BridgeShort`, 1080x1920, 30 fps).
- Script, voice and sound: `~/bridge-short/`.
- Style research: `references/`.

The style reference is a channel studied privately (`references/`: STYLE_BIBLE.md, reports/*.md, frames/).
- Those videos are private study material. Never upload, publish or send them.
- Never copy their scripts, characters or shots. Story, characters and voice are always OUR OWN.
- Never say the channel's name in a voice-over or description.

User rules that apply:
- No captions or labels in the video.
- English only.
- Voice: one steady tone.
- Jokes are researched real facts.
- Standard mix: music +3 dB, SFX -6 dB, voice unchanged.
- Final export 2K vertical: 1440x2560, `--scale 1.3333`.
- Opus max verifies every check.
- Save after every step.
- "Just do it" once the script is approved.

## Pipeline
1. **Topic and facts.** Pick one surprising true story with a physical cause and effect (it should be visual), plus a simple everyday analogy that explains it.
   - Write the facts with their source at the top of `SCRIPT.md`.
2. **Script** (~3.7 words/s, 55-70 s). Beat formula from the bridge short:
   - **Hook:** the absurd fact in one line ("Seventy-four soldiers knocked down an iron bridge just by walking across it.").
   - **Setting:** year, place, pride.
   - **Action:** escalation over three or four beats, each a little bigger.
   - **Snap:** the moment it breaks.
   - **Deadpan double line:** "Luckily ... / Unluckily ...".
   - **Explanation:** one physics idea through an everyday analogy (the kid on a swing).
   - **Consequence:** the rule it created.
   - **Callback twist:** the ending.
   - Never describe on screen what the viewer already sees.
3. **Voice.** Record or generate `voice.wav`, then make its word timings `words.json` (any speech-to-text with word times).
   - Put them in the project folder, e.g. `~/bridge-short/voice/`.
   - Hand-fix the word timings. Everything is timed to words.json.
4. **Shot plan** in `story.ts`: copy `templates/bridge/` (the final bridge source, same as `src/videos/bridge/` of your Remotion project) to `src/videos/<name>/`, register a new composition next to `BridgeShort` in `src/Root.tsx`, and rename it.
   - `SHOTS`: id, a/b frames, light day/golden, crowd mode, `tau` story time.
   - `XFADE`: crossfades at place/time changes.
   - `FLASH_AT`: a white flash after the hook.
   - `CAMS`: per-shot keys `{f, dur?, cut?, s: f => {pos, look, fov, roll}}`.
   - `KICKS`: impact flip-shakes.
   - `DOF`/`dofAt`: lens blur per beat.
   - `FOREGROUND`: near framing props.
   - Every visual reaction lands 0-3 frames before its word.
5. **World.**
   - `lights.ts`: LIGHTS plus the LOOK sky/river/cloud palette per light.
   - `shade.ts`: two-tone shading and `untone()`.
   - `build.ts`: town, houses and Kenney CC0 kit props, recoloured, with sight-line culling of anything blocking a camera.
   - `paint.ts`: HD Canvas2D painted textures with seams painted in as dark tints.
   - `world.ts`: sky shader, fog = horizon colour, river, per-frame posing, sun frozen per shot and camera-relative.
6. **Characters.**
   - `toon.ts`: the Canvas2D `Pen` drawing API, 600x1000 design canvas, soles at y = 975, bean heads, stick limbs, plum variable-width outline, face library.
   - `cast.ts`: camera-facing billboards, crowds, effect cards, blinks, walk cycles. All billboards go on the `NOINK` layer.
   - `cardshade.ts`: cards lit by the scene light, no emissive.
7. **Post** (`Bridge.tsx` + `ink.ts` + `lensblur.ts` + `grade.ts`).
   - Composer order: ink → lens pre → lens main → bloom → NEUTRAL tone map → grade (MSAA 4).
   - Every pixel parameter scales with output height / 1920.
8. **Sound** (`~/bridge-short/audio/`: timeline.py, sfx_gen.py, sfx.py, music.py, instruments.py, dsp.py, mix.py).
   - Event table → synthesized SFX plus an original score ducked under the voice.
   - Output: mix.wav (48 kHz, exact length), stems and cues.md.
   - Lock the picture's time constants before the sound is made. Any later timing change means re-running the sound.
9. **Look checks.** Run `node scripts/bridge-stills.mjs out/<name>/x_ 100,400 0.5`.
   - Set `BRIDGE_ENTRY=src/sandboxes/<n>/index.tsx` to render a sandbox copy.
   - Make side-by-sides against reference frames with `python scripts/sbs_sheet.py out.jpg --ours ... --ref ...`.
   - Send them to an Opus max verifier: 1-3 images, a ≤200-word verdict.
10. **Final render.**
    - `npx remotion render src/index.ts <Comp> out/<name>/video.mp4 --codec h264 --crf 16 --scale 1.3333`
    - Mux with `-map 0:v:0 -map 1:a:0` (Remotion writes a silent audio track).
    - QA: `python scripts/short_qa.py final.mp4 out/<name>/qa shots.json` checks every frame for black, white, frozen and pop frames, plus loudness and sheets.
    - Fix anything it finds, then run a deep Opus audit.
11. **Deliver.** SendUserFile the final mp4, update memory, write notes.

## Final look values (bridge after 4 verifier rounds; all in `templates/bridge/`)
Start every new short from `templates/bridge/` (copy to `src/videos/<name>/`): the look lives in these values, the
story lives in story.ts / build.ts layout / cast.ts roles. Opus max scores at the end: colour day 7, golden 5-6,
lighting 5-6, ink 6, textures 5-6, characters 7, depth 6-7, composition 6-7 (from 5/3/4/5/4/7/4/6 at the start).
- **Light presets** (`lights.ts`, sun camera-relative and frozen per shot):
  - day: az 75 / el 48, lit [1.58,1.5,1.33], shade [.30,.29,.36], lift [.05,.06,.11] (cool tint ONLY in shade), sky
    top #3e7fd2 → horizon #b8d0ec, fog = horizon, 25→300 m, uSat 1.08, ~15 % blue haze past 60 m.
  - golden: az 6 / el 7 (backlit low sun), key toward #FFB36B ×1.5, violet-blue shade (facade ≈ #444380), sky
    #ffc47c → #8a6f8a, peach fog only past 50 m, sun disc #ffcf9e + bloom, contact blobs + real card shadows.
- **Albedos** (`paint.ts`/`build.ts`): muted brick / cream / ochre / terracotta houses with plinth, cornice, sills,
  hoods, door step, chimney + pot, heights 7 / 8.4 / 9.8 m; warm grey/beige stone (saturation −35 % from "storybook");
  ochre wood deck C* ~51-58 with near-black seams; iron dark and desaturated. Never lemon, never lavender lit faces.
- **Ink** (`ink.ts`): #030303, 5.1 px near (6 m) → 3.3 px far (90 m) at 1080x1920, creases ×0.8 from 35°, depth
  jumps from 1 %, fog tint 0.3 only far; character cards on layer `NOINK` (they carry their own plum ink).
- **Lens** (`lensblur.ts` + `dofAt` in story.ts): cInf px at 1080: hook/river CU 15-16, close-ups 12-14, MS 10-12,
  park 7, golden sway 5, wides/aerials 0. Focus on the eyes of the front row.
- **Post** (`Bridge.tsx`): ink → lens pre → lens main → bloom (thr 1.0, int .5, 6 levels, radius .6) → NEUTRAL tone
  map → grade; MSAA 4. Film grain exists but is OFF (reference measures no grain; it quadruples the file size).
- **Characters** (`toon.ts`/`cast.ts`/`cardshade.ts`): bean heads h/w .94, plum ink #241726, eyes .26 W apart, no blush,
  14 expressions; adults 3.5 head-heights; walk bob 5 %, lean 1-7°, planted 0.8 m stride; blinks 0.5-2.3 s; tight
  contact shadow ~.5 under the feet + sun-direction tail by day; golden rim inner only (never an offset outer copy).
- **Camera** (`story.ts`): cuts on big jumps, smootherstep glides 18-40 f, every hold creeps (no static hold ≥4 f),
  flip kicks decaying over 7 f on impacts, 4-4.6° rolls, foreground props (`FOREGROUND`) in ~1/3 of shots.

## Look targets (measured from the reference; see references/reports/)
- **Ink:**
  - World lines: about 3 px at 1080 wide, near-black #0b0a0c, nearly constant with distance. Silhouettes, depth jumps and normal creases only. No line on wall/floor joins or the horizon. Character cards are never outlined by the world ink.
  - Character ink: dark plum #201628-#2f1d28, thicker on the bottom/shadow side.
- **Colour:**
  - Day median luma ~.57. Non-ink p5 ~.23 (no crushed blacks). Lit chroma C* p90 ~55. Day sky top ~#5e93d9, horizon 1.28x brighter.
  - Golden: peach horizon #ffd08a, violet-blue shadows (never brown), backlit sun.
- **Depth:**
  - DOF on about 1/3 of screen time (CU, MS, inserts), cInf 10-16 px at 1080, focus on the eyes. Establishing and wide shots stay sharp.
  - Fog = horizon colour, 25→420 m day, 20→360 m golden.
  - Bloom only on HDR emissives: threshold 1.0.
- **Camera:**
  - Hard cuts on big jumps. Everything else is a smootherstep glide of 18-40 f, peak ≤ 25 px/f at 720p.
  - Every hold pushes in 0.15-0.35 %/f, with 0.6 %/f on punch words.
  - Rolls of 4-5°.
  - No hand-held float. Impact shakes flip sign every frame and decay over 7 f.
- **Characters:**
  - About 3.5 head-heights tall, legs 1.4 HH.
  - Stick limbs about 0.055 of head width, ink-dot hands.
  - Bean head (h/w ~0.93, narrower chin, pointier crown).
  - No blush or eye highlights. Faces at brightness ~.93.
  - Walk bob 3-7 %, lean 1-7°, planted feet.
  - Blinks every 0.5-2.3 s, lasting 4 f.

## Look-match loop (how the bridge look was reached; reuse for any "make it look like X" pass)
1. **Measure the reference first.** One analysis agent per topic (motion, depth, textures, characters, lighting, outlines, grade), each writing numbers, not adjectives, to `references/reports/<topic>.md`. Shared brief: `compare/CONTEXT.md`. Reports are the source of truth for every agent after.
2. **Sandboxes, one owner per file.** Copy the project folder to `src/videos/<name>_{cam,world,mat,char,post}` with entries `src/sandboxes/<n>/index.tsx` (same `index.tsx` as main, only the import path changed). Ownership used for the bridge:
   - cam: story.ts (camera keys, cuts, kicks, DOF table, foreground props)
   - world: world.ts, lights.ts, shade.ts, grade.ts (sun, sky, fog, river, colour, grade)
   - mat: paint.ts, build.ts (painted textures, buildings, props)
   - char: toon.ts, cast.ts, cardshade.ts (character drawing, posing, card lighting, fx cards)
   - post: ink.ts, Bridge.tsx, lensblur.ts (outline pass, composer, lens blur, bloom)
   Agents only edit their own files in their own sandbox and render stills with `BRIDGE_ENTRY=src/sandboxes/<n>/index.tsx node scripts/bridge-stills.mjs out/<name>/<n>/x_ <frames> 0.5`.
3. **Merge** with `python scripts/bridge_merge.py [cam,world,mat,char,post]` (backs main up to `backup/bridge_<time>/`, copies owned files only). Then `npx tsc --noEmit` (filter to the project folder), then **re-sync every sandbox from main** (`cp -f src/videos/<name>/* src/videos/<name>_<n>/`) before the next round.
4. **Verify** with a fresh Opus max agent: side-by-side sheets (`python scripts/sbs_sheet.py out.jpg --ours a b --ref r1 r2`), score each area out of 10 (colour day / golden, lighting, ink, textures, characters, depth, composition) plus a numbered fix list with frame numbers. Map each fix to the file owner and resume that owner's agent with SendMessage (keeps its context). Bridge scores: round 1 5/3/4/5/4/7/4/6 → round 2 5/4/5/6/4/7/6/6 → round 3 see notes.
5. Contracts between owners live in exported constants (e.g. `NOINK` layer from ink.ts, `dofAt`/`FOREGROUND` from story.ts, `LIGHTS`/`LOOK` from lights.ts). Changing a contract means telling every owner that reads it.

## Gotchas
- Kenney nature-kit materials are teal: recolour them by material name in build.ts.
- Cameras end up inside houses: the sight-line culler in build.ts/world.ts removes anything blocking any camera ray.
- Ink in the same merged effect pass as DOF gets overwritten. Ink must be its own pass before the blur.
- Pixel-sized post parameters must scale with resolution: stills are at 0.5, the final at 1.333. R3F clamps dpr to ≥ 1, so `--scale 0.5` stills still render a 1080x1920 buffer.
- **Disk:** every Remotion bundle copies `public/` (~1.3 GB) into `the temp folder\remotion-webpack-bundle-*` and never deletes it. 60+ GB filled the system drive during the bridge passes and a render died with ENOSPC. Always render with `export TEMP='<big drive>/remotion-tmp' TMP='<big drive>/remotion-tmp'` (bridge-stills.mjs sets it itself) and ask the user before deleting old bundle folders in the shared the temp folder (auto mode blocks the sweep). <big drive>/remotion-tmp fills too (~500 folders / 110 GB after the 4 bridge rounds): when a project is finished and nothing is rendering, delete its `remotion-webpack-bundle-*` and `puppeteer_dev_chrome_profile-*` folders there.
- In JS strings write `"<big drive>/remotion-tmp"`, never `"<big drive>\remotion-tmp"` (`\r` becomes a carriage return).
- Remotion writes a silent audio track: mux with `-map 0:v:0 -map 1:a:0`.
- Sub-agents can't write report files in this harness: ask them for a ≤150-word final message and save it yourself to `out/<name>/<n>/REPORT.md`.
- Scratch `.ts` files inside `out/` break `tsc`: name them `.txt`.
- 2K renders run ~4 fps (≈10 min for 67 s). Stills at 0.5 take 3-8 s each after a ~40 s bundle.
- Film grain at 2.5 % made the 2K file 910 MB instead of ~250 MB and will turn to mush on YouTube; the reference has none. Keep it off.

## Readability traps the verifier caught (check every new prop/shot for these)
- **A swing drawn as one rope that disappears behind the kid reads as a HANGING.** Always two ropes to a wide plank
  with the kid between them (`swingKid` in toon.ts), and a frame whose legs clear the arc (A-frame at each end).
- Props or flotsam closer than ~2 m to a lens become huge cut-off blobs at the frame edge. When a camera moves, re-check
  every prop near its new position (the river cameras moved into the floating hats).
- A foreground bush cut by the frame edge reads as a "green ball"; a foreground prop must be recognisable or gone.
- Flags: red + yellow star/diamond reads as Vietnam's. Use a period-correct flag (St George's cross for England 1831).
- Painted signs lit by a side sun go grey-blue and unreadable: give the sign face an unlit material.
- Golden rim light done as an offset outer copy turns every hat into a picture frame: inner rim only, head/torso only.
- Balcony/railing rails at chest height through characters: put the lens ~2.5 m up so rails cross legs, not torsos.
- Flat holds read as freezes in QA (frozen span ≥12 f): every hold needs a creeping push-in.

---

# SKILL ORIGINAL: make-2d-video

# aiTrends 2D Studio: full video pipeline

Turn a voiceover (plus an optional script) into a finished MP4 where every frame is drawn by code (HTML canvas + Three.js), captured frame by frame in headless Chromium and encoded with ffmpeg. No image or video generation models are used, so characters stay consistent and text stays crisp. Invent the visuals fresh for every script; only the style is fixed.

**Bundled resources (this skill's base directory):**
- `kit/`: the tested studio (renderer, engine, tools, audio, the `examples/reaction-time` reference project). Install it into the workspace as `aitrends-2d-studio/` (see `references/install.md`).
- `references/install.md`: install, dependency table, doctor, self-test.
- `references/style-bible.md`: **the look** (characters, sets, light, camera, editing, graphics, 9:16 rules, don'ts). Read it fully before designing any shot.
- `references/story-breakdown.md`: writing `story/bible.md` and `story/shots.json`, plus pacing rules.
- `references/engine-api.md`: the scene API, config and shots.json schemas, and the three-kit reference.
- `references/sound-design.md`: the music plan, SFX library, ambience and levels.
- `references/render-qc.md`: preview and final render, speed guide, QC checklist, troubleshooting.

Stage commands from this plugin re-run one stage: `/new-video` (stages 1–4), `/video-styles` (5), `/render-video` (6 and 8), `/video-sound` (7). When running end to end, follow this file and load references as each stage needs them.

## Golden rules
1. **Keep the narration intact.** Never rewrite, add, drop or reorder spoken words. Every shot illustrates the words spoken while it is on screen.
2. **Same style, new pictures.** Invent sets, props, characters and actions from this script. Use `examples/reaction-time` only to learn the API.
3. **Show what is said, literally and specifically.** Abstract lines become visual metaphors, diagrams or close-up reactions.
4. **Draft, look, fix, then render.** Open contact sheets and stills yourself (read the PNGs) before any full render. Rendering is slow; looking is fast.
5. **Three user checkpoints:** setup choices, style choice from rendered samples, and the preview before the final render. If the user is away, take the defaults, state what was assumed, and continue.
6. **Files are the truth.** Each video lives in `aitrends-2d-studio/projects/<slug>/`, and `story/shots.json` owns all timing.

## Stage 1: Intake
Ask once (AskUserQuestion when available) for:
- **Voiceover file** (required).
- **Script:** timestamped (SRT/VTT/JSON/TXT with `[mm:ss]`), plain text (best: aligned to the audio, keeping exact wording), or none (transcribed).
- **Aspect:** 16:9 or 9:16.
- **Resolution:** 720p / 1080p (recommended) / 1440p / 2160p (4K). Mention that higher resolutions take much longer to render.
- **Captions:** default on for 9:16, off for 16:9.
- **Music:** generated, the user's own file, or none. **SFX:** on or off.
- **Title.**

Don't ask about style yet. Defaults: 16:9, 1080p, 24 fps, generated music + SFX, style `dusk`.

## Stage 2: Install or check the studio
Follow `references/install.md`: copy `kit/` to `aitrends-2d-studio/` in the workspace (skip if already present), install dependencies, run `python tools/verify_kit.py` and `python tools/doctor.py`, then run the example self-test. Report what was installed in one short list.

## Stage 3: Project + timeline
Run inside `aitrends-2d-studio/`. Use `python3` where `python` isn't Python 3.
```bash
python tools/new_project.py --name <slug> --voice "<voiceover path>" [--script "<script path>"] --aspect 16:9 --resolution 1080p --title "<title>" [--captions]
```
- Timestamped script: `python tools/parse_script.py --project projects/<slug> --script projects/<slug>/input/script.<ext>`
- Plain script: `python tools/transcribe.py --project projects/<slug> --script projects/<slug>/input/script.txt --lang en --model small`. The match must be ≥ 90%; if it's lower, tell the user the script and audio differ.
- No script: `python tools/transcribe.py --project projects/<slug> --lang en --model small` (`base` on slow machines, `large-v3` for hard audio).

Check `timeline/transcript.txt` (its end should be near the voiceover length), then run `python tools/beats.py --project projects/<slug>` to get the beat sheet and a draft `story/shots.json`.

## Stage 4: Story breakdown
Read `references/style-bible.md` and `references/story-breakdown.md`. Write `story/bible.md` (logline, cast with one signature colour each, locations with moods and key lights, motifs, music plan). Write `scenes/cast.js`. Rewrite `story/shots.json`: contiguous shots, cut on phrase boundaries 0.1–0.2 s early, a median of 2–3.5 s, an establishing shot per new location, and a concrete `scene` description for each. Send the user a 5–10 line plan summary and continue unless they object.

## Stage 5: Style choice
Build the 1–2 most-used sets plus 3 representative shots (a wide shot with ≥ 2 characters, a `bigFace` close-up, a data/blueprint or 3D shot). Then render:
```bash
node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> --styles dusk,midnight,golden,neon,storybook --at <t1>,<t2>,<t3> --scale 0.5
```
Open `build/styles/style_grid.png`, then show it with one line per style: **dusk** (mauve shadows, amber key, cream rim), **midnight** (cold navy, blue-white rims, sodium practicals), **golden** (warm orange and teal), **neon** (deep blacks, magenta and teal), **storybook** (soft pastel, low contrast). Save the pick in `config.json` → `style`. For tweaks, add a custom entry to `STYLES` in `engine/styles.js` (moods overrides plus the `grade` lift/gamma/gain, sat, contrast, vignette, grain, bloom) and re-render that row.

## Stage 6: Build every shot
Read `references/engine-api.md` and the example scenes in `examples/reaction-time/scenes/` once. Work in batches of 8–12 shots:
1. Write `scenes/sets/<location>.js` (reusable, anchored to `GY = E.H * V(0.8, 0.68)` and `CX = E.W/2`, with walls, floor and sky extended past the frame) and `scenes/shots/NN_<name>.js` with `registerShot('<id>', {...})` for the ids in shots.json.
2. Run `node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> --sheet --scale 0.5`. Open every `build/sheets/sheet_XX.png` and check it against the QC checklist in `references/render-qc.md`. Fix any MISSING shots, errors printed by the renderer, and composition problems.
3. Check motion with `--stills t1,t2,t3` or `--from A --to B --scale 0.5` (writes `build/preview.mp4`).

Non-negotiables from the style bible:
- Rim light on every lit figure.
- Colours from `mood()`/`S()`.
- A camera move in every shot.
- Atmosphere (dust, rain, glows).
- Moody value structure.
- Graphics synced to words with `wordTime()`.
- `V()` for every aspect-dependent value.
- No `Math.random`/`Date.now`.

## Stage 7: Sound
Read `references/sound-design.md`. Fill `story/shots.json` → `music` (contiguous mood sections) and add per-shot `sfx` cues and `amb` beds. Mixing happens after the final render (Stage 8), or run `python audio/mix.py --project projects/<slug>` now to audition `build/mix.wav`.

## Stage 8: Render and deliver
Read `references/render-qc.md`.
1. Preview: `node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> --from 0 --to 30 --scale 0.5`. Show the user the preview or a contact sheet before the long render.
2. Final: `node render.mjs --project projects/<slug>`. Run it in the background for long videos and poll the log. The log prints fps and ETA, so share the estimate.
3. Run `python audio/mix.py --project projects/<slug>`, which generates music and SFX, ducks the music under the voice, normalises to −14 LUFS and muxes `out/<title>.mp4`.
4. QC the final file: duration matches, audio is present, and a 1-frame-per-10-s contact sheet looks right. Deliver the MP4 (send the file), then give the location in plain words and one line on what to tweak next if needed.

## Defaults and judgement calls
- When unsure about a visual, choose the more literal, more cinematic option: a close-up for emotion, an insert for objects, a blueprint for mechanisms, 3D for big spaces.
- Keep about 15–25 shots per minute. Use 1–3 freeze-frames per video and about one 3D shot per minute.
- Keep each character's outfit colour fixed for the whole video, and keep screen direction consistent within a scene.
- Prefer re-rendering a fixed shot range over the whole video only when the user is comfortable splicing. Otherwise re-render everything.

# SKILL ORIGINAL: new-video

# /new-video: intake → install → timeline → story breakdown

This command covers stages 1–4 of the aiTrends 2D Studio pipeline. Shared references live in the sibling skill folder `../make-2d-video/references/` (relative to this skill's base directory), and the studio kit lives in `../make-2d-video/kit/`.

## 1. Intake
Ask once (AskUserQuestion when available), skipping anything the user already said:
- **Voiceover file** (required).
- **Script:** timestamped (SRT, VTT, JSON, or TXT with `[mm:ss]`), plain text, or none.
- **Aspect:** 16:9 or 9:16.
- **Resolution:** 720p / 1080p / 1440p / 2160p.
- **Captions:** default on for 9:16.
- **Music:** generated, own file, or none. **SFX:** on or off.
- **Title.**

Defaults: 16:9, 1080p, generated music + SFX.

## 2. Studio
If `aitrends-2d-studio/render.mjs` doesn't exist in the workspace, follow `../make-2d-video/references/install.md` in full. Otherwise run `python tools/doctor.py` inside it and fix anything reported.

## 3. Project and timestamps (inside `aitrends-2d-studio/`)
```bash
python tools/new_project.py --name <slug> --voice "<voiceover>" [--script "<script>"] --aspect <16:9|9:16> --resolution <res> --title "<title>" [--captions]
```
- **Timestamped script** → `python tools/parse_script.py --project projects/<slug> --script projects/<slug>/input/script.<ext>`. It accepts SRT, VTT, JSON (`[{start,end,text}]`, `{segments}`, `{words}`) and TXT lines like `[00:12.5] text`, `00:12 - 00:15 text` or `00:00:12,500 --> 00:00:15,000 text`. If the parser reports no timestamps, fall back to the plain-script route.
- **Plain script** → `python tools/transcribe.py --project projects/<slug> --script projects/<slug>/input/script.txt --lang <code> --model small`. It keeps the script's exact words with Whisper timing. Require ≥ 90% match; if lower, tell the user the script and audio differ.
- **No script** → `python tools/transcribe.py --project projects/<slug> --lang <code> --model small` (`base` for speed, `large-v3` for difficult audio). The first run downloads the model.

Open `timeline/transcript.txt` and confirm it reads correctly and ends near the voiceover length. Then run `python tools/beats.py --project projects/<slug>` (add `--force` to overwrite an existing shots.json).

## 4. Story breakdown
Read `../make-2d-video/references/style-bible.md` and `../make-2d-video/references/story-breakdown.md` fully, then:
1. Write `projects/<slug>/story/bible.md`: logline and tone, the cast (one saturated signature item each), locations (mood, key light, window view, props), motifs and the music plan.
2. Write `projects/<slug>/scenes/cast.js` with the outfits.
3. Rewrite `projects/<slug>/story/shots.json` following the pacing rules. Every shot needs a concrete `scene` description, plus transitions, bars, sfx and amb where they matter.
4. Send the user a 5–10 line summary: the cast, the locations, the shot count, and the highlights per act.

Finish by saying the next step is `/video-styles` (or continue with the full pipeline in the `make-2d-video` skill if the user asked for the whole video).

# SKILL ORIGINAL: render-video

# /render-video: build, QC, preview and final render

Work inside `aitrends-2d-studio/` on the active project (`projects/<slug>`; if several exist, ask which one). Shared references are in `../make-2d-video/references/` relative to this skill's base directory. Read `engine-api.md`, `style-bible.md` and `render-qc.md` before writing or fixing scenes.

## 1. Build or repair shots (batches of 8–12)
- List what's missing: `node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> --sheet --scale 0.4`. The console lists MISSING shots and shot errors, and `build/sheets/sheet_XX.png` shows one still per shot.
- Write `scenes/sets/*.js` and `scenes/shots/NN_name.js` (`registerShot(id, …)` for each shot id). Study `examples/reaction-time/scenes/` for working patterns.
- After each batch, re-run the sheet and **open every sheet PNG** against the QC checklist in `render-qc.md`. Fix and repeat until there are no MISSING or ERROR frames and every shot passes.
- To check motion, use `--stills t1,t2,t3` (several moments of one shot) or `--from A --to B --scale 0.5` (writes `build/preview.mp4`).

## 2. Preview checkpoint
Render `node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> --from 0 --to 30 --scale 0.5` and send the user the preview, or the contact sheets, before the long render. Mention the expected render time (speed guide in `render-qc.md`: frames = seconds × 24; ~2 frames/s at 720p on a small 2-core CPU, 1080p ~2× slower, 4K ~8× slower, desktops 3–5× faster).

## 3. Final render
- To change resolution or aspect, edit `projects/<slug>/config.json` (`resolution`, `aspect`). Switching aspect needs a fresh sheet review, because 9:16 framing differs.
- Run `node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> [--workers N] [--format jpeg]` and run it in the background for anything over a minute or two: `nohup node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> > build/render.log 2>&1 &`. Poll the log and report progress and ETA at sensible intervals.
- Use fewer workers if memory is tight (about 1–1.5 GB each at 1080p, more at 4K). With no GPU, add `--gl swiftshader` (already the Linux default).
- Output: `build/video_silent.mp4`. The end of the log lists any shot errors. Fix them and re-render if there are any.

## 4. Sound, mux and deliver
Make sure the music plan and SFX are set (see `/video-sound`), then run `python audio/mix.py --project projects/<slug>`, which writes `out/<title>.mp4`. QC: `ffprobe` duration ≈ `story/shots.json` duration, an audio stream is present, and a contact sheet of the final file (`ffmpeg -i out/<file>.mp4 -vf fps=1/10,scale=320:-1,tile=6x5 -frames:v 1 build/final_sheet.png`) looks right. Send the MP4 to the user and say in plain words where it was saved.

---

# SKILL ORIGINAL: video-sound

# /video-sound: music, SFX, ambience and the final mix

Work inside `aitrends-2d-studio/` on the active project. Read `../make-2d-video/references/sound-design.md` (relative to this skill's base directory) first.

## 1. Music plan
- Read `story/bible.md` and the shot list, then write `story/shots.json` → `"music"`: contiguous sections `{start, end, mood, intensity}` that change at story turns (title card, discovery, danger, climax, resolution, explanation), typically every 30–90 s.
- Moods: `mystery, tension, action, wonder, hopeful, sad, triumph, playful, calm, dark, epic`. Intensity 0–1 adds pulse, arpeggios and drums.
- Audition a mood: `python audio/music.py --mood tension --intensity 0.7 --dur 20 --out build/test_tension.wav`, and send the WAV if the user wants to listen.
- **User's own track:** copy it into `projects/<slug>/input/` and set `config.json` → `"music": {"enabled": true, "file": "input/<name>.mp3", "gainDb": -17}`. It loops or trims, and the end fades.
- **No music:** `"music": {"enabled": false}`.

## 2. SFX and ambience
- Per shot: `"sfx": [{"at": <s from shot start>, "name": "<effect>", "gain": -8}]` (optional `dur`, `seed`, `pitch`; `name` may be a path to the user's own WAV/MP3 inside the project). Per shot ambience: `"amb": {"name": "rain|wind|city|roomtone|murmur|hum", "gain": -20}`.
- List the library with `python audio/sfx.py --list`, or audition it with `python audio/sfx.py --demo build/sfx_demo`.
- Place cues on actions and graphics: `whoosh`/`swish` on whips and fast moves, `pop`/`blip` when graphics appear, `counter` under ticking numbers, `riser` then `impact`/`stinger` on reveals, `slowmo`/`freeze` for time effects, `hit`/`kick`/`footstep` on contact. Never stack more than ~2 cues at the same instant.

## 3. Mix and mux
Run `python audio/mix.py --project projects/<slug>`. It ducks the music −9 dB and ambience −5 dB under the voice, normalises to `config.loudness` (−14 LUFS default), writes `build/mix.wav`, and, when `build/video_silent.mp4` exists, writes `out/<title>.mp4`.
- Levels: `config.music.gainDb` (default −17 relative to the voice) and `config.sfx.gainDb` (default −6). Change them by 2–3 dB per iteration.
- QC: listen points or `ffmpeg -i out/<file>.mp4 -af ebur128=framelog=quiet -f null -` (integrated loudness about −14 LUFS) and check that the duration matches the video.

---

# SKILL ORIGINAL: video-2d

# Skill 2D — do nicho ao vídeo inteiro (ScriptForge + GrowKaz Stickman)

## Gatilho e escopo (regra do usuário, 2026-11-07)
- Esta skill fica **apenas armazenada** e só é usada quando o usuário mandar algo relacionado a **"use a skill 2d"** (variações: "use skill 2d", "skill 2d"). Mesmo padrão das outras skills dele. **Não aplicar por padrão** a outros vídeos e projetos.
- Quando acionada, ela faz o **vídeo inteiro do jeito que ele pedir**: o pedido dele (tema, duração, formato, idioma, estilo) manda sobre qualquer padrão das fontes. Defaults servem só para o que ele não definir.

## As duas fontes (integrais, dentro desta pasta)
1. **ScriptForge** — `sources/2D_Niche_Prompt_for_Claude.pdf` (+ `.txt` extraído) e versão formatada em `references/scriptforge-niche-roteiro.md`. Cuida de: nicho → ideias → roteiro de narração.
2. **GrowKaz Stickman Studio** — plugin original em `sources/GrowKaz_Stickman.plugin` e extraído intacto em `growkaz-plugin/`:
   - `growkaz-plugin/skills/make-2d-video/SKILL.md` — pipeline completo (ler integralmente ao acionar)
   - `growkaz-plugin/skills/make-2d-video/references/` — `style-bible.md` (a bíblia do visual, ler inteira antes de desenhar qualquer cena), `story-breakdown.md`, `engine-api.md`, `sound-design.md`, `render-qc.md`, `install.md`
   - `growkaz-plugin/skills/make-2d-video/kit/` — o estúdio: renderer (`render.mjs`), engine (core/rig2d/fx2d/ui2d/styles/three-kit/player), tools (new_project, transcribe, parse_script, beats, doctor, verify_kit), áudio (music/sfx/mix/dsp) e o projeto-exemplo `examples/reaction-time`
   - Subcomandos do plugin (etapas isoladas, dentro de `growkaz-plugin/skills/`): `new-video` (etapas 1–4), `video-styles` (5), `render-video` (6 e 8), `video-sound` (7)

## O visual (GrowKaz, resumo — o detalhe obrigatório está no style-bible)
Stick figures pretos com **rim light** fina do lado da luz, em cenários vetoriais cinematográficos 2.5D de baixo contraste luminoso (sombras roxas/marinho, destaques âmbar, 1 fonte de luz forte por cena, poeira/chuva, profundidade com camadas borradas), closes gigantes de rosto (`bigFace`), momentos freeze-frame "tempo parado", números/gráficos animados sincronizados à fala, diagramas blueprint azul-marinho, e tomadas 3D low-poly em órbita (Three.js). Cada quadro é código — personagens consistentes e texto sempre nítido. Estilos prontos: **emerald** (padrão GrowKaz), **ember**, **arctic**, **sakura**, **retro** (+ customizados via `engine/styles.js`).

## Fluxo completo (quando ele pede o vídeo inteiro)

### Fase A — Nicho, ideia e roteiro (ScriptForge)
- Se ele **já trouxer tema/roteiro/narração prontos**, pular esta fase e ir direto à Fase C/D com o material dele.
- Se ele quiser escolher: Passo 1 (menu de 5 nichos × 5 subnichos, ou subnicho próprio) → Passo 2 (exatamente 10 ideias, título <60 caracteres + hook de 1 linha) → Passo 3 (roteiro 450–600 palavras, 3–4 min, no estilo de narração do nicho; estrutura HOOK 0:00–0:15 / SETUP 0:15–0:40 / CORPO 0:40–3:00 com 3–5 beats escalando / PAYOFF 3:00–3:30 / OUTRO 3:30–4:00; formato com timestamps, [NARRATION] e [VISUAL]; fechar com 3 títulos alternativos, texto de thumbnail de 2–4 palavras, 5 tags, contagem de palavras e duração estimada).
- Regras ScriptForge: fatos precisos, nunca inventar estatística/citação/estudo; escrever para o ouvido; psicologia/manipulação sempre como consciência/autoproteção.
- Duração/formato diferentes do padrão 3–4 min (ex.: Shorts de 60s) seguem o pedido dele, mantendo a estrutura proporcional.

### Fase B — Narração (áudio)
- O pipeline GrowKaz parte de um **arquivo de narração (voiceover)**. Se ele enviar o áudio, usar como está: **nunca reescrever, adicionar, cortar ou reordenar palavra falada** (regra de ouro 1 do GrowKaz).
- Se só houver roteiro, gerar a narração com o TTS disponível no ambiente (texto falado limpo, sem pronunciar pontuação; testar trecho curto antes da narração completa) ou pedir o áudio a ele se nenhuma voz servir. Voz clonada só com autorização registrada, como nas outras produções dele.

### Fase C — Instalação do estúdio (uma vez por máquina/workspace)
Seguir `growkaz-plugin/skills/make-2d-video/references/install.md`:
- Copiar `growkaz-plugin/skills/make-2d-video/kit/` para `growkaz-stickman-studio/` no workspace do projeto (nesta VM: `~/workspace/growkaz-stickman-studio/`; no PC dele: dentro de `E:\muse ia\`). Nunca sobrescrever `projects/` existente.
- `python tools/verify_kit.py` tem que dar **29/29**. Depois instalar: ffmpeg, pacotes Node (`npm install`: renderer, Three.js, fontes), Chromium headless (`npx playwright install chromium`) e Python `numpy scipy faster-whisper`; rodar `python tools/doctor.py` até tudo OK e o autoteste do exemplo (`node render.mjs --project examples/reaction-time --sheet --scale 0.3` → 14 quadros, sem erros).
- Estado verificado nesta VM em 2026-11-07: Node 24, Python 3.12, ffmpeg 8.1.2, numpy/scipy presentes; **faltam** faster-whisper, `node_modules` e o Chromium do Playwright (instalam na primeira produção, ~1 GB no total).

### Fase D — Projeto e timeline
- `python tools/new_project.py --name <slug> --voice "<narração>" [--script "<roteiro>"] --aspect <16:9|9:16> --resolution <720p|1080p|1440p|2160p> --title "<título>" [--captions]`
- Roteiro com timestamps → `parse_script.py`; roteiro puro → `transcribe.py --script` (exige match ≥90%); sem roteiro → `transcribe.py` (Whisper; modelo baixa na 1ª vez). Conferir `timeline/transcript.txt` e rodar `beats.py` (gera o rascunho de `story/shots.json`).
- Defaults GrowKaz: 1080p, 24 fps, música gerada + SFX, estilo emerald, legendas queimadas ligadas no 9:16 e desligadas no 16:9 — tudo cede ao pedido dele (o padrão dele em Shorts é 9:16 1080x1920).

### Fase E — Breakdown da história
Ler `style-bible.md` + `story-breakdown.md` inteiros. Escrever `story/bible.md` (logline/tom, elenco com **uma cor-assinatura saturada por personagem**, locações com mood/luz/props, motivos, plano de música), `scenes/cast.js` e reescrever `story/shots.json`: tomadas contíguas, corte 0,1–0,2s antes da frase, mediana de 2–3,5s, **15–25 tomadas/min**, shot de abertura para cada locação nova, descrição concreta de cena por tomada. Resumo de 5–10 linhas para ele e seguir (autonomia dele: não travar esperando aprovação do que já foi pedido).

### Fase F — Estilo
Montar os 1–2 cenários mais usados + 3 tomadas representativas (plano aberto com ≥2 personagens, close `bigFace`, tomada de dados/blueprint ou 3D) e renderizar a grade:
`node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> --styles emerald,ember,arctic,sakura,retro --at <t1>,<t2>,<t3> --scale 0.5`
Mostrar a grade (`build/styles/style_grid.png`) com 1 linha por estilo e gravar a escolha em `config.json`. Se ele não estiver presente/respondendo, usar o padrão (emerald ou o que combina com o tema) declarando o assumido e continuar.

### Fase G — Construção das cenas (lotes de 8–12 tomadas)
Ler `engine-api.md` e os exemplos em `examples/reaction-time/scenes/`. Para cada lote: escrever `scenes/sets/<locação>.js` e `scenes/shots/NN_<nome>.js` (`registerShot`), rodar `node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> --sheet --scale 0.5`, **abrir todas as folhas de contato** e corrigir contra o checklist de QC (`render-qc.md`) — nada de render completo com tomada MISSING/ERROR. Checar movimento com `--stills` ou preview parcial.
Innegociáveis do style-bible: rim light em toda figura iluminada; cores só de `mood()`/`S()` (nada hard-coded); **câmera se move em toda tomada**; atmosfera (poeira/chuva/brilhos); estrutura de valores moody (60–75% tons escuros, 1–3 acentos brilhantes); gráficos sincronizados à palavra com `wordTime()`; `V()` em todo valor dependente do formato; **proibido `Math.random`/`Date.now`** (usar `rng(seed)` e `t`); figura sempre no chão com sombra de contato; no máximo ~4 personagens em foco; no 9:16, rostos/textos na área segura central e legendas a ~79% da altura.

### Fase H — Som
Ler `sound-design.md`. Preencher `music` em `shots.json` (seções contíguas por mood — mystery, tension, action, wonder, hopeful, sad, triumph, playful, calm, dark, epic — trocando nas viradas da história, a cada 30–90s) e os cues de SFX/ambiência por tomada (biblioteca de 36 efeitos em `audio/sfx.py`; convenções: whoosh em movimento rápido, pop/blip quando gráfico entra, riser→impact/stinger na revelação, freeze no tempo parado; no máx. ~2 cues no mesmo instante). Trilha própria dele entra via `config.music.file`; sem música, `enabled: false`.

### Fase I — Render, mix e entrega
Ler `render-qc.md`.
1. Preview: `node render.mjs --project projects/<slug> --from 0 --to 30 --scale 0.5` → mostrar preview/folha de contato antes do render longo, com a estimativa de tempo (guia: ~2 fps em 720p numa CPU fraca de 2 núcleos; 1080p ≈2×, 4K ≈8×; desktop moderno 3–5× mais rápido).
2. Render final: `node render.mjs --project projects/<slug>` (em segundo plano se for longo, acompanhando o log com fps/ETA). Sai `build/video_silent.mp4`, sem erros de tomada.
3. Mix: `python audio/mix.py --project projects/<slug>` — gera música/SFX, ducking (−9 dB música, −5 dB ambiência sob a voz), normaliza a **−14 LUFS** e muxa `out/<título>.mp4`.
4. **QA obrigatório antes de declarar pronto** (padrão dele): ffprobe (duração ≈ shots.json, áudio presente) + folha de contato do arquivo final (1 quadro a cada 10s) inspecionada + checklist de QC inteiro. Entregar o MP4 anexado, com specs e ressalvas honestas — só o que foi realmente testado.

## Regras permanentes dele que valem nesta skill
- **Caminho mais rápido:** render frame a frame é pesado — preferir o PC dele (SSH/Tailscale, pasta `E:\muse ia`) quando ele estiver acessível e for mais rápido; **avisar sempre** quando estiver usando o PC dele ou quando não conseguir conectar.
- **Autonomia:** executar o que ele pediu sem pedir aprovação a cada etapa; falar com resultado ou problema real. Os "checkpoints" do GrowKaz (setup, estilo, preview) viram entregas mostradas a ele, não bloqueios — sem resposta, valem os defaults declarados.
- Conversa em **pt-BR**; o idioma do roteiro/narração segue o pedido dele para cada vídeo.
- Correção localizada: corrigir só a tomada/trecho com problema e re-renderizar o menor intervalo possível, sem recomeçar o vídeo inteiro.

# SKILL ORIGINAL: fabrica-videos

# Fábrica de Vídeos — fluxo padrão do canal

Um tema entra, um vídeo pronto pra postar sai. Não reinventar o formato a cada vídeo: este fluxo É o formato. Desvios só por ordem explícita do ENZO.

## Etapas

1. **Tema e referência** — o ENZO dá o tema (ou aprova sugestão). Se ele mandar referência visual (vídeo, quadro, folha de modelo), a referência manda: adotar verbatim e mostrar no próximo output.
2. **Roteiro de retenção** — curto, episódio independente, segunda pessoa, 40–70 s de narração (~110–175 palavras). Usar um dos DOIS MODELOS do vídeo-fonte:
   - **Modelo história**: uma pergunta muito impactante que martela na mente do espectador e **só é respondida no final**.
   - **Modelo por etapas**: premissa forte + etapas em sequência, cada uma puxando a próxima, pra viciar até o fim.
   Sem ideia? Escolher o modelo e pedir ideias de histórias ("E se...?") antes de escrever. Ação concreta, sem final filosófico/moral em roteiro de luta. Números por extenso, anos em dígitos, só reticências e pontos como pausa. Marcar os registros de voz no roteiro: [CONVERSA] abertura/fecho, [NARRADOR] desenvolvimento, [SUSSURRO] batida de perigo/suspense.
3. **Narração** — voz will clonada, VoiceStudio no MacBook (perfil `afe1dbfa`, OmniVoice), gerada em blocos por registro. Sussurro: 3 tomadas, entra a mais baixa, tratamento −5,5 dB + EQ próximo (método aprovado no teste D. B. Cooper). Alinhamento palavra por palavra pras legendas. **Pilotos e conceitos novos: montar primeiro com voz de MARCAÇÃO local (Piper) pra aprovar o visual; a voz will final só é gerada depois do conceito aprovado** (economia de geração no Mac — é a ordem do vídeo-fonte: marcação → aprova a cena → voz profissional → sincroniza).
4. **Cenas (aiTrends 2D Studio)** — vertical 1080×1920, estilo golden (luz dourada, poeira, vinheta). Maioria 2D com fatia generosa de 2.5D (camadas com parallax + push-in). Herói = família visual do Caio. Regras de cena do ENZO:
   - **Painel do sistema e lutas = visão do herói (POV)**, não do narrador: painel flutua na altura dos olhos dele; luta em primeira pessoa com o slime/monstro vindo pra câmera.
   - **Braço em POV é cadeia ancorada**: ombro fixo fora de quadro, dois ossos com cotovelo (IK), sem teleporte de mão — interpolação contínua, objeto pego sobe grudado na mão.
   - **Gosma/sangue de verdade**: corpo gelatinoso com wobble e squash de impacto; estouro com fios, gotas, poça persistente. Quem se suja fica sujo: manchas desenhadas na roupa e no corpo, persistentes até o fim do vídeo (nada de bolinha simbólica). Em luta com corte, os dois lados sangram e a marca continua.
   - Nada de elemento aleatório fora do roteiro.
5. **Legendas** — palavra por palavra, maiúsculas, fonte pesada sem serifa, branco com contorno preto, palavras-chave em amarelo.
6. **QC antes de entregar** — ffprobe (duração/resolução) + quadros extraídos do MP4 final conferidos nos momentos críticos (painel, luta, sujeira, POV). Nunca entregar sem conferir os quadros.
7. **Render** — no MacBook (caffeinate ligado, não atrapalhar outras produções), master 1080×1920 + cópia leve 720×1280 pra tocar no chat.
8. **Pacote de publicação** — entregar junto: título pronto pra colar + hashtags. (Postagem no TikTok/YouTube é manual pelo ENZO ou via navegador com login dele — não há conector nativo de TikTok.)

## Formatos derivados
- **Documentário dossiê** (Guerra Fria, JFK, PGM): mesmo fluxo, cenas estilo dossiê (documentos, mapas, foto cards de arquivo com licença conferida), duração livre conforme o pedido.
- **Série em partes** (isekai Leo & Aria): manter personagens e ganchos de continuação entre episódios.
- Formato novo que se repetir vira **skill própria** (uma por formato), como no vídeo-fonte.

## REGRA DE OURO (do vídeo-fonte)
**Toda correção do ENZO — e todo acerto confirmado — é anotada nesta skill (ou na skill do formato) na mesma hora.** A skill é o manual que impede o erro de se repetir: braço em POV que descola, bolinha no lugar de mancha, painel fora da visão dele — corrigiu uma vez, entra na regra e nunca mais volta. Sem essa anotação, a entrega não está completa.

# SKILL ORIGINAL: muse-cartoon-video-maker

# Muse Cartoon Video Maker — Skill

## Missão
Atue como diretor de animação, roteirista, artista técnico 3D e engenheiro de pipeline. Transforme uma ideia e uma imagem de referência em um vídeo vertical curto, com cenas animadas, narração, efeitos sonoros, legendas e exportação MP4. Trabalhe de forma executável: crie arquivos reais, rode os comandos disponíveis, examine os resultados e corrija erros.

Não afirme que renderizou ou exportou algo sem verificar o arquivo final.

## Estilo visual obrigatório: referência enviada pelo usuário
Use a imagem de referência fornecida na conversa como guia de estilo, não como conteúdo a ser copiado literalmente.

Características:
- Animação 3D cartoon estilizada, limpa e colorida.
- Personagens humanoides simples, com cabeça grande e corpo compacto, silhueta legível e mãos/pés simplificados.
- Rosto minimalista ou sem detalhes faciais excessivos, conforme a referência.
- Materiais lisos, cores saturadas e contrastes fortes.
- Cenários escolares/urbanos cotidianos com formas geométricas simples e objetos grandes bem definidos.
- Iluminação clara, sombras suaves, aparência de animação familiar e acabamento de vídeo curto para redes sociais.
- Câmera vertical, enquadramentos próximos do personagem e movimentos suaves.
- Legendas grandes, em caixa alta, com contorno escuro e destaque de palavras em amarelo/branco.
- Elemento gráfico de contexto no canto superior (por exemplo, contador, dia, altura ou progresso), apenas quando fizer sentido para a história.

Não usar: terror VHS, realismo fotográfico, estética sombria, ruído/granulação pesada, personagens com aparência humana realista, texto ilegível ou excesso de detalhes.

## Regras de consistência
1. Crie uma ficha visual para cada personagem antes de animar.
2. Registre proporções, cores, roupas, cabelo, acessórios e materiais.
3. Reutilize o mesmo modelo 3D e materiais em todas as cenas.
4. Não mude escala, roupa, rosto ou paleta sem justificativa narrativa.
5. Mantenha direção de luz, estilo de cenário e linguagem de câmera consistentes.
6. Se a imagem de referência estiver disponível como arquivo, inspecione-a e use-a como referência de composição, paleta e acabamento. Não invente detalhes que não aparecem nela.

## Fluxo de trabalho
### Fase 1 — Planejamento
- Defina público, premissa, gancho dos primeiros 2 segundos e duração (normalmente 45–60 s).
- Escreva uma história com começo imediato, progressão visual clara, surpresa final e pergunta/CTA opcional.
- Divida em cenas de 3–8 segundos.
- Para cada cena, descreva: ação, cenário, enquadramento, movimento de câmera, expressão/pose, áudio e texto na tela.
- Entregue um storyboard textual e uma lista de assets antes de gerar.

### Fase 2 — Preparação técnica
- Verifique as ferramentas realmente disponíveis: Blender, Python, FFmpeg e gerador de voz, se houver.
- No Windows, descubra caminhos reais de executáveis; não presuma diretórios.
- Use Blender em modo background para criar/renderizar cenas por Python quando disponível.
- Use FFmpeg para montar vídeo, áudio, legendas e exportar MP4.
- Se uma ferramenta não estiver instalada, informe e ofereça alternativa. Não invente APIs, comandos ou capacidades.

### Fase 3 — Construção 3D
- Prefira modelos low-poly/cartoon feitos com malhas simples quando não houver assets externos.
- Construa personagens com formas simples e materiais Principled BSDF.
- Crie cenário modular, câmera, iluminação e animação por keyframes.
- Use movimentos claros e econômicos: caminhada, olhar, apontar, reação, virar a cabeça, pegar/soltar objeto.
- Anime câmera e personagem com easing; evite movimentos robóticos e mudanças bruscas não intencionais.
- Para cenas complexas, divida em planos curtos e renderize cada plano separadamente.
- Salve arquivos .blend e scripts .py organizados.

### Fase 4 — Narração e som
- Narração em português brasileiro, com voz natural e ritmo envolvente.
- Se não houver TTS disponível, gere o roteiro de locução e deixe claro que o áudio ainda precisa ser produzido.
- Adicione música de fundo discreta e efeitos sonoros sincronizados.
- Não use música protegida sem autorização. Prefira áudio original ou licenciado.

### Fase 5 — Edição
- Monte os planos na ordem correta.
- Sincronize narração, música, efeitos e legendas.
- Legendas: português brasileiro, alto contraste, fonte pesada, contorno escuro, linhas curtas e posicionamento dentro da área segura.
- Destaque poucas palavras por vez; não cubra o rosto nem a ação principal.
- Exporte em MP4 H.264, 1080x1920, 30 fps (ou 24 fps se a renderização exigir), áudio AAC.
- Preserve proporção 9:16 sem esticar a imagem.

### Fase 6 — Controle de qualidade
Antes de entregar:
- Verifique se o MP4 existe e tem duração correta.
- Confira resolução, FPS, codec e presença de áudio.
- Assista/inspecione o vídeo renderizado, procurando frames pretos, flicker, objetos atravessando o cenário, personagens inconsistentes, cortes ruins e legendas cortadas.
- Corrija os problemas encontrados e renderize novamente.
- Informe arquivos gerados, caminhos, duração, resolução e limitações restantes.

## Organização sugerida
video_project/
  reference/
  scripts/
  assets/
  scenes/
  audio/
  subtitles/
  renders/
  exports/
  docs/
  README.md

## Regras de execução para agentes de código
- Comece verificando o ambiente e listando ferramentas disponíveis.
- Trabalhe em etapas pequenas e executáveis; não despeje um projeto gigante sem testar.
- Crie primeiro um protótipo de 5–8 segundos com um personagem e um cenário.
- Renderize um frame de teste; corrija enquadramento, materiais e iluminação.
- Só depois produza as demais cenas.
- Use scripts completos e salve-os no projeto; não peça ao usuário para copiar blocos enormes se puder criar os arquivos diretamente.
- Execute cada comando e leia o resultado. Corrija erros reais.
- Não substitua a entrega por prompts para outra IA, salvo se o usuário pedir.
- Não prometa viralização, monetização ou qualidade impossível com as ferramentas disponíveis.
- Se a plataforma de chat não puder executar código ou renderizar, explique que ela só pode fornecer roteiro/código e ofereça um pacote de arquivos executáveis para Claude Code, terminal ou Blender.

## Formato da resposta ao usuário
Seja direto e explique:
1. O que foi criado.
2. Quais arquivos foram realmente gerados.
3. Como executar/renderizar.
4. O que ainda depende do usuário ou de uma ferramenta externa.

Faça uma pergunta de cada vez quando faltar informação essencial.
