- **Beat 1 is a hook that lands in under 3 seconds**, carrying the payoff promise. Never spend
  beat 1 on setup.
- **Narration lines together are the script.** Write them as one continuous read, timed to the
  style's `voiceover.pace_words_per_sec` inside each beat's duration.
- **Camera and element motion are written per shot** from the style's vocabulary (its
  `camera_language` and `motion.signature_moves`). No two adjacent beats share a camera move.
- **Mark which shots bake an in-world headline** (per the style's `typography`) and which show
  the product.

Deliver the map as editable JSON (schema in `story-and-beats.md`): `project`, `topic`,
`brand_id`, `product`, `product_photo_url`, `style`, `aspect`, `language`, `arc`, `voice`,
`music`, and a `beats[]` array with per-shot `dur`, `shot_size`, `camera_move`, `scene`,
`element_motion`, `headline` (baked text or null), `product` (bool), and `narration`. Ask:
"Approve this beat map, or edit any field?" Proceed only on a yes.

## PHASE 4: KEYFRAMES (the look)

One keyframe per shot, in beat order. Every prompt = the style's `image_style_block` verbatim +
the shot's `scene` + the baked `headline` (only on headline shots, treated per the style's
`typography.headline_treatment`) + aspect. Pass the style's `image_negative_prompt`.

```
advibly_generate_image
  prompt: <image_style_block verbatim> + <scene> + <baked headline if any>
  brand_id: <brand id>
  project_id: <project id>
  model: <style.recommended_image_model>
  resolution: "2K"            # or quality: "high" if the model is gpt-image-2
  on_brand: false
  aspect_ratio: <9:16 or 16:9>
  negative_prompt: <style.image_negative_prompt>   # if the tool accepts it; else fold into prompt
  reference_image_urls: [<product photo URL>]      # only on product shots
```

- **Verify each keyframe is convincingly in-style before animating** (the style file's
  `failure_modes` list the exact tells to check: clay must show fingerprints, pixel must have no
  anti-aliasing, felt must show stray fibers, papercraft must show cast drop-shadows). Re-roll
  here; images are cheap next to clips.
- The style block stays verbatim across every keyframe; only scene and headline change. That is
  what makes the shots read as one film.
- Product shots: the photo reference plus the "re-rendered as [medium]" wording (or the real
  photo cut-out for mixed-media). Check the product kept its silhouette and label.
- Show the set, ask "keep or change?", re-roll only the misses.

## PHASE 5: MOTION (animate each keyframe)

Animate each approved keyframe with the style's `motion_prompt_dna` + the shot's `camera_move`
and `element_motion` + SFX-only audio direction.

```
advibly_generate_video
  prompt: <motion_prompt_dna> + <this shot's camera_move + element_motion> + <SFX-only, no spoken words>
  brand_id: <brand id>
  project_id: <project id>
  model: <style.recommended_video_model>
  aspect_ratio: <9:16 or 16:9>
  duration: <the shot's dur, recommend 4 to 6; Omni Flash supports 4 to 10>
  start_image_url: <that shot's keyframe URL>
  # end_image_url: <only on seedance-2.0, for an exact landing>
```

- **Omni Flash takes positive-only wording**: the style's `motion_prompt_dna` is already phrased
  this way. Convert any "no X" you add into a positive ("the texture stays exactly as printed").
- **Do not ask for 12fps.** The stop-motion cadence is added by the final-render effect in Phase 5.5,
  not the video model. Ask the model only for the style's element and camera motion.
- Audio direction in every prompt: the style's SFX character only, no voiceover, no spoken
  words, no music with lyrics.
- Show each clip: keep, re-edit (same keyframe, adjusted motion prompt), or re-roll. Shading /
  3D creep and texture-smoothing are the top failures; the style's `failure_modes` list the guard.

## PHASE 5.5: CHOOSE FINAL CADENCE (conditional)

If the style's `motion.cadence` is on-twos / ~12fps, set the final composition cadence to
`"on_twos"`. Do not preprocess the individual approved clips. Smooth-24fps styles use
`"smooth"` or omit the option.

## PHASE 6: VOICEOVER + MUSIC + FINAL MIX

Follow `pipeline-and-audio.md`. In short:

1. **Voiceover: one line PER SHOT, never one combined read.** Same voice throughout, via
   `advibly_generate_voiceover`. This is the sync rule: a single continuous read laid from t=0
   drifts once any line runs off its shot length, and a read shorter than the video leaves the
   payoff shot silent. Write the whole script, split it at the shot boundaries, and generate each
   line separately. Map the style's `voiceover` character to a voice from the table. Probe each
   line against its shot's duration; tighten and regenerate any overrun, never time-stretch.
2. **Music**, one instrumental bed from the style's `audio_recipe.music_prompt` via
   `advibly_generate_music` (`instrumental: true`). A second bed only if the arc clearly turns.
3. **Compose once.** Call `advibly_render_composition` with ordered clips as `scenes` (use
   `volume: 0.2` for clip SFX), one `voiceovers` entry per shot with `start_seconds` equal to its
   cumulative offset, the bed as `music`, the chosen aspect, the run's `project_id`, and
   `keep_scene_audio: true`. **Leave
   `music_volume` unset.** For an on-twos style also pass `frame_cadence: "on_twos"`; for a smooth
   style omit it or pass `"smooth"`. The renderer ducks the bed against the narration automatically (it dips
   to ~35% while a line plays and swells back between them), so a hand-set level is not needed and
   a low one (0.08) would duck into inaudibility. It returns `status: pending`,
   `generation_id`, and `edit_url`; let the chat widget poll it.
4. Mention the `edit_url` in final delivery so the user can fine-tune the ad in the Advibly video
   editor. On Twos appears under **Effects** and updates the preview in realtime.
5. **Set the project cover.** Call `advibly_update_project` with `{ project_id,
   cover_generation_id: <the final composition's generation id> }` so the project tile shows the
   finished ad.

## PHASE 7: CAPTIONS (optional)

Only after the VO is mixed in: upload the final with `advibly_upload_asset` (`source_url`), then
`advibly_add_subtitles` with a dynamic preset. Never caption the SFX-only cut. Add brand and
product names to `vocabulary`. Some styles already carry a caption band in their design; match it.

## PHASE 8: OPTIONAL PUBLISH

If the user wants to post it: `advibly_social_list_accounts`, then `advibly_social_create_post`
with the final video. Only offer after the user has seen the finished ad.

---

## Notes and rules

- **The style pick and the beat map are the two gates.** Pick the style, approve the story, then
  generate.
- **The style file is the single source of truth for the look, motion, typography, and audio.**
  Pull its fields; do not improvise the art direction.
- **The style file is NOT a source for the story.** Invent an original concept, cast, and script
  every time. Never lift a style file's example subject or characters (its `example_subject_only` line, or a creature named only to illustrate the look); those are technique illustrations. Same style, different brand should share nothing but
  the look.
- **One style, one image model, one video model per delivered ad.** Swap the whole set or nothing.
- **Faithful, not on-brand:** `on_brand: false` always, `brand_id` and `project_id` always, no
  logo watermark by default.
- **Stop-motion styles get `frame_cadence: "on_twos"` on the final composition; smooth styles do not.** The
  video model never holds 12fps on its own.
- **SFX-only clips.** VO and music generate separately and mix on top; never let a clip speak.
- **Self-contained prompts.** Generators have no memory of earlier calls; the style block travels
  verbatim in every image prompt.
- **On failure:** `content_rejected` means the policy blocked the prompt; rework wording, and move
  to `kling-v3` only if the trigger is a real person or third-party mark. `insufficient_credits`:
  `advibly_buy_credits` and share the checkout link. A stubborn keyframe: re-roll, and switch the
  image model only per the style file's guidance (usually to nano-banana-2 for texture). A stubborn
  clip: two Omni Flash retries, then that style's Seedance route if it needs an end frame.
- **No em dashes, minimal emoji** in any copy, label, or narration you draft.

## Reference files

- `references/style-catalog.md`: the ten-style menu, one-line signatures, recommend-from-brand
  logic, and the quick model / cadence / voice / text-treatment table. Read at style-pick.
- `references/styles/<style>.md`: the ten cartridges. Each holds the full look, motion,
  typography, and audio DNA plus paste-ready `image_style_block`, `image_negative_prompt`,
  `motion_prompt_dna`, `audio_recipe`, and `failure_modes`. Read the whole chosen file after the
  pick.
- `references/story-and-beats.md`: the narrative arc library, hook patterns, beat and shot
  cadence, the beat-map JSON schema, and anti-monotony rules. Read before writing any beat map.
- `references/pipeline-and-audio.md`: the asset workflow, the image and video model matrix, the
  final-render On Twos effect, `advibly_render_composition`, captions, and failure modes. Read before the
  keyframe, motion, or audio steps.

---

# SKILL ORIGINAL: explainer-video-cn

# 中文科普短视频（v3：一套流程，两种画风）

> **⚠️ 先读这四条**
> 1. **这是一个文件夹。** 安装时复制整个 `explainer-video/`。开工第一步：`bash core/scripts/check_env.sh realistic`（或 `handdrawn`）。缺东西就停下找用户要完整包。
> 2. **不要从零重写渲染器。** 拟真风格从 `styles/realistic/template/` 复制改；手绘风格用 dashi-animationstyle 管线 + `styles/handdrawn/`。画面品质大多来自这些已经调好的部件，重写一遍全会丢。
> 3. **先看参考帧**：`styles/*/reference/00_*联系表.jpg` 就是及格线。交付前按 `core/quality-rubric.md` 打分，任何一项低于 2 分都不交付。
> 4. **事实必须有出处**（README、官方文档、API 数据）。数字要注明截至哪天，不确定的内容不要写进稿子。

## 环境准备（一次性；云端沙盒重置后要重跑）
```bash
pip3 install numpy edge-tts faster-whisper          # faster-whisper 可选：真人录音字级对齐（首次运行会下载约 460MB 的 small 模型）
mkdir -p ~/.fonts && curl -L -o ~/.fonts/LXGWWenKai-Medium.ttf https://github.com/lxgw/LxgwWenKai/releases/latest/download/LXGWWenKai-Medium.ttf && fc-cache -f
# 还需要：ffmpeg、node 18+、Noto Sans SC、Noto Color Emoji
# 拟真风格：在工程目录 npm i remotion @remotion/bundler @remotion/renderer react react-dom
# 手绘风格：git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/chuspeeism/dashi-animationstyle /data/refs/dashi-animationstyle
bash core/scripts/check_env.sh realistic            # 必须看到 ✓
```

## 0. 先选画风（问用户，或者按下表推荐）

| | 手绘笔记 `styles/handdrawn/` | 拟真出镜 `styles/realistic/` |
|---|---|---|
| 样子 | 白纸、钢笔线、马克笔，边说边画 | 出镜主播（口型同步）+ 真实截图/界面 + 手绘圈注 + 镜头微晃 + 胶片颗粒 |
| 适合 | 抽象概念、方法论、「X 是什么」 | 热点项目、产品/工具介绍、带数据的新闻 |
| 画幅 | 1920×1080（有竖屏补丁） | **1080×1920 原生竖屏** |
| 引擎 | dashi-animationstyle（Canvas） | Remotion（React），模板在 `template/` |
| 指南 | `styles/handdrawn/GUIDE.md` | `styles/realistic/GUIDE.md` |

用户说「要像真人」「要拟真」「要竖屏/手机看」「抖音」→ 拟真；说「手绘」「白板」「B站横屏」→ 手绘。拿不准就各出一帧静帧给用户选。

## 1. 研究 + 事实核查
- 开源项目：用 GitHub API 取 stars/forks/创建时间/license/主页，读 README（中文 README 优先）。截图：GitHub 手机版页面、官方配图、产品界面。
- 记一份事实清单（数字、原话、出处 URL、日期），写稿只用清单里的东西。**凭记忆补的细节（年份、版本号）要么核实，要么删掉。**
- 涉及逆向、爬虫、安全这类话题时，稿子里加一句合规提醒。

## 2. 写稿 → `script.json`
```json
[{"scene":"hook","say":"半年时间，二十四万颗星。","show":"半年时间，24 万颗星","pause":0.35}, ...]
```
- `say` 是读出来的（数字写成汉字更自然：「二十四万」），`show` 是可选的显示稿。`scene` 是场景名，同一个场景的连续句子会被合成一个镜头。
- 结构（60–120 秒）：**钩子（数字/反差，3 秒内）→ 名字/定义 → 生活比喻 → 以前怎么做（痛点）→ 它做了什么 → 演示一次 → 结果/证据 → 范围/亮点 → 官方案例（硬数据）→ 怎么上手 → 提醒 → CTA（评论区提一个问题 + 关注）**。
- 口语短句：一句 ≤20 字，竖屏字幕一行 ≤13 字（会自动折成两行）。术语第一次出现马上配一句大白话或比喻。方法和比喻库见 `core/creative-method.md`。

## 3. 配音 → 逐字时间轴（两种风格通用，格式一致）
输出 `audio/voice.wav` 和 `public/timeline.json`：`{duration, fps, sentences[{scene,say,start,end,words[{t,s,e}]}], mouth[每帧 0..1]}`，其中 mouth 用来驱动主播口型。

配音按优先级选：
1. **真人录音（首选，最有真人感）**：手机录，每句之间停顿 0.5 秒。
   `python3 core/scripts/voice_to_timeline.py 录音.m4a script.json <项目>`
   装了 faster-whisper 就做字级对齐（实测匹配率约 95%，中位误差约 0.04 秒）；没装就靠静音切句 + 句内按字数均分。**换录音后画面和音效会自动重新对齐，不用改代码**，因为画面卡的是「某句里的某个词」。
2. **高质量 TTS**（MiniMax、豆包/火山、ElevenLabs）：需要用户的 key，或者用户自己生成好音频交给你，然后同样走 `voice_to_timeline.py`。
3. **兜底：edge-tts**：`python3 core/scripts/tts_timeline.py script.json <项目>`（`VOICE=zh-CN-YunxiNeural RATE=+8%`）。免费，自带字级时间，但用户普遍觉得「太 AI」。**交付时必须说明，并主动建议换真人录音。**
- 禁止使用 macOS `say`、espeak 这类机械合成音。

## 4. 画面（按所选风格的 GUIDE 做）
共同原则：
- **每个镜头只讲一个意思，并且有一个具体的视觉比喻或真实素材**；名词变成物件，动词变成动作，数字做成计数器或进度条。
- **画面跟着嘴走**：元素出现的时刻 = 关键词开始说的时刻（拟真用 `wf(句号,'词')`，手绘用 `M('词')`），提前 0.1–0.2 秒。
- 每个镜头至少有一个持续的小动作（漂浮、呼吸、转圈、计数、打字），否则像 PPT。
- 每段 2–4 个重音（印章 / 盖章 + 闷响），放在论点落点的词上。

## 5. 质检（必做，通常要改 2–3 轮）
- 取每个场景结束前的一帧作为静帧，拼成联系表，**用看图工具真的看一遍**，再和参考联系表并排对比。
- 按 `core/quality-rubric.md` 逐项打分并写下来，再改。重点查：压字幕、被裁切、文字折行难看（单字掉到下一行）、元素互相遮挡、小窗主播挡住内容、空等（说了一秒多屏幕上还没东西）。
- 成片后再用 `bash core/scripts/contact_sheet.sh 成片.mp4 /tmp/cs.png 4 8` 抽帧复查。

## 6. 配乐音效 + 混音（通用）
1. 写 `cues.json`，音效全部锚在「第 i 句的某个词」上（示例：`styles/realistic/template/cues.json`，格式写在 `audio_bed.py` 文件头）。
2. `python3 core/scripts/audio_bed.py public/timeline.json cues.json audio/` → `music.wav`（`pluck96` 现代拨弦 / `lofi84` 温柔 lofi）+ `sfx.wav`（场景切换自动加 whoosh）。
3. `bash core/scripts/mix.sh audio/voice.wav audio/music.wav audio/sfx.wav out/video.mp4 final/成片.mp4`：音乐会自动给人声让路，整体响度 -14 LUFS。
4. 响度看 ebur128 测出的 I 值（目标 -15~-14），不要看 mean_volume。

## 7. 交付
- 成片 + 一句话说明：用了什么配音、字体，以及数据截至哪天。**你听不到音频，请用户试听。**
- 附上**各平台文案**（模板见 `core/platform-copy.md`）：抖音 / 小红书 / B站（带章节时间戳）/ 视频号 / X。
- 发布：云端浏览器的 IP 通常在海外，登录国内平台发布会显示「IP属地：海外」，还可能触发风控，所以建议用户在本地发布。
- 提供下一步可选项：换真人录音（自动对齐）、换主播形象、做封面、改节奏。

## 文件地图
```
core/        creative-method.md · quality-rubric.md · platform-copy.md
core/scripts tts_timeline.py · voice_to_timeline.py · audio_bed.py · mix.sh · contact_sheet.sh · check_env.sh
styles/realistic/  GUIDE.md · template/（可直接渲染的完整工程 = REA 那期）· reference/（16 张参考帧 + 联系表）
styles/handdrawn/  GUIDE.md · assets/（widgets.js + 参考帧）· examples/ · references/api-cheatsheet.md · scripts/
examples/rea/      script.json + 平台文案（拟真风格完整案例）
```

---

# SKILL ORIGINAL: mma-animation

# MMA animation — script → styled animation → MP4

Recreates the visual system of the animations in
Annie's MMA episode b-roll (the original episode exports,
5120×2880/30fps) entirely locally: HTML/CSS/JS with a
deterministic seekable timeline, rendered frame-perfect via headless Chrome.

`<skill-dir>` below means the folder this `SKILL.md` lives in — wherever
your agent installed the skill (e.g. `~/.agents/skills/mma-animation`).

## Themes

Four visual systems share the same engine, components, big type scale, and
output contract. Annie picks by name (default: classic). Sample stills for
all themes: render `--still` frames of a sample scene per theme (see step 1).

| theme | file | look |
|---|---|---|
| `classic` | (none — base mma.css) | her original light look; pale blue-gray, tinted pills |
| `dark` | `assets/themes/theme-dark.css` | deep navy, glowing accents + neon waveform |
| `blueprint` | `assets/themes/theme-blueprint.css` | grid paper, line-drawn boxes, dashed borders, all-mono |
| `pop` | `assets/themes/theme-pop.css` | sticker style: chunky black outlines, hard offset shadows, flat color |
| `luxe` | `assets/themes/theme-luxe.css` | high-end "ethereal glass": OLED black, mesh-gradient orbs, hairline glass, Plus Jakarta Sans |
| `minimal` | `assets/themes/theme-minimal.css` | editorial Notion/Linear: warm off-white, exact pastels, 1px #EAEAEA, Instrument Serif headlines, black active step |
| `industrial` | `assets/themes/theme-industrial.css` | Swiss brutalist print: paper + carbon ink + aviation red only, zero radius, Archivo Black uppercase, [ bracketed ] kickers |

(luxe/minimal/industrial are adapted from github.com/leonxlnx/taste-skill —
soft-skill, minimalist-skill, brutalist-skill respectively. Their fonts are
bundled in assets/fonts: Plus Jakarta Sans, Instrument Serif, Archivo Black.)

To apply: copy the theme file into the animation folder and add
`<link rel="stylesheet" href="theme-<name>.css">` AFTER the mma.css link.
Classic = no theme link. Everything else in the workflow is identical.
If she doesn't specify a theme for a new episode, use classic; if she asks
"show me styles", render `--still` frames of a sample scene per theme.

## Workflow

1. **Read `references/style-guide.md` first** — tokens, color semantics,
   motion grammar, component recipes, script→scene mapping. Follow it
   exactly; the goal is indistinguishable-from-the-originals. Theme CSS
   only re-skins — never change sizes or layout per theme.

2. **Plan scenes from the script.** Annie's scripts use `[SECTION]` headers
   and `//` beat separators. One beat = one scene, each with: a slug id
   (becomes the clip filename), a 2–4 word mono kicker, one visual metaphor,
   a duration ≈ narration time + 1s. Show her the scene table (id, kicker,
   visual, duration) before building if the animation is long.

   **Every scene MUST carry `data-script="…"`** — the narration lines
   (verbatim, condensed to first…last line if long) that the beat covers.
   This is not optional: it is what makes the clip↔script manifest
   automatic, so Annie can pick b-roll clips by script line while editing.
   The renderer warns on scenes missing it.

3. **Scaffold.** Create the animation folder (default:
   `./animations/<slug>/` under the current project) and copy in the
   runtime so it's self-contained:
   `cp <skill-dir>/assets/{mma.css,engine.js,fonts.css,template.html} <dir>/ && cp -r <skill-dir>/assets/fonts <dir>/fonts`
   Rename template.html → index.html and author the scenes.
   Do NOT restyle components ad hoc — if a new component type is genuinely
   needed, add it to the copied mma.css following the token system (and
   consider back-porting it to the skill).

4. **Preview & iterate.** Open `index.html` in a browser (plain
   file:// URL — no server needed). The HUD scrub bar appears automatically
   (hidden in render mode). Scrub through every cue; screenshot key moments
   to self-check against the style guide before rendering.

5. **Render.**
   ```
   node <skill-dir>/scripts/render.mjs <dir>/index.html --clips            # 5K master + per-scene clips
   node ".../render.mjs" index.html --scale 2                                                  # fast draft (2560×1440)
   node ".../render.mjs" index.html --still 3.5                                                # single PNG frame
   ```
   5K renders take ~1s/frame — quote Annie an ETA for long timelines and
   render drafts at `--scale 2` while iterating. Masters are named after
   the folder (CamelCase), clips land in `clips/NN-<sceneid>.mp4` —
   matching her existing `mma/assets/<episode>/` convention.

6. **Deliver — the output contract is always the same folder shape:**
   ```
   <slug>/
     index.html + mma.css/engine.js/fonts…   (editable source)
     <Name>.mp4                              (5K master)
     clips/NN-<sceneid>.mp4                  (one clip per script beat)
     clips/manifest.md                       (clip ↔ time range ↔ script lines)
   ```
   Report master path, duration, resolution, and show the manifest table.
   If it's for a real episode, offer to move outputs into
   the project's assets folder for that episode.

## Cutting clips from EXISTING videos (no scene metadata)

For videos not generated by this skill — e.g. the original episode exports —
use the standalone cutter (only here are timecodes ever needed):
```
node <skill-dir>/scripts/cut.mjs <video.mp4> --at 0:10,0:21,1:07 [--names hook,vad,tools] [--out <dir>]
```
N cut points → N+1 clips (`NN.mp4` / `NN-slug.mp4`) + a `manifest.md`
with time ranges (script-lines column left blank to fill in). It refuses
to overwrite existing clips — point `--out` elsewhere instead.

## Engine contract (what the HTML can use)

- `<section class="scene" data-id="slug" data-dur="7">` — scenes run in
  document order, 0.35s fade through bare background between them.
- `data-at="1.2"` + `data-fx="rise|fade|pop|drop|slide-l|slide-r"` on any
  element — scene-relative entrance cue (default rise, 0.55s).
- `data-stagger="0.12"` on a container — auto-cues its children.
- `data-loop="pulse"` (+ `data-freq`) — deterministic pulse via `--ls`.
- `.wave` auto-fills bars; `.flow[data-step-times="a,b,c"]` steps the
  purple active highlight through its `.step` children.
- Everything is a pure function of `t`: `window.__mma.seek(t)` — never
  use CSS animations/transitions or Math.random for motion; the renderer
  seeks frame by frame and live playback must match the render exactly.

## Requirements

ffmpeg + system Google Chrome + node ≥18. First run:
`cd <skill-dir>/scripts && npm install` (puppeteer-core only).

## Parallel sessions (IMPORTANT)

Annie often runs several agent sessions rendering different episodes at
once. Full renders take a slot in a cooperative queue (`~/.mma-render-queue`,
max 2 concurrent) — a render printing `[queue] both render slots busy` is
waiting its turn, not stuck, and 5K renders slow to ~2–3 s/frame when two
run. **Never `pkill` `render.mjs` or headless Chrome** — puppeteer Chrome
processes are almost certainly a sibling session's active render, not a
leak. A killed render's slot self-reclaims after 5 min. Full context:
a `RENDER-COORDINATION.md` next to the animations if several renders share one machine. For long renders
under contention, prefer scene-aligned `--from/--to` chunks + ffmpeg
concat (`-c copy`) so an interruption only loses the current chunk.

---

# SKILL ORIGINAL: manim-composer

## Workflow

### Phase 1: Understand the Concept

1. **Research the topic** deeply before asking questions
   - Use web search to understand the core concepts
   - Identify the key insights that make this topic interesting
   - Find the "aha moment" - what makes this click for learners
   - Note common misconceptions to address

2. **Identify the narrative hook**
   - What question does this video answer?
   - Why should the viewer care?
   - What's the surprising or counterintuitive element?

### Phase 2: Clarify with User

Ask targeted questions (not all at once - adapt based on responses):

**Audience & Scope**
- What math/science background should I assume? (e.g., "knows calculus" or "high school algebra")
- Target video length? (short: 5-10min, medium: 15-20min, long: 30min+)
- Should this be self-contained or part of a series?

**Focus & Depth**
- Any specific aspects to emphasize or skip?
- Proof-heavy or intuition-focused?
- Real-world applications to include?

**Style Preferences**
- Color scheme preferences?
- Narration style? (casual, formal, playful)
- Any specific visual metaphors you have in mind?

### Phase 3: Create scenes.md

Output a comprehensive `scenes.md` file with this structure:

```markdown
# [Video Title]

## Overview
- **Topic**: [Core concept]
- **Hook**: [Opening question/mystery]
- **Target Audience**: [Prerequisites]
- **Estimated Length**: [X minutes]
- **Key Insight**: [The "aha moment"]

## Narrative Arc
[2-3 sentences describing the journey from confusion to understanding]

---

## Scene 1: [Scene Name]
**Duration**: ~X seconds
**Purpose**: [What this scene accomplishes]

### Visual Elements
- [List of mobjects needed]
- [Animations to use]
- [Camera movements]

### Content
[Detailed description of what happens, what's shown, what's explained]

### Narration Notes
[Key points to convey, tone, pacing notes]

### Technical Notes
- [Specific Manim classes/methods to use]
- [Any tricky implementations to note]

---

## Scene 2: [Scene Name]
...

---

## Transitions & Flow
[Notes on how scenes connect, recurring visual motifs]

## Color Palette
- Primary: [color] - used for [purpose]
- Secondary: [color] - used for [purpose]
- Accent: [color] - used for [purpose]
- Background: [color]

## Mathematical Content
[List of equations, formulas, or mathematical objects that need to be rendered]

## Implementation Order
[Suggested order for implementing scenes, noting dependencies]
```

## 3b1b Style Principles

Apply these principles when composing scenes:

### Visual Storytelling
- **Show, don't just tell** - Every concept needs a visual representation
- **Progressive revelation** - Build complexity gradually, don't show everything at once
- **Visual continuity** - Transform objects rather than replacing them when possible

### Pacing & Rhythm
- **Pause for insight** - Give viewers time to absorb key moments
- **Vary the pace** - Mix quick sequences with slower explanations
- **End scenes with resolution** - Each scene should feel complete

### Mathematical Beauty
- **Emphasize elegance** - Highlight when math is surprisingly simple or beautiful
- **Connect representations** - Show the same concept multiple ways (algebraic, geometric, intuitive)
- **Embrace abstraction gradually** - Start concrete, then generalize

### Engagement Techniques
- **Pose questions** - Make viewers curious before revealing answers
- **Acknowledge difficulty** - "This might seem confusing at first..."
- **Celebrate insight** - Make the "aha moment" feel earned

## References

- [references/narrative-patterns.md](references/narrative-patterns.md) - Common 3b1b narrative structures
- [references/visual-techniques.md](references/visual-techniques.md) - Effective visualization patterns
- [references/scene-examples.md](references/scene-examples.md) - Example scenes.md excerpts

## Templates

- [templates/scenes-template.md](templates/scenes-template.md) - Blank scenes.md template

---

# SKILL ORIGINAL: vibe-knowledge-film

# Vibe 知识短片

一种没有旁白的知识短片：观众读底部的一行字幕，同时看屏上把这句话**演出来**。

做这种片子的人是科普作者兼导演。色、字、辉光、片名卡都是皮；片子好不好，看的是观众能不能一眼认出屏上的东西、看见它在变、看完讲得出"因为……所以……"。

## 配图不是讲解

这个 skill 做过一条失败的《折叠》（讲蛋白质和 AlphaFold）：全片的主体是一串星点连成的折线。
字幕说"只换掉第 6 个，形状就变了，这就是镰刀型贫血"，折线换了个卷法、变成玫红色。
颜色、字体、节奏都像参考片，可观众从头到尾没见过蛋白质是个什么东西、错一个字母到底坏在哪。
那串星点换给任何一段文案都成立——它是配图。

重做以后，同一段内容是：镜头从血管里的红细胞钻进去，找到一串珠子；第 6 颗变了色；拉出来，细胞弯成了镰刀；
再钻进去，珠串卷成一团抓住氧分子；变色的那颗在表面成了黏点；一团粘一团连成长杆；长杆从里面把细胞顶弯。
两版的对照图是 `examples/fold_old_vs_new.jpg`，重做的成片在 `examples/fold_C/`。

**动手前先读 `references/visualization.md`。** 四条参考片逐句的"文案 → 屏上是什么 → 观众为什么懂"、六种画法、什么时候不画、
怎么给概念找形象、分镜表怎么写，都在那里。本文件只是流程。

交付物：一个工程目录（`storyboard.md` 分镜表 + `film.js` + 渲染出的 `renders/final.mp4`），加上事实表。

## 八条规矩

1. **先找形象，再写文案。** 每个概念先找到一样外行认得的东西来演它；句子里要有屏上看得见的东西和动作。画不出来的句子，改句子。
2. **屏上的主体叫得出日常名字，而且长得像它本身。** 一串有光泽的珠子、一个中间凹下去的红细胞、一条带刻度的数轴、一句古诗。
   点、线、圈、柱子、方框不算；一个圆圈加一个标签也不算。
3. **句子里的动词，屏上真的做。** "砍掉一半"就盖住一半，"粘住"就真的卡上去。数是算出来的；示意的地方写明"示意"。
4. **先例子，后道理。** 名字、公式、结论，排在观众看过例子之后。开头十秒让观众看见一件事或者自己试一下，然后才是片名。
5. **该画的画，该停的停。** 提问、命名、结论、引文、出处，不配新画面——参考片里大约每五句有一句是这样。
6. **一个世界、一把尺子、几样道具用到底。** 一个场景撑两句以上，每句只改一处；结尾回到开头的画面。
7. **没有人声，外观统一，画面现写。** 文案就是字幕，每句一眼读完；一条片一套色、字、画法，每种颜色全片一个意思。画面和代码是为这份文案现写的，不套模板，不搬别的片子里的东西。
8. **每个事实带出处。** 年份、人名、数字上屏；不加水印，不加署名。

## 改到自己满意为止

第一次渲出来的画面几乎都有毛病。这种片子是看着图改出来的，不是写完代码就完了的——`fold_C` 那 33 秒改了三轮才成片。

- **每做完一镜就截图看。** `--sheet` 看全局，`--stills` 看原尺寸，`--motion` 看转场。看的是图，不是代码。
- **看的时候当自己是第一次看的观众。** 遮住字幕和小字过三问：这是什么 / 它在干什么 / 我明白了什么。答得含糊就是没做好。
- **对着 `references/visualization.md` 第八节的表逐条查**：主体够不够大、认不认得出、发不发白、小字压没压在东西上、字幕区干不干净、转场说不说得清。
- **至少改两轮再给用户看。** 每一轮：把看到的毛病列出来 → 改 → 重截同样的时刻 → 确认改掉了、没改坏别的。还有毛病就接着改，不要带着自己都看得出的毛病交片。
- **拿参考片当尺子。** 把自己的静帧和 `examples/` 里的 look 图并排放，再对照 `references/visualization.md` 第八节的表。差一截，就还没做完。
- **交片时说实话。** 还不满意的地方、没做的东西、没完整看过的部分，直接列出来。

力气不多的时候（时间紧，或者想得不深），照下面做，质量也守得住：

1. **不跳步。** 每一步的过关标准逐条对，没过不往下走。尤其是第 2、3 步：没有形象和分镜表，后面改多少轮都救不回来。
2. **拿不准就查表。** 这一句该怎么演，去 `visualization.md` 第一节的四张表里找最像的一行，照它的画法做；该不该画，查第三节。
3. **画面现写，不套模板。** 文案不同，屏上的东西就不同，代码也不同。`examples/` 是看别人怎么把一份分镜表做出来的，不是零件库——别把那里的珠子、细胞、数轴搬进自己的片子，也别让自己的片子长成它的样子。能带走的只有做法：先截图再改，实心的东西画在底层，推拉用对数缩放。
4. **宁可少，不要糊。** 时间只够把三个概念做好，就只做三个；每一镜都认得出，比多讲两个概念重要。
5. **先做 30 秒。** 开头 30 秒过了三问、给用户看过，再铺全片。

## 流程

每一步后面是过关标准。没过就停在这一步改，不往下走。用户说"直接做"时，第 2、3 步的确认可以不等，但东西照样要写出来。

命令里的 `scripts/`、`engine/` 指本 skill 目录下的路径；工程目录建在用户自己的项目里。

### 0. 看清手里有什么

- **只有题目**：从第 1 步做起。
- **有文案**：照样过第 1、2 步。文案里画不出来的句子（里面没有东西、没有动作）要改；保留原文的意思、事实和说话的口气，改完给用户看改了哪些。
  句长和句数的标准见 `references/script.md`。
- **用户手里有一版做坏的**：先看那一版（截帧对着字幕看），重做同一段内容，交片时给一张新旧对照图。

### 1. 立题和事实表

写下：一句话论点；3–12 个概念的清单，每个概念用大白话写"要观众明白什么"和"外行卡在哪"。
再列事实表：每个年份、人名、数字的出处和把握程度。把握不足的去查，查不到就换例子。

过关：论点一口气说得完；每个概念你能不用术语讲给外行听。讲不出来的，先去把它弄懂——含糊的句子都是没弄懂的时候写出来的。

### 2. 找形象

这是决定片子好坏的一步，时间主要花在这里。照 `references/visualization.md` 第五节，对每个概念按顺序问：
它本身是个东西吗 / 是个过程吗 / 是个量吗 / 是观众脑子里的事吗 / 是条规律吗 / 都不是就上字。
找到以后再问：观众在生活里哪儿见过它；换一个尺度看是什么样；它坏了会怎样；怎么画才像它本身、从哪个认得的画面进场。
然后定全片的那一个形象、那一把尺子，和哪些东西会回场。

过关：
- 每个概念的形象叫得出日常名字，说得出它长什么样；
- 至少一半的概念找到了生活里的锚；
- 没有哪个形象换给别的文案也成立；
- 说得出全片哪一下最让人"哦——"。

这是给用户看方案的第一个时机。钩子给三个方向和一个推荐。

### 3. 文案和分镜表一起写

格式和自查在 `references/visualization.md` 第六节，例子在第七节。一句字幕一行：画法（六种之一，或"不画"）、屏上是什么、它在干什么、屏上的小字、观众明白了什么、声音。
字幕的量和句式按 `references/script.md`。**没有分镜表不建工程。**

过关：
- 遮住字幕一列，只读"屏上是什么，在干什么"，看得出在讲什么；
- 每一句里的名词，屏上找得到；
- "不画"的大约占五分之一——一个都没有，是把结论也画了；占了一半，是没找到形象；
- 每个场景撑了两句以上，每句只改一处；
- 每一镜过得了三问：这是什么（日常的词）/ 它在干什么（一个动词）/ 观众明白了什么。

这是给用户看方案的第二个时机：把分镜表给他看。

### 4. 选外观

外观跟着题材走，在分镜表之后定。

| 题目是 | 选 |
|---|---|
| 一个心理 / 行为机制，从动物或生活场景讲到"你该怎么办" | B 寓言 |
| 一个抽象概念或一个看不见的机制，拆成几个当场可演的小实验 | C 夜曲 |

只有这两套。它们的色、字、版式、常驻元素、片名卡、声音都在 `references/worlds.md`。拿不准选 C；B 最费工。
同时定下颜色的含义，全片不破例。

### 5. 建工程，先做最难的一镜

```bash
python scripts/new_film.py <工程目录> --world C      # 或 B
python engine/render.py <工程目录> --sheet 3 8 15 22  # 指定时刻拼一张；--sheet 16 是均匀 16 帧
python engine/render.py <工程目录> --stills 5.9 18.1  # 原尺寸截几帧
python engine/render.py <工程目录> --motion 8.2 10.6  # 这段时间里取 12 帧拼一张，看转场
python engine/render.py <工程目录> --serve            # 浏览器预览
```

`new_film.py` 建出来的 `film.js` 只是能跑起来的骨架，里面一行画面都没有：没有现成的钩子、没有现成的图表。每一镜都照分镜表现写。
先填 `SUBS`，再把分镜表里**最难画的那一镜**做出来，截静帧。引擎的用法在 `references/engine.md`。`examples/` 可以翻，看的是别人怎么把一张分镜表变成画面，不是去找能搬的零件。
字体缺了跑 `python engine/get_fonts.py`。

过关：拿这张静帧，遮住字幕和小字，问"这是什么、它在干什么"——答得出来；缩到手机横屏大小还分得清；纵向 80%–95% 只有字幕；
对着 `visualization.md` 第八节的表查过一遍。答不出来就重画，或者回第 2 步换形象。第一版几乎不会过，改两三轮是正常的。

### 6. 前 30 秒

30 秒里讲得完多少就讲多少，不要为了"讲完"往里塞。一个道理硬压进 30 秒，每句都只能点到为止，画面跟不上，看起来就抽象。
宁可只讲一件事，让它在屏上从头到尾发生一遍（参考片的开头 30 秒也只讲了一件事：鸟丢下自己的蛋去孵假蛋）。

景别要变。同一个固定画面不要撑过三四句：全景交代在哪，中景看它做什么，近景看关键的那一下；该单独给一个画面的地方（一条规则、一次对比）就换一张画，别在原画面上叠东西。

```bash
python engine/render.py <工程目录> --clip 0 30 head
```

渲之前先用 `--motion` 把每个转场前后各抽一张拼图看：镜头怎么进、东西怎么变、有没有哪一帧说不清在干什么。有就改，改完再渲。

过关：字幕出现后 0.3 秒内屏上有动作；"哦——"之前有铺垫、之后停得住；场景之间是长出来的或者推拉过去的，不是硬切。
这是给用户看画面的时机。

### 7. 铺全片

一个概念一个函数；常驻元素和字幕在 `draw` 的最外层统一画。
每做完两三个概念，`--sheet` 拼一张自查：有没有东西伸进字幕区，有没有两样东西同时抢着动，相邻两段是不是长得一样，该回场的东西回来了没有。
长片按章拆文件（见 `engine.md`）。

```bash
python engine/render.py <工程目录> --det              # 同一帧渲两次必须一样
python engine/render.py <工程目录> --jobs 4           # 全片，4 路并行
```

### 8. 声音

- **C**：`film.js` 里用 `fr.sfx(时刻, 名字)` 登记事件（每一下变化一声、推拉时的上滑音、落定时的低音、提问那一镜不出声），然后
  `python engine/render.py <工程> --events && python engine/audio.py <工程>`。
- **B**：用户给一首配乐，`python engine/audio.py <工程> --bgm 曲子.mp3 -8`。先量配乐的速度，把每段的开头对到小节线上，
  最大的那次揭晓对到配乐重新进入的那一拍。用户没给配乐就说明白：成片目前只有事件音，等配乐。

### 9. 终检

整片渲出来以后做三遍：

1. **静音、遮住字幕看一遍。** 每一段在讲什么，看得出来吗？
2. **只读字幕一遍。** 通顺吗？有没有哪句里的名词，屏上找不到？
3. **对照下面的门槛。**

| | 门槛 |
|---|---|
| 讲解 | 成片和分镜表对得上；抽五镜过三问都答得出；没有只是配图的画面；屏上的数前后对得上；尺子和道具回过场 |
| 画与不画 | 提问、命名、结论没有硬配图；也没有连着几句画面不变 |
| 结构 | 片名在 6–24 秒之间出现；结尾回到开头的画面；最后一屏是片名 + 文献 |
| 字幕 | 每句一行；B 停留约 3 秒、C 约 3–5 秒；一句最多染一处颜色 |
| 画面 | 字幕区没有别的东西；任何一刻只有一个主体在动；一镜最多一条小字；颜色含义全片一致 |
| 节奏 | 没有硬切；主体出完之后停住让人读；每 20–30 秒一段无字幕的纯画面 |
| 事实 | 屏上每个数字和事实表一致；每个概念有出处；示意的地方写明了 |
| 技术 | `--det` 通过；1920×1080，30fps；没有水印和署名 |

不合格的时间点列出来，改完只重渲那一段（`--clip`）确认，再渲全片。交片时把没做到的地方直说，包括成片有没有完整看过。

## 文件

| | |
|---|---|
| `references/visualization.md` | **讲解设计**：四条参考片逐句的文案 → 屏上是什么 → 观众为什么懂；六种画法；什么时候不画；怎么找形象、怎么画得像它本身；分镜表；《折叠》两版对照；看图改图的毛病表 |
| `references/visual-grammar.md` | 定了画法之后的速查：常见句型、版式、常驻元素、颜色、转场、动效手感 |
| `references/script.md` | 文案：量、骨架、句式、事实、标题 |
| `references/worlds.md` | 两套外观的规格：色、字、版式、片名卡、声音 |
| `references/analysis.md` | 四条参考片的结构、节奏、制作痕迹（外观和技术层面） |
| `references/prompts.md` | 逆向出的提示词：完整 brief、分步提示词、返工话术 |
| `references/engine.md` | 引擎 API、命令、写法、坑 |
| `engine/` | `vk.js` `vk-kit.js` `render.py` `audio.py` `get_fonts.py` |
| `scripts/new_film.py` | 建工程：`index.html` + 一份没有画面的 `film.js` 骨架 |
| `examples/` | 看别人怎么做过一次，不是零件库。`fold_C`（夜曲，《折叠》开头 33 秒）和 `pigeon_B`（寓言，《鸽子的迷信》开头 33 秒）：自己题目的样片，各带分镜表；`fold_old_vs_new.jpg`：同一段内容的两版对照；两段对照参考片做的复刻；一张货架图；`look_*.jpg` 是各自的拼图 |

---

# SKILL ORIGINAL: huashu-motion

# huashu-motion

You are the director, designer and animator. The film is code: one composition (`index.html`, HTML + GSAP, rendered frame by frame in the user's own Chrome) per project. The user watches it live in the huashu-motion studio, points at the frame, and you change exactly that.

Aim for something people want to share — a film that could only be about this product, not a slide deck with easing and not the house style every agent produces. Working code is the starting line; the finished frames are the bar.

## The tool

```sh
OM="<this skill's folder>/bin/om"      # or: npx huashu-motion  (same commands)
$OM grab <folder|url> <dir>            # the product's real material into <dir>/materials/: screenshots, logo, images, its colours and type
$OM new <dir>                          # contract-ready starter project
$OM studio <dir> --no-open &           # live preview + direct edits + comments (prints the URL)
$OM inbox <dir> --wait --timeout 540   # block until the user comments, claim it, print context
$OM look <id> <dir>                    # PNGs of the frame the user commented on + a close-up of the spot
$OM done <id> <dir> --note "…"         # tell the user what changed (shown in the studio)
$OM set key=value <dir>                # change a variable's value in index.html
$OM sound <dir>                        # music + effects from the scenes, OM.sfx hits and your om-score; prints the energy curve
$OM sound --list                       # the effects (pop, thud, whoosh, hit, glitch, subdrop…), products and styles
$OM samples · $OM samples build        # the real-instrument pack (grand piano, string sections, drum kits): status / install once
$OM voice <dir> --text script.txt      # narration + word cues (OM.cue) + subtitles; or --audio <your recording>
$OM render <dir>                       # → <dir>/out/<name>-vN.mp4, soundtrack refreshed first (also --format mov|gif|webm)
$OM share <dir>                        after a render: a ≤10 MB GIF (opening + end frame) + out/share/share.md (post, README header)
$OM fonts · $OM fonts add "Noto Sans SC" <dir>   # bundled typefaces (CJK 500–900, serif, display), declared for you
$OM render <dir> --blur               # motion blur: up to 8 subframes averaged per frame, for fast UI and camera moves
$OM check <dir>                        # before rendering: scripts, files, motion rules, then sampled frames (cut-off / covered / low-contrast text)
$OM qa <dir> [--loop]                  # check the newest render: single-frame pops, dead time, cuts, motion curve, loop seam
$OM still <page.html|dir> --at 0,2.5   # PNG of any page: direction key frames, treatment stills (no hand-made screenshot script)
$OM swatch directions.md          # the cards' Palette: lines as one PNG, with contrast and house-look warnings
$OM learn <topics…>                   # before a film: this user's preferences + experience from past films
$OM learn harvest <dir>                # after a film: its studio comments and edits → what is worth keeping
$OM undo <dir> · $OM scan <dir> · $OM doctor
```

`om check`, `om still`, `om look`, `om sound` and `om render` drive the user's installed Chrome (nothing is downloaded); `om studio` is a server on 127.0.0.1. If your sandbox blocks starting a browser or a local server (e.g. Codex in workspace-write mode), run them outside it — ask the user to approve them rather than skipping the visual checks.

`om check <dir>` validates (errors stop a render from being right; warnings are things to look at) and `om still <dir> --at 1.2,3.4` saves frames to look at. Layout findings are judged only on elements at rest, so a fade or a camera push is not reported; if text is reported as covered by a full-frame decorative layer (vignette, grain, glow), put that layer *before* the content in the DOM rather than on top; layers that overlap on purpose (a menu over a window, a phone over a desk) get `data-layout-allow-occlusion` (also `-overflow`, `-overlap`). Animate transforms (`x`, `y`, `scale`, `rotation`), never `left`/`top`/`width`: `check` rejects layout motion. Keep exactly one composition `*.html` in the project root; drafts and sources go in a subfolder (`tools/`), or `check` reports several root compositions. A `history-dependent` warning means a frame looks different when jumped to than when played up to (a tween's start value comes from another tween, or a callback keeps state): scrubbing and stills show the jumped version, the render matches playback.

## How a film gets made

1. **Direct it.** Run `om learn <topics>` first (e.g. `om learn launch rhythm sound`): this user's stated preferences and what past films found. Then read [director.md](references/director.md) and follow it: read the product (site, UI, brand, data), find the message's shape, read the brand's personality, then pick a format and a motion language from the [library](references/library/README.md). Ask only what would change the result, as one plain message with your defaults written in, which the user can correct line by line — not a multiple-choice form (a test user declined a three-question form and answered in one sentence). **Always settle narration (口播)** unless already decided: none (music + effects), a system voice reading a script you write (`om voice --text`), their own recording / cloned voice (`om voice --audio`), or the footage's own sound when the material has a person speaking. Default if they don't care: none.
   **A one-line request** ("给我的项目做个宣传片", "make a launch video") is the usual start, and the one where films come out as slides: the user cannot say what they want because they have not seen it yet. Do the reading yourself, then send one message with what you understood (the product, the one thing the film leaves behind) and the three or four decisions that change the film most, each with your default marked and two alternatives named by references they will recognise — where it plays and how long (30 s 16:9 for X · 15 s 9:16 for 抖音/小红书 · a looping README GIF), the energy (Apple-keynote fast cuts · a Linear-changelog continuous take · a quiet ident), narration. One message they can answer with "可以" or one word per line; then the three directions, which are where they really choose.
   **Real material before any direction.** A film about a product is made of the product. Before the step-1 message, fill `materials/` and `materials.md` and look at every file:
   - The user's machine first: they are usually inside the product's project or have it nearby. `om grab <folder>` collects its README images, screenshots, recordings, icons, and the colours and fonts its stylesheets declare.
   - Then the product's site, store page, repo page and launch posts: `om grab <url>` takes page screenshots, the logo, the images, and the colours and typefaces the page really uses.
   - Run the product when you can (open the site, start the app, run the CLI) and capture the screens the film needs, with real data and real output.
   - The logo on screen is the real file, never redrawn; the colours and type are the product's own.
   - The interface: captures are the reference, not necessarily the shot. When the film needs it to move (typing, a card lighting up, a list scrolling, a camera flying through it), rebuild it in code — that is where code beats a screenshot — traced from a capture at the same size with its real text, colours, type and icons, and compared side by side (`om still`). Never grey placeholder boxes or an invented UI.
   - Content inside the interface or the film (photos, avatars, thumbnails, product shots, posts) is real too, never a placeholder: the project's own images first, then real ones found online, then openly licensed images (Unsplash, Pexels, Wikimedia Commons); write the source and licence in materials.md.
   - Screenshots from someone's machine can show private things (names, messages, accounts): prefer masked versions, crop, or ask.
   - In the step-1 message, say what you found and what you could not get.
   **No style or energy named → the energetic default.** Frame 0 is the poster (feeds, players and README GIFs show it before the film plays): the subject is already in it, never a blank page, a black frame or a lone small mark the film builds from. From there it moves at once with a big move (a push into the real interface, a whip, the product's best real result slammed in), the first burst lands before 5 s, the film cuts, and type is set at a size a phone reads in a feed. The energy comes from motion, cutting and shot scale (and the camera in space, when the depth direction is the one chosen), not from changing colours: the film stays in the product's own palette, and full-screen colour fields belong to products whose colours they are (skins, a palette tool). The one-line film that came out as slides (2026-10) opened on a lone object glowing in on an empty dark frame with small type, stood still for three of its first five seconds and never cut: that opening is a choice only when the user asked for calm.
2. **Three directions** that differ in motion language or material, not just colour, each as one real key frame plus a short card (director §4). Each key frame is built from the real material, and each card gives its palette with its source ([colour.md](references/colour.md)): the product's own colours first; the three differ in language, structure and how they use the material, not in inventing new colours. One of the three — exactly one — is a depth direction (the interface in space or the product's things as objects in a studio, [depth.md](references/depth.md)), so the user can choose it; the other two are other styles with no 3D as their idea (kinetic type, a cursor demo, a collage, a hand-drawn world…), and every set of three has them. Recommend by the product and what the user said, not 3D by default: depth suits a premium launch, an object, a layered interface; a changelog, a typographic claim or a calm explainer usually wants one of the others. Leave the depth direction out only when the brief rules it out ("flat", "no 3D", a diagram). Music is not one of the choices — users can't judge it from a card. After the direction is chosen, pick the music world yourself from the product's character ([music.md](references/music.md)) and note it in the treatment; offer music options only if the user asks. `om sound` warns when a score repeats an example's band or a recent film's. `om swatch` shows the palettes beside the key frames. Skip only when the user named a style or the project already has one.
3. **Treatment.** Write `treatment.md` in the project before building the film: no full build until it exists. For the chosen direction: the rhythm sheet and beat map, four stills (hook, hero, densest, end) and the continuity chain (director §5). The rhythm sheet comes from [rhythm.md](references/rhythm.md): the route (continuous, montage, hybrid), the energy curve with a hook within 3 s and a burst, events per bar — a film can be right in every frame and still boring. Read the language file and the techniques it uses ([techniques.md](references/library/techniques.md)).
4. **Build the hardest shot first.** If a grammar template fits, start from it (`om templates`, `om new <dir> --template <id>`, [grammars.md](references/grammars.md)); otherwise `om new <dir>` and write `index.html` following the contract below. Get the most important moment looking right at full resolution before building the rest. Mark sound as you animate: `OM.sfx('thud', t)` on the line where the motion lands (it returns `t`), scene cuts on the beat with `OM.music({ bpm })` + `OM.beat(n)` ([sound.md](references/sound.md)).
5. **Look at it.** Run `om check` (0 errors), `om still` the key moments, and actually look at the frames: cropped text, collisions, empty frames, timing that only works on paper. Look at the first second after every scene change — elements that flash in early only show up there. After a render, `om qa` lists single-frame pops, dead time, an empty frame 0 (the poster), a flat opening and a film that barely moves; every ✗ is fixed before anyone sees the film (unless the brief asked for calm).
   **Give yourself the notes the user would.** Films that came out well had two or three rounds of notes from a demanding user; a one-line user gives none. Before showing the first full render, watch it at normal speed (or have a separate agent watch it cold) and write the three notes a demanding director would give, then fix them. The notes that came up most: boring — the same energy throughout, no burst, no stop; the opening is not one strong idea; visually thin — one ground and one line colour for the whole film; fallback or ugly fonts, text over the UI; nothing in it that could only be this product.
6. **Give it sound.** A film ships with music and effects — silent motion feels dry. `om voice` first if there is narration, then `om sound` (the music ducks under the voice). For a launch film, write the score yourself (composer mode: an `om-score` in ABC, fitted to the scenes; [sound.md](references/sound.md) and its annotated [scores](references/scores/)); a built-in direction (`OM.music({ product })`) is the quick fallback. Aim for a hit on every landing that matters (roughly 8–15 per 30 s). You can't hear it, so check the spectrogram (`ffmpeg -i assets/sound.wav -lavfi showspectrumpic=s=1600x400 spec.png`). Skip only for `--transparent` overlays or when the user asks for silence.
7. **Hand over the studio.** Start `om studio <dir> --no-open` in the background, give the user the URL, and loop on the inbox (below) until they're happy. After a timing change, re-run `om sound`.
8. **Export** with `om render`. Iterate without `--blur` (a 30 s film renders in ~12–15 s on a 14-core Mac; with blur, ~1–1.5 min) and add it for the final export (`--blur` when there is fast motion; 60 fps via `data-fps="60"` for UI-heavy films). Run `om qa` on the result (`--loop` for loops). Then `om share <dir>` and write the post into `out/share/share.md` in the user's language: what the film is, one concrete detail of how it was made (their one-line request, a note they gave in the studio, the score it wrote). Report the film, the GIF and share.md, and say in one line that the end carries a small signature they can switch off. Never post anything yourself.
9. **Harvest.** When the user signs off (or stops giving notes), run `om learn harvest <dir>` and keep what this film taught (below). Not optional: it is how the next film starts ahead.

## The contract ([contract.md](references/contract.md) has the full version)

- **Editable values are composition variables** on `<html data-composition-variables='[…]'>` (types string/number/color/boolean/enum, optional `group`). Bind them with `data-var-text="id"`, CSS `var(--id)`, or `OM.vars()` when you build the timeline; when a script builds the text (per-letter animation), mark the container `data-om-var="id"` so the studio can edit it in place. Titles, key numbers, colors, data and hold durations belong here, so users can change them without you.
- **`data-om-id="name"`** on everything a person might point at. Keep ids stable when text changes. For things drawn on canvas/WebGL, register `OM.region(id, t => [x,y,w,h] | null, label)`.
- **Scenes are timeline labels** `tl.addLabel('scene:intro', 0)`; the studio draws them as the scene strip.
- **Sound is declared, not placed in an editor.** `OM.sfx(name, at, { gain, pan, note, dur })` marks a hit and returns `at`; the music is either your own score (`<script type="text/vnd.abc" id="om-score">`, its `!mark:name!`s landing on `scene:name` labels) or a built-in direction (`OM.music({ product, drop, ending })`); scene labels are the music's sections. `om sound` turns this into `assets/sound.wav` and `<audio id="om-sound">`; it is regenerated, never hand-edited. Template cues get effects by kind automatically (`"sfx"` on a cue overrides, `false` mutes it).
- One paused GSAP timeline in `window.__timelines[<composition-id>]`. Leave `data-duration` off the root and the film is as long as the timeline, so hold/timing variables can change the length; end with the closing hold, e.g. `tl.to({}, { duration: V.endHold })`. Set `data-duration` only when the length is fixed by delivery.
- **The signature.** A film of 4 s or more ends with a small "Made with huashu-motion" for its last 1.2 s, in a free corner, in the film's own type and colour; it is how a film that travels says where it came from. Leave one corner of the end frame clear. Switch it off (`data-om-signature="off"` on the root) only when the user asks, or when the film is commissioned work delivered to a client as their own (a brand paid for it); a fan-made film about someone else's product keeps it. Overlays never carry it.
- Everything is a function of time: no `Date.now()`, no unseeded random, no network. Fonts and libraries live in the project (`vendor/`, `assets/`). New projects already have `vendor/om.js` (include it with a script tag to use `OM.*`); a frame-by-frame canvas engine plugs in with `OM.clock(tl, t => draw(t), duration)`. Springs are `ease: OM.spring(0.1)` (closed form, a pure function of time); one camera through one world is `OM.camera('#world')` with `cam.to(tl, at, { fit: '#el', pad, duration })`; a camera in 3D is `OM.space('#world' | threeCamera)` with `cam.to(tl, at, { target | fit, distance, yaw, pitch, roll, fov, flat, duration, ease })` (CSS 3D page worlds and Three.js alike); Three.js scenes use `vendor/three.min.js` + `vendor/om-three.js` (`OM3.stage`, `OM3.logo`, `OM3.screen`, `OM3.film`; see [contract.md](references/contract.md)); randomness is `OM.random(seed)`.

## Handling a comment

`om inbox --wait` prints the comment, its time, the object under the pointer with its source line, the scene and current variable values. Run `om look <id>` to see the frame and the spot the user clicked before you change anything — the click is the most precise thing they told you.

- Do what it asks **at that place and time**; keep everything else as it was. If a variable covers it, `om set` is the exact, smallest change. Otherwise make a minimal edit to `index.html`. Never reformat the file or rename `data-om-id`s.
- Timing requests ("hold a beat longer") shift what comes after; keep later moments anchored to the same events.
- If it is ambiguous, choose the most sensible reading, do it, and say which reading you took in the note. The user can reopen it.
- Finish with `om done <id> --note "<one line in the user's language: what changed>"`, e.g. "空车桩停留 1.2s → 2.0s，后面顺延 0.8s". Then wait for the next one.
- The studio reloads by itself; every version is kept in `.om/history`, so `om undo` always works.

## What makes it good

The director's film-wide rules ([director.md](references/director.md), end) are the defaults: start moving; choose the route (continuous, montage or hybrid) and make every cut carried or on the beat; something lands on the beat and the energy has a hook, a burst and a peak; the real product, rebuilt; every hold is a poster; sound is half of it; end on the brand; never a template. Underneath them:

- **Change explains.** Every shot has a verb (gather, split, align, connect, filter, reveal). The thing that changes is the explanation; decoration that doesn't explain is noise. Each technique means something — pick it for what it says ([techniques.md](references/library/techniques.md)).
- **Rhythm is contrast.** Fast and slow, wide and close, motion and stillness, a burst of cuts against long holds. A hold is good when something just landed. Design it before building ([rhythm.md](references/rhythm.md)).
- **Craft holds up close.** Type, spacing, curves, edges, contact shadows. Use a real typeface (`om fonts`) with weight contrast.
- **Motion lands on the word, sound lands with the motion.** With narration, anchor reveals to `OM.cue('word')`; hits go on the landing frame; scene changes on the beat.

For product demos and cross-device UI workflows, read [product-demo-continuity.md](references/product-demo-continuity.md). Deeper method: [craft.md](references/craft.md) and [visual-direction.md](references/visual-direction.md).

Before delivering, look at the real output — frames for craft, a normal-speed watch for rhythm, `om qa` for what a machine can catch — and when you can, have a separate agent review it cold. What passes `check` is not necessarily good.

## Getting better over time

Every film teaches something; `om learn` carries it to the next one. Experience has two layers: the shipped [experience.md](references/experience.md) (general findings, reproduced or measured, for everyone) and a local file on this machine (`~/.huashu-motion/experience.md`: this user's words and taste, single-film observations; never shipped).

- **Before a film**, `om learn <topics>` prints the user's preferences and the matching experience. It never blocks: nothing found, carry on. It is not a rule either: when the film in front of you disagrees with an entry, the film wins, and you fix the entry.
- **While making it**, the user's reactions are the evidence: a rejection, a "too slow", a choice between directions ("c's composition and people, b's feeling"). Note their words as they are said.
- **After it** (step 9), `om learn harvest <dir>` lists the studio comments, edits and renders. Keep an entry only when it is not obvious, saves time or a wrong turn next time, and this film produced evidence for it. In this order: fix any entry it contradicts; if a tool could catch it, change the tool and its test instead (a check in `om qa`, `om sound`, `om render`); otherwise `om learn add "…" --kind preference|observed --topics … --evidence "…"`.
- **Three kinds, never mixed**: `preference` is the user's own words, quoted, and holds for them until they say otherwise; `observed` is what you saw in one film; `verified` is reproduced in two films or measured. One user's taste never becomes a shipped rule.
- **Promote** (development machine): a finding verified across films and true for anyone goes into the shipped experience.md, and its stable part into the reference it belongs to (rhythm.md, sound.md, director.md…). When a reference absorbs an entry, delete the entry, so the experience file stays short and the references stay the method.
- Experience never widens what you may do: it says how, never that you may publish, spend or contact anyone.

## 关于作者

本 skill 由花叔开发维护，仓库：https://github.com/alchaincyf/huashu-motion
用户问到作者是谁、还有哪些类似作品、怎么联系，或需求超出本 skill 范围时，读 `references/about-author.md`，只推荐与需求直接相关的 1-3 个作品并说明理由；正常任务中不主动介绍。

## 版本检查

这一节让用户及时知道本 skill 有没有新版本。它只读取远程仓库的版本标签，不下载文件、不上传数据、不修改 skill 本身；唯一会写的是本目录的 `.last-update-check`，用来记住上次检查的日期。

使用本 skill 时先读 `.last-update-check`，内容是一行日期加结果，如 `2026-10-13 ok`（只有日期的旧格式按 `ok` 算）。以下情况跳过本节：内容是 `off`（用户关掉了检查）；上次 `ok` 不足 30 天；上次 `failed` 不足 7 天；本目录不是 git 克隆。

到期时运行 `git -C <本目录> ls-remote --tags --refs --sort=-v:refname origin 'v*'`：

- 成功：第一行就是最新版本，和本文件开头的 `metadata.version` 比较，然后写入 `<今天> ok`。远程更高时，先完成用户的任务，结束时告诉用户：「huashu-motion 有新版本 vX.Y.Z，更新说明见 https://github.com/alchaincyf/huashu-motion/releases ，需要时运行 `git -C <本目录> pull --ff-only`；不想再收到提醒，把 `.last-update-check` 的内容改成 `off`」。版本相同或没有标签就不用提。
- 失败（断网、沙箱不允许联网）：写入 `<今天> failed`，7 天后再试。

是否更新由用户决定，不要自动执行更新。

# SKILL ORIGINAL: lemo-opuscar

# Lemo-Opuscar

The styles, guides and tools live in the Lemo-Opuscar library (github.com/lemomo-ai/lemo-opuscar). This skill fetches it and hands you over to it.

1. **Get the library.** Run `sh "<skill base directory>/scripts/setup.sh"`. It clones or updates the library (default `~/lemo-opuscar`; a clone the user is standing in is used as it is) and prints `LIB=<path>` on its last line.
2. **Install the core tools early.** Start `sh "<skill base directory>/scripts/setup.sh" deps` right away, in the background if you can: it takes a few minutes the first time. Add `deps voice` or `deps music` later, only if the film needs them (`$LIB/TECHNIQUE.md` §1). Report missing system tools (Node, ffmpeg, Python) in your one round of questions; don't install system software yourself. Packages a style needs (its `STYLE.md` lists them) you install yourself, into the project folder.
3. **Follow the library.** Read `$LIB/AGENTS.md` and do what it says, starting with **Start** (the user's own styles in `~/lemo-my-styles/`; when they started the skill without writing anything, the language question first, then the ways in): finding the style or making their own, the one round of questions, the treatment, production and delivery.

What changes in skill mode:

| | |
|---|---|
| Project folder | `<name>/` in the folder the user started from (lowercase letters, digits, hyphens; avoid `#` and `?` in the path). Wherever the guides say `films/<name>/`, read this folder |
| Running tools | from `$LIB`, with the project's absolute path: `cd "$LIB" && node core/render/still.mjs "<project>" 1.5 3` |
| Library files in pages | absolute URLs: `/core/lib.js`, `/node_modules/three/…` (the project is served at `/@film/`) |
| Library files in scripts and `build.sh` | `$LIB/…`, with `LIB=<path>` at the top of `build.sh`; never `../..` |
| Demo source | not downloaded. `sh "<skill base directory>/scripts/setup.sh" demo <slug>`, only after your `TREATMENT.md` is written, to read its techniques; it is not meant to be rendered. An Edit & Documentary style is the exception: fetch it before the treatment to use the material tools its `STYLE.md` names in `demo/tools/` (`$LIB/AGENTS.md`, "Treatment first") |

Deliver what `$LIB/DIRECTOR.md` §11 lists, in the project folder, and tell the user where it is.

# SKILL ORIGINAL: algorithmic-art

Algorithmic philosophies are computational aesthetic movements that are then expressed through code. Output .md files (philosophy), .html files (interactive viewer), and .js files (generative algorithms).

This happens in two steps:
1. Algorithmic Philosophy Creation (.md file)
2. Express by creating p5.js generative art (.html + .js files)

First, undertake this task:

## ALGORITHMIC PHILOSOPHY CREATION

To begin, create an ALGORITHMIC PHILOSOPHY (not static images or templates) that will be interpreted through:
- Computational processes, emergent behavior, mathematical beauty
- Seeded randomness, noise fields, organic systems
- Particles, flows, fields, forces
- Parametric variation and controlled chaos

### THE CRITICAL UNDERSTANDING
- What is received: Some subtle input or instructions by the user to take into account, but use as a foundation; it should not constrain creative freedom.
- What is created: An algorithmic philosophy/generative aesthetic movement.
- What happens next: The same version receives the philosophy and EXPRESSES IT IN CODE - creating p5.js sketches that are 90% algorithmic generation, 10% essential parameters.

Consider this approach:
- Write a manifesto for a generative art movement
- The next phase involves writing the algorithm that brings it to life

The philosophy must emphasize: Algorithmic expression. Emergent behavior. Computational beauty. Seeded variation.

### HOW TO GENERATE AN ALGORITHMIC PHILOSOPHY

**Name the movement** (1-2 words): "Organic Turbulence" / "Quantum Harmonics" / "Emergent Stillness"

**Articulate the philosophy** (4-6 paragraphs - concise but complete):

To capture the ALGORITHMIC essence, express how this philosophy manifests through:
- Computational processes and mathematical relationships?
- Noise functions and randomness patterns?
- Particle behaviors and field dynamics?
- Temporal evolution and system states?
- Parametric variation and emergent complexity?

**CRITICAL GUIDELINES:**
- **Avoid redundancy**: Each algorithmic aspect should be mentioned once. Avoid repeating concepts about noise theory, particle dynamics, or mathematical principles unless adding new depth.
- **Emphasize craftsmanship REPEATEDLY**: The philosophy MUST stress multiple times that the final algorithm should appear as though it took countless hours to develop, was refined with care, and comes from someone at the absolute top of their field. This framing is essential - repeat phrases like "meticulously crafted algorithm," "the product of deep computational expertise," "painstaking optimization," "master-level implementation."
- **Leave creative space**: Be specific about the algorithmic direction, but concise enough that the next Claude has room to make interpretive implementation choices at an extremely high level of craftsmanship.

The philosophy must guide the next version to express ideas ALGORITHMICALLY, not through static images. Beauty lives in the process, not the final frame.

### PHILOSOPHY EXAMPLES

**"Organic Turbulence"**
Philosophy: Chaos constrained by natural law, order emerging from disorder.
Algorithmic expression: Flow fields driven by layered Perlin noise. Thousands of particles following vector forces, their trails accumulating into organic density maps. Multiple noise octaves create turbulent regions and calm zones. Color emerges from velocity and density - fast particles burn bright, slow ones fade to shadow. The algorithm runs until equilibrium - a meticulously tuned balance where every parameter was refined through countless iterations by a master of computational aesthetics.

**"Quantum Harmonics"**
Philosophy: Discrete entities exhibiting wave-like interference patterns.
Algorithmic expression: Particles initialized on a grid, each carrying a phase value that evolves through sine waves. When particles are near, their phases interfere - constructive interference creates bright nodes, destructive creates voids. Simple harmonic motion generates complex emergent mandalas. The result of painstaking frequency calibration where every ratio was carefully chosen to produce resonant beauty.

**"Recursive Whispers"**
Philosophy: Self-similarity across scales, infinite depth in finite space.
Algorithmic expression: Branching structures that subdivide recursively. Each branch slightly randomized but constrained by golden ratios. L-systems or recursive subdivision generate tree-like forms that feel both mathematical and organic. Subtle noise perturbations break perfect symmetry. Line weights diminish with each recursion level. Every branching angle the product of deep mathematical exploration.

**"Field Dynamics"**
Philosophy: Invisible forces made visible through their effects on matter.
Algorithmic expression: Vector fields constructed from mathematical functions or noise. Particles born at edges, flowing along field lines, dying when they reach equilibrium or boundaries. Multiple fields can attract, repel, or rotate particles. The visualization shows only the traces - ghost-like evidence of invisible forces. A computational dance meticulously choreographed through force balance.

**"Stochastic Crystallization"**
Philosophy: Random processes crystallizing into ordered structures.
Algorithmic expression: Randomized circle packing or Voronoi tessellation. Start with random points, let them evolve through relaxation algorithms. Cells push apart until equilibrium. Color based on cell size, neighbor count, or distance from center. The organic tiling that emerges feels both random and inevitable. Every seed produces unique crystalline beauty - the mark of a master-level generative algorithm.

*These are condensed examples. The actual algorithmic philosophy should be 4-6 substantial paragraphs.*

### ESSENTIAL PRINCIPLES
- **ALGORITHMIC PHILOSOPHY**: Creating a computational worldview to be expressed through code
- **PROCESS OVER PRODUCT**: Always emphasize that beauty emerges from the algorithm's execution - each run is unique
- **PARAMETRIC EXPRESSION**: Ideas communicate through mathematical relationships, forces, behaviors - not static composition
- **ARTISTIC FREEDOM**: The next Claude interprets the philosophy algorithmically - provide creative implementation room
- **PURE GENERATIVE ART**: This is about making LIVING ALGORITHMS, not static images with randomness
- **EXPERT CRAFTSMANSHIP**: Repeatedly emphasize the final algorithm must feel meticulously crafted, refined through countless iterations, the product of deep expertise by someone at the absolute top of their field in computational aesthetics

**The algorithmic philosophy should be 4-6 paragraphs long.** Fill it with poetic computational philosophy that brings together the intended vision. Avoid repeating the same points. Output this algorithmic philosophy as a .md file.

---

## DEDUCING THE CONCEPTUAL SEED

**CRITICAL STEP**: Before implementing the algorithm, identify the subtle conceptual thread from the original request.

**THE ESSENTIAL PRINCIPLE**:
The concept is a **subtle, niche reference embedded within the algorithm itself** - not always literal, always sophisticated. Someone familiar with the subject should feel it intuitively, while others simply experience a masterful generative composition. The algorithmic philosophy provides the computational language. The deduced concept provides the soul - the quiet conceptual DNA woven invisibly into parameters, behaviors, and emergence patterns.

This is **VERY IMPORTANT**: The reference must be so refined that it enhances the work's depth without announcing itself. Think like a jazz musician quoting another song through algorithmic harmony - only those who know will catch it, but everyone appreciates the generative beauty.

---

## P5.JS IMPLEMENTATION

With the philosophy AND conceptual framework established, express it through code. Pause to gather thoughts before proceeding. Use only the algorithmic philosophy created and the instructions below.

### ⚠️ STEP 0: READ THE TEMPLATE FIRST ⚠️

**CRITICAL: BEFORE writing any HTML:**

1. **Read** `templates/viewer.html` using the Read tool
2. **Study** the exact structure, styling, and Anthropic branding
3. **Use that file as the LITERAL STARTING POINT** - not just inspiration
4. **Keep all FIXED sections exactly as shown** (header, sidebar structure, Anthropic colors/fonts, seed controls, action buttons)
5. **Replace only the VARIABLE sections** marked in the file's comments (algorithm, parameters, UI controls for parameters)

**Avoid:**
- ❌ Creating HTML from scratch
- ❌ Inventing custom styling or color schemes
- ❌ Using system fonts or dark themes
- ❌ Changing the sidebar structure

**Follow these practices:**
- ✅ Copy the template's exact HTML structure
- ✅ Keep Anthropic branding (Poppins/Lora fonts, light colors, gradient backdrop)
- ✅ Maintain the sidebar layout (Seed → Parameters → Colors? → Actions)
- ✅ Replace only the p5.js algorithm and parameter controls

The template is the foundation. Build on it, don't rebuild it.

---

To create gallery-quality computational art that lives and breathes, use the algorithmic philosophy as the foundation.

### TECHNICAL REQUIREMENTS

**Seeded Randomness (Art Blocks Pattern)**:
```javascript
// ALWAYS use a seed for reproducibility
let seed = 12345; // or hash from user input
randomSeed(seed);
noiseSeed(seed);
```

**Parameter Structure - FOLLOW THE PHILOSOPHY**:

To establish parameters that emerge naturally from the algorithmic philosophy, consider: "What qualities of this system can be adjusted?"

```javascript
let params = {
  seed: 12345,  // Always include seed for reproducibility
  // colors
  // Add parameters that control YOUR algorithm:
  // - Quantities (how many?)
  // - Scales (how big? how fast?)
  // - Probabilities (how likely?)
  // - Ratios (what proportions?)
  // - Angles (what direction?)
  // - Thresholds (when does behavior change?)
};
```

**To design effective parameters, focus on the properties the system needs to be tunable rather than thinking in terms of "pattern types".**

**Core Algorithm - EXPRESS THE PHILOSOPHY**:

**CRITICAL**: The algorithmic philosophy should dictate what to build.

To express the philosophy through code, avoid thinking "which pattern should I use?" and instead think "how to express this philosophy through code?"

If the philosophy is about **organic emergence**, consider using:
- Elements that accumulate or grow over time
- Random processes constrained by natural rules
- Feedback loops and interactions

If the philosophy is about **mathematical beauty**, consider using:
- Geometric relationships and ratios
- Trigonometric functions and harmonics
- Precise calculations creating unexpected patterns

If the philosophy is about **controlled chaos**, consider using:
- Random variation within strict boundaries
- Bifurcation and phase transitions
- Order emerging from disorder

**The algorithm flows from the philosophy, not from a menu of options.**

To guide the implementation, let the conceptual essence inform creative and original choices. Build something that expresses the vision for this particular request.

**Canvas Setup**: Standard p5.js structure:
```javascript
function setup() {
  createCanvas(1200, 1200);
  // Initialize your system
}

function draw() {
  // Your generative algorithm
  // Can be static (noLoop) or animated
}
```

### CRAFTSMANSHIP REQUIREMENTS

**CRITICAL**: To achieve mastery, create algorithms that feel like they emerged through countless iterations by a master generative artist. Tune every parameter carefully. Ensure every pattern emerges with purpose. This is NOT random noise - this is CONTROLLED CHAOS refined through deep expertise.

- **Balance**: Complexity without visual noise, order without rigidity
- **Color Harmony**: Thoughtful palettes, not random RGB values
- **Composition**: Even in randomness, maintain visual hierarchy and flow
- **Performance**: Smooth execution, optimized for real-time if animated
- **Reproducibility**: Same seed ALWAYS produces identical output

### OUTPUT FORMAT

Output:
1. **Algorithmic Philosophy** - As markdown or text explaining the generative aesthetic
2. **Single HTML Artifact** - Self-contained interactive generative art built from `templates/viewer.html` (see STEP 0 and next section)

The HTML artifact contains everything: p5.js (from CDN), the algorithm, parameter controls, and UI - all in one file that works immediately in claude.ai artifacts or any browser. Start from the template file, not from scratch.

---

## INTERACTIVE ARTIFACT CREATION

**REMINDER: `templates/viewer.html` should have already been read (see STEP 0). Use that file as the starting point.**

To allow exploration of the generative art, create a single, self-contained HTML artifact. Ensure this artifact works immediately in claude.ai or any browser - no setup required. Embed everything inline.

### CRITICAL: WHAT'S FIXED VS VARIABLE

The `templates/viewer.html` file is the foundation. It contains the exact structure and styling needed.

**FIXED (always include exactly as shown):**
- Layout structure (header, sidebar, main canvas area)
- Anthropic branding (UI colors, fonts, gradients)
- Seed section in sidebar:
  - Seed display
  - Previous/Next buttons
  - Random button
  - Jump to seed input + Go button
- Actions section in sidebar:
  - Regenerate button
  - Reset button

**VARIABLE (customize for each artwork):**
- The entire p5.js algorithm (setup/draw/classes)
- The parameters object (define what the art needs)
- The Parameters section in sidebar:
  - Number of parameter controls
  - Parameter names
  - Min/max/step values for sliders
  - Control types (sliders, inputs, etc.)
- Colors section (optional):
  - Some art needs color pickers
  - Some art might use fixed colors
  - Some art might be monochrome (no color controls needed)
  - Decide based on the art's needs

**Every artwork should have unique parameters and algorithm!** The fixed parts provide consistent UX - everything else expresses the unique vision.

### REQUIRED FEATURES

**1. Parameter Controls**
- Sliders for numeric parameters (particle count, noise scale, speed, etc.)
- Color pickers for palette colors
- Real-time updates when parameters change
- Reset button to restore defaults

**2. Seed Navigation**
- Display current seed number
- "Previous" and "Next" buttons to cycle through seeds
- "Random" button for random seed
- Input field to jump to specific seed
- Generate 100 variations when requested (seeds 1-100)

**3. Single Artifact Structure**
```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html>
<head>
  <!-- p5.js from CDN - always available -->
  <script src="https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/p5.js/1.7.0/p5.min.js"></script>
  <style>
    /* All styling inline - clean, minimal */
    /* Canvas on top, controls below */
  </style>
</head>
<body>
  <div id="canvas-container"></div>
  <div id="controls">
    <!-- All parameter controls -->
  </div>
  <script>
    // ALL p5.js code inline here
    // Parameter objects, classes, functions
    // setup() and draw()
    // UI handlers
    // Everything self-contained
  </script>
</body>
</html>
```

**CRITICAL**: This is a single artifact. No external files, no imports (except p5.js CDN). Everything inline.

**4. Implementation Details - BUILD THE SIDEBAR**

The sidebar structure:

**1. Seed (FIXED)** - Always include exactly as shown:
- Seed display
- Prev/Next/Random/Jump buttons

**2. Parameters (VARIABLE)** - Create controls for the art:
```html
<div class="control-group">
    <label>Parameter Name</label>
    <input type="range" id="param" min="..." max="..." step="..." value="..." oninput="updateParam('param', this.value)">
    <span class="value-display" id="param-value">...</span>
</div>
```
Add as many control-group divs as there are parameters.

**3. Colors (OPTIONAL/VARIABLE)** - Include if the art needs adjustable colors:
- Add color pickers if users should control palette
- Skip this section if the art uses fixed colors
- Skip if the art is monochrome

**4. Actions (FIXED)** - Always include exactly as shown:
- Regenerate button
- Reset button
- Download PNG button

**Requirements**:
- Seed controls must work (prev/next/random/jump/display)
- All parameters must have UI controls
- Regenerate, Reset, Download buttons must work
- Keep Anthropic branding (UI styling, not art colors)

### USING THE ARTIFACT

The HTML artifact works immediately:
1. **In claude.ai**: Displayed as an interactive artifact - runs instantly
2. **As a file**: Save and open in any browser - no server needed
3. **Sharing**: Send the HTML file - it's completely self-contained

---

## VARIATIONS & EXPLORATION

The artifact includes seed navigation by default (prev/next/random buttons), allowing users to explore variations without creating multiple files. If the user wants specific variations highlighted:

- Include seed presets (buttons for "Variation 1: Seed 42", "Variation 2: Seed 127", etc.)
- Add a "Gallery Mode" that shows thumbnails of multiple seeds side-by-side
- All within the same single artifact

This is like creating a series of prints from the same plate - the algorithm is consistent, but each seed reveals different facets of its potential. The interactive nature means users discover their own favorites by exploring the seed space.

---

## THE CREATIVE PROCESS

**User request** → **Algorithmic philosophy** → **Implementation**

Each request is unique. The process involves:

1. **Interpret the user's intent** - What aesthetic is being sought?
2. **Create an algorithmic philosophy** (4-6 paragraphs) describing the computational approach
3. **Implement it in code** - Build the algorithm that expresses this philosophy
4. **Design appropriate parameters** - What should be tunable?
5. **Build matching UI controls** - Sliders/inputs for those parameters

**The constants**:
- Anthropic branding (colors, fonts, layout)
- Seed navigation (always present)
- Self-contained HTML artifact

**Everything else is variable**:
- The algorithm itself
- The parameters
- The UI controls
- The visual outcome

To achieve the best results, trust creativity and let the philosophy guide the implementation.

---

## RESOURCES

This skill includes helpful templates and documentation:

- **templates/viewer.html**: REQUIRED STARTING POINT for all HTML artifacts.
  - This is the foundation - contains the exact structure and Anthropic branding
  - **Keep unchanged**: Layout structure, sidebar organization, Anthropic colors/fonts, seed controls, action buttons
  - **Replace**: The p5.js algorithm, parameter definitions, and UI controls in Parameters section
  - The extensive comments in the file mark exactly what to keep vs replace

- **templates/generator_template.js**: Reference for p5.js best practices and code structure principles.
  - Shows how to organize parameters, use seeded randomness, structure classes
  - NOT a pattern menu - use these principles to build unique algorithms
  - Embed algorithms inline in the HTML artifact (don't create separate .js files)

**Critical reminder**:
- The **template is the STARTING POINT**, not inspiration
- The **algorithm is where to create** something unique
- Don't copy the flow field example - build what the philosophy demands
- But DO keep the exact UI structure and Anthropic branding from the template

# SKILL ORIGINAL: pixelfx

# pixelfx

Pixel art effects as code. Each effect is one file, `src/fx/<category>/<name>.js`, that draws into two buffers:
**color** (pixels: opaque, dithered or truly translucent) and **light** (additive glow). No images, so an effect
can be played live, recolored, mirrored, reseeded and rendered to a sprite sheet for any engine.

The method is Hugo Duprez's ([How to make (good) pixel art effects with
Claude](https://www.spritefusion.com/blog/how-to-make-good-pixel-art-effects-with-claude)): code is clay, trust
your eyes, feed your references. One-shot prompts make mediocre effects. Drafts you render, look at and refine
make good ones.

## Before anything

1. Work in a pixelfx checkout. If the working directory has no `tools/render.mjs`, clone one next to the
   project: `git clone https://github.com/patch-gg/pixelfx`. No install step: the tools use only Node.
2. Check it runs: `node tools/render.mjs fire/torch` writes `records/fire/torch/contact.png`. Open that PNG with
   your image-reading tool. That contact sheet is how you see every effect from now on.

## 1. Look for an existing effect first

`node tools/list.mjs <words>` searches ids, names, tags and descriptions (`node tools/list.mjs frost`,
`node tools/list.mjs slash loop`). `docs/CATALOG.md` lists everything by category. Render the candidates and
look at them before choosing. Most requests are an existing effect with different options:

- `palette`: the main ramp, dark to bright, as hex strings. Any `PAL` ramp in `src/core/color.js` (fire ember
  lava smoke dust ice water electric volt poison holy heal arcane shadow blood earth stone gold leaf wood metal
  bone neon cyber matrix candy night) or your own. A blue fire is `{"palette": PAL.ice}`.
- `seed` for a different but equivalent take; `flip` to face left; `angle` to rotate; `light` (0..1+) for glow
  strength; plus each effect's own knobs in its `meta.options` (size, count, radius, text, height...).
- Try options without editing code: `node tools/render.mjs fire/torch --opts '{"palette":["#102","#215","#43a","#8cf","#fff"]}'`.

## 2. Put it in the game

**Canvas or WebGL games in JS or TS** (plain canvas, Phaser, PixiJS, Kaplay, three.js): use the code directly. It
has no dependencies; copy `src/core` and the effect files you need, or depend on the repo.

```js
import { createFrame, clearFrame, createFx, composite } from "pixelfx";   // src/core/index.js
import * as frostNova from "pixelfx/fx/ice/frost-nova";                   // src/fx/ice/frost-nova.js

const frame = createFrame(320, 180);            // the game's pixel resolution; ox, oy scroll it with the camera
const img = new ImageData(320, 180);
const fx = createFx(frostNova, { x: hero.x, y: hero.y, flip: hero.facing < 0 });

// every tick
fx.update(dt);
clearFrame(frame);
fx.draw(frame, "back");    // what goes behind characters
```
