# SKILL ORIGINAL: nuevo-video

# Nuevo video

La persona puede no saber programar: habla simple, corre tú los comandos y nunca le pidas que pegue claves en el chat.

| Lo que escribe | Qué haces |
|---|---|
| `/nuevo-video <tiktok\|youtube\|ambos> <tema>` | **Video** (pasos 0–9). Si no dice la plataforma, pregunta: "¿TikTok, YouTube o ambos?" |
| `/nuevo-video editorial [cambio]` | **Línea editorial y fondos**: crearla o cambiar algo puntual (sección al final). |
| `/nuevo-video voz` | **Voces**: ver o cambiar la voz de cada personaje y el modo (sección al final). |
| "dame los textos de `<id>`" | Ve directo al paso 6. |
| "ya dejé mi audio de `<id>`" | `npm run sincronizar -- <id>` → paso 8. |

## Video

0. **Preparación** (rápida, cada vez): `npm run estado`. La última línea dice
   `ESTADO node=… paquetes=… personajes=… canal=… editorial=… clave=… modo=…`.
   Si `npm` o `node` no se reconocen, o dice `node=viejo` o `paquetes=faltan`: haz "Preparar el estudio" de
   `CLAUDE.md` (instala lo que falte) y vuelve aquí.
1. **Perfiles.** Solo se piden si no hay uno propio:
   - `personajes=ejemplo` o `falta` → pregunta: **Crear mis personajes** (sigue el skill `personajes`, en
     `.claude/skills/personajes/SKILL.md`, y vuelve aquí) · **Usar los de ejemplo por ahora** ·
     **Quedarme con los de ejemplo** (pon `"plantilla": false` en `perfiles/personajes.json` para no volver a preguntar).
   - `canal` o `editorial` = `ejemplo` o `falta` → pregunta: **Configurar mi línea editorial** (sección
     "Línea editorial" de abajo, y vuelve aquí) · **Usar la de ejemplo por ahora** · **Quedarme con la de ejemplo**
     (pon `plantilla: false` en `perfiles/linea-editorial.md` y `"plantilla": false` en `perfiles/canal.json`).
   - "Por ahora" vale para esta conversación: no lo vuelvas a preguntar hasta la próxima.
   - Lee **`CLAUDE.md`** (diálogo, tipos de escena, encuadres, poses, reglas), **`perfiles/linea-editorial.md`**
     (quién habla, duración y estructura por plataforma), y de **`perfiles/personajes.json`** y **`perfiles/canal.json`**
     los nombres, papeles, personalidades, extras, @usuario y lema.
2. **Fondo** de cada video: `npm run ambientes -- --siguiente <tiktok|youtube>` (rotan claros y oscuros por plataforma).
   Si son dos videos, cada uno con el de su plataforma.
3. **Propón el guion primero**, sin crear archivos. Una tabla por plataforma con columnas:
   **escena · tipo y encuadre · quién · qué dice · qué se ve**. Debajo: fondo elegido y "~N palabras · ~S s"
   (~2.5 palabras por segundo de voz).
   - Sigue **exactamente** `perfiles/linea-editorial.md` para cada plataforma: duración, escenas, líneas, palabras,
     largo máximo de cada línea, gancho, estructura, cierre (`pregunta` o `curso`) y series.
   - **El diálogo es natural, no forzado:** cada línea la dice quien mejor transmite la idea, según "Quién habla" de la
     línea editorial y la personalidad de cada personaje. Usa los nombres reales de los personajes en la tabla.
     La voz en off solo cuando suma (dato, fecha, caso, transición; máx. 1 línea por escena). Los extras solo si la idea
     necesita a esa persona (máx. 1–2 escenas). Una escena con una sola voz está bien.
   - **Variedad visual:** no más de 2 `duo` seguidos, al menos una escena dinámica (`diagrama`, `pizarra`, `linea`,
     `pasos`, `comparar`). En listas y diagramas, cada elemento aparece cuando se nombra (`aparece`).
   - **Ambos:** mismo tema, dos guiones distintos (no copiar uno en el otro), cada uno con su serie.
   - Última línea: la firma de la línea editorial, dicha por el principal (con el @usuario escrito, ej. `@tucanal`).
   - `serie`: `"<TEMA> · <LEMA>"`. Verifica datos y cifras. Respeta la lista "Evitar" de la línea editorial.
   - Pregunta: **Aprobar** · **Ajustar** (qué cambiar). Itera hasta el visto bueno.
4. Con el visto bueno, crea `src/videos/<tema>-tiktok.ts` y/o `src/videos/<tema>-youtube.ts` (kebab-case, sin tildes)
   con `canal`, `ambiente` y `dialogo` en cada escena, y regístralos **al final** de `src/videos/index.ts`
   (el orden define la rotación de fondos). Usa `src/videos/cielo-azul-*.ts` como modelo de formato.
5. **Verifica:** `npm run typecheck` y `npm run revisar -- <id>`. Mira **solo** `out/revisar/<id>-hoja.png`
   (una toma por línea en una sola imagen): textos que se salen, solapes con personajes o títulos, tomas donde no se ve
   a quien habla. Corrige y vuelve a revisar. Las imágenes sueltas de `out/revisar/<id>/` son solo para ampliar un detalle.
6. **Entrega de textos** (siempre, por cada video):
   1. Corre `npm run guion -- <id>`: genera `out/<id>-guion-voz.txt` (con quién dice cada línea) y
      `out/<id>-guion-plano.txt` (solo el texto).
   2. Escribe los textos de publicación en `out/<id>-publicar.md`, siguiendo la sección "Textos para publicar"
      de `perfiles/linea-editorial.md` (descripción de TikTok; título y descripción de YouTube; hashtags fijos).
   3. **Muéstralos en el chat**, cada uno en su propio bloque de código para copiar y pegar, sin markdown dentro:
      **Guion por voces** (el contenido exacto de `out/<id>-guion-voz.txt`) y los textos de publicación.
7. **Voces con ElevenLabs. Confirma antes de generar**, una sola vez aunque sean dos videos:
   1. Corre `npm run voz:ia -- <id> --plan` por cada video: muestra qué voz tiene cada personaje, cuántas líneas dice,
      el modo y los caracteres. No gasta nada y funciona sin clave.
   2. **Si dice `SIN_CLAVE`** (no hay clave de ElevenLabs), pregunta:
      - **Pegar mi clave de ElevenLabs** → explícale: elevenlabs.io → Developers → API Keys → crear una con
        permiso Text to Speech (y Voices: Read para ver los nombres) → abrir el archivo `.env` de esta carpeta →
        pegarla después de `ELEVENLABS_API_KEY=` → guardar → avisarte. Luego vuelve a correr el `--plan`.
      - **Usar mi propio audio** (grabado o de otra app) → que lea el guion por voces del paso 6 **en orden, con una
        pausa corta entre líneas**, y guarde el audio como **`public/audio/<id>.mp3`** (también vale .wav o .m4a),
        un archivo por video, y te avise. Entonces corre `npm run sincronizar -- <id>` y sigue al paso 8.
      - **Sin voz por ahora** → sigue al paso 8 (vista previa en silencio; se le pone voz después).
   3. **Con clave, pregunta** (con la herramienta de preguntas si está disponible), mostrando las voces del plan:
      "¿Genero las voces así? Voz en off: <voz> · <Principal>: <voz> · <Secundario>: <voz> · Extras: <voz>.
      Usará ~N caracteres de los M disponibles." Si todos tienen la misma voz, dilo en una línea.
      Opciones: **Generar con estas voces** · **Cambiar alguna voz** · **Sin voz IA (usaré mi audio o ninguno)**.
      No generes nada sin su respuesta.
   4. **Cambiar alguna voz:** `npm run voces` lista las voces de su cuenta (nombre + voice ID), o acepta un voice ID
      que pegue. Pregunta qué personaje cambia y si es **solo para este video** (`npm run voz:ia -- <id> --secundario <voiceId>`;
      también `--principal`, `--narrador`, `--extra`) o **su nueva voz por defecto** (`npm run voces -- --usar <personaje> <voiceId>`).
      Si quiere oírla antes: `npm run voces -- --escuchar <personaje>` (el comando dice dónde quedó el mp3, en `out/voces/`).
   5. **Genera:** `npm run voz:ia -- <id>` (con las banderas de voz si eligió "solo para este video"), una vez por video.
      Crea `public/audio/<id>.mp3` y los subtítulos sincronizados con quién habla. Lo ya generado queda en caché:
      si luego cambia una línea, solo se paga esa.
   6. Si falla, explica el mensaje en simple. Si una voz **no está en su cuenta** (por ejemplo la voz por defecto, que
      es de otra cuenta), muéstrale sus voces con `npm run voces` para elegir otra. Si no hay créditos o la clave no
      tiene permisos, ofrece las opciones de "sin clave".
8. **Exporta el video:** `npm run render -- <id>` por cada video (2–5 min). Con voz sale `out/<id>.mp4` (video final).
   Sin voz sale `out/<id>-SIN-VOZ.mp4`: una vista previa **en silencio**; díselo con esas palabras, nunca la presentes
   como el video final. Si el render se detiene por "voz sin sincronizar", corre `npm run sincronizar -- <id>` y vuelve a exportar.
9. **Cierra** con una lista corta: fondo usado, voces (nombre por personaje, o "audio propio" / "sin voz"), duración de
   cada video y las rutas `out/<id>.mp4`, `out/<id>-guion-voz.txt`, `out/<id>-publicar.md`. Si quedó sin voz, recuerda
   en una línea dónde dejar el audio (`public/audio/<id>.mp3`) para terminarlo.

Si un tema necesita una escena que no existe, propón el tipo nuevo antes de crearlo (ver "Añadir un tipo de escena nuevo" en `CLAUDE.md`).

## Línea editorial (`/nuevo-video editorial`)

Vive en dos archivos: **`perfiles/linea-editorial.md`** (cómo se escribe: tono, público, quién habla, reglas y
estructura por plataforma, textos de publicación) y **`perfiles/canal.json`** (lo que se ve en el video: @usuario,
cómo se lee, lema, redes, cierre, estilo `agil`/`clase` por plataforma y fondos). El ejemplo está en `plantillas/ejemplo/`.

**Crear** (si es de ejemplo o no existe). Muestra primero, en 5–6 líneas, cómo es la línea de ejemplo y pregunta
en un solo mensaje:
1. Nombre del canal, **@usuario** y cómo se pronuncia.
2. **De qué trata** y **para quién** (público).
3. **Tono** (cercano, divertido, experto, inspirador…) y qué quiere **evitar**.
4. **Idioma y acento.**
5. Por plataforma: **objetivo**, **duración** y si comparten tema o van distintas. Sugerencia por defecto:
   TikTok ágil 40–60 s para comentarios; YouTube clase 60–90 s para búsquedas.
6. **Fondos:** **los 6 fondos que rotan** (por defecto) · **solo mis colores de marca** · **mezclar ambos**.
   Si elige marca o mezclar: su color principal y uno secundario (y, si quiere, el fondo oscuro y el claro).
7. Opcional: lema o nombre de la serie, **hashtag fijo**, frase de cierre, redes, qué dice la tarjeta final de YouTube.

Lo que no indique, propónlo tú tomando el ejemplo como base y díselo. Luego:
- Escribe `perfiles/linea-editorial.md` con la **misma estructura de secciones que el ejemplo** (incluida "Quién habla"
  con los nombres reales de sus personajes) y `plantilla: false` en el encabezado. Convierte la duración en palabras
  (~2.5 por segundo), escenas (una idea por escena, ~7–9 s cada una) y líneas.
- Escribe `perfiles/canal.json` con `"plantilla": false` (formato igual al ejemplo; `estilo`: `agil` o `clase`;
  `fondos.usar`: `rotar`, `marca` o `mezclar`; colores `#RRGGBB`; `idioma` tipo `es-PE`, `es-MX`, `es-ES`, `en-US`).
- Corre `npm run perfiles` y corrige hasta "✔ Perfiles válidos".
- Si cambió fondos o colores: `npm run ambientes` (tabla de contraste e imágenes en `out/ambientes/`). Mira las de
  marca: si algún texto no se lee, el script ya oscurece o aclara el color; si igual no convence, propón otro tono.
  Dile que puede verlas abriendo `out/ambientes.html`.
- Resume la línea editorial en 5–6 líneas y pide su visto bueno.

**Cambiar algo** (`/nuevo-video editorial más humor en TikTok`, "que YouTube dure 75 s", "cambia mi @", "usa mis colores"):
edita solo esa parte en el archivo que corresponda (guion → `.md`; lo visual y los fondos → `canal.json`), valida con
`npm run perfiles` y confirma el cambio en una línea. Aplica a los videos que se creen después; ofrece rehacer
alguno ya hecho si el cambio lo afecta (un @usuario o colores nuevos se aplican solos al volver a exportar).

Para volver al ejemplo: `npm run perfiles -- --restaurar canal` y `npm run perfiles -- --restaurar editorial` (confirma antes).

## Voces (`/nuevo-video voz`)

- `npm run voces -- --info` → voz de cada personaje, modo y créditos. Si dice `SIN_CLAVE`, explica cómo pegar la clave en `.env`.
- Cambiar la voz de un personaje: sigue la sección "Voces" del skill `personajes` (`npm run voces`, `--usar`, `--escuchar`).
- **Modo** (`config/voces.json`):
  - `lineas` (por defecto): una línea a la vez con `eleven_multilingual_v2`. Funciona en todos los planes.
  - `dialogo`: todas las voces en una sola actuación con `eleven_v3` (más natural, admite `tono`). Antes de cambiar,
    prueba si su plan lo permite: `npm run voces -- --probar` (~90 caracteres). Cambiar: `npm run voces -- --modo dialogo`.
    Si un día falla, `voz:ia` vuelve solo al modo por líneas y lo avisa.

---

# SKILL ORIGINAL: video

# Video Editing Skill

Edits, processes, and renders videos using FFmpeg and Remotion. Optimized for two output formats:
- **Long-form YouTube** (16:9, 1920×1080)
- **YouTube Shorts** (9:16, 1080×1920)

---

## Intelligent Editing Workflow

This skill enables the AI agent to act as a smart video editor. Before executing commands:

1. **Analyze** — Examine videos (duration, resolution, audio) using ffprobe
2. **Transcribe** — Get speech content for smart cuts (if needed)
3. **Ask** — Clarify user intent and preferences
4. **Plan** — Propose edit approach for approval
5. **QA Test** — Run automated tests before user preview (see references/qa-testing.md)
6. **Preview** — Only show to user after QA passes
7. **Iterate** — Refine based on feedback

See references/intelligent-editing.md for detailed workflow, questions to ask, and quality checks.

---

## Tool Selection

| Task | FFmpeg | Remotion |
|------|--------|----------|
| **Stitching** | Same codec, no effects | Transitions, overlays, programmatic |
| **Transitions** | Simple crossfades | Multiple types, custom timing |
| **Captions** | SRT burn-in | Word-by-word highlighting |
| **Teasers/Shorts** | Quick clips | Text overlays, branded elements |
| **Title Cards** | N/A | Animated section headers |
| **Graphics** | Static overlays | Animated thumbnails, end screens |

**General guidance:**
- **FFmpeg**: Fast CLI operations, batch processing, format conversion, simple edits
- **Remotion**: Styled content, animations, preview before render, React components

---

## Output Formats

### Long-Form YouTube
- Resolution: 1920×1080 (16:9)
- Frame rate: 30fps
- Typical length: 10-25 minutes
- Captions: Optional but recommended for accessibility
- Title cards: Between major sections
- End screen: CTA + subscribe prompt

### YouTube Shorts
- Resolution: 1080×1920 (9:16)
- Frame rate: 30fps
- Max length: 60 seconds
- Captions: Required (most Shorts watched without sound initially)
- Fast pacing: 1.3x playback speed for energy
- Hook in first 2 seconds

### Teaser Clips (from long-form → Shorts)
- Pull 3-4 highlight clips from the full video
- Total 25-30 seconds
- Each clip must start with an explicit subject (never pronouns)
- Never start clips with: "But", "And", "So", "Now", "It", "This", "That", "They"
- First clip must NOT overlap with the video intro
- Add word-by-word captions
- Smooth fade transitions (0.4s) between clips

---

## Reference Files

Read these files as needed:

### Intelligence Layer
| File | Contents |
|------|----------|
| references/intelligent-editing.md | Smart editing workflow, questions, quality checks |
| references/video-analysis.md | Analyze videos with ffprobe |
| references/transcription.md | Whisper transcription for speech analysis |
| references/qa-testing.md | **QA tests before user preview** |

### Core Editing
| File | Contents |
|------|----------|
| references/stitching.md | Combine multiple clips into one video |
| references/transitions.md | Add fade, slide, wipe between clips |
| references/captions.md | Add subtitles and word-by-word captions |
| references/teasers.md | Create 30-60 second teasers and Shorts |
| references/title-cards.md | Add chapter headers, bumpers, section titles |
| references/graphics-generation.md | Generate thumbnails, overlays, social graphics |

### Technical Reference
| File | Contents |
|------|----------|
| references/ffmpeg-basics.md | Common FFmpeg patterns and troubleshooting |
| references/remotion-setup.md | Remotion project setup and workflow |
| references/remotion-tips.md | Animations, timing, springs, sequences, and captions |
| references/reference-implementation.md | **Complete working code to recreate the project** |

---

## Quick Start

### Transcription (Fast — GPU Accelerated on Mac M2)
```bash
# 1. Setup whisper.cpp (one-time)
npx ts-node scripts/setup-whisper.ts

# 2. Transcribe all videos (Metal GPU accelerated)
npx ts-node scripts/transcribe-fast.ts
```

### FFmpeg (CLI)
```bash
# Check installation
which ffmpeg || brew install ffmpeg

# Basic concat (same codec)
ffmpeg -f concat -safe 0 -i list.txt -c copy output.mp4

# Burn in subtitles
ffmpeg -i video.mp4 -vf "subtitles=captions.srt" output.mp4

# Extract audio for transcription
ffmpeg -i video.mp4 -vn -acodec pcm_s16le -ar 16000 -ac 1 audio.wav
```

### Remotion (React)
```bash
# Start preview (always do this first)
npm run dev

# Render long-form (only when user requests)
npx remotion render FinalVideo out/video.mp4

# Render Short/teaser
npx remotion render TeaserOnly out/teaser.mp4
```

---

## Typical Workflows

### Workflow 1: Full YouTube Video Edit
1. User drops raw clips into project folder
2. Agent analyzes each clip (ffprobe → duration, resolution, codec)
3. Agent transcribes all clips (whisper.cpp)
4. Agent proposes edit plan: clip order, transitions, title card placement
5. User approves plan
6. Agent stitches clips with transitions
7. Agent adds title cards between sections
8. Agent generates captions
9. Agent runs QA tests
10. User previews in Remotion Studio
11. Agent renders final 1080p video

### Workflow 2: Create YouTube Shorts from Long-Form
1. Agent reads transcript of the full video
2. Agent identifies 3-4 strongest standalone moments (clear subject, impactful statement)
3. Agent proposes teaser clip selections with timestamps
4. User approves selections
5. Agent extracts clips, applies 1.3x speed, adds captions
6. Agent renders in 9:16 vertical format
7. User reviews

### Workflow 3: Add Captions to Existing Video
1. Agent transcribes video (whisper.cpp with word-level timestamps)
2. Agent generates caption file (.captions.json)
3. Two options:
   - Simple: Burn SRT subtitles via FFmpeg (fast, basic styling)
   - Styled: Word-by-word highlighting via Remotion (professional, animated)
4. Agent renders captioned video

---

## Key Lessons Learned

### Transcription (IMPORTANT)
- **Use `@remotion/install-whisper-cpp`** — NOT Python whisper (10x faster with GPU)
- Use `base.en` model for speed, `medium.en` for final quality
- **Required parameter**: `whisperCppVersion: '1.5.5'` in transcribe() call
- Enable `tokenLevelTimestamps: true` for word-level captions
- Whisper.cpp uses Metal GPU on Mac M2 — fast local transcription

### Captions
- **Active word**: Yellow `#FFD700`
- **Spoken words**: White `#FFFFFF`
- **Upcoming words**: Light gray `#E0E0E0`
- **Font**: Roboto at 48px for 1080p
- **Black stroke outline** (works on ANY background):
  ```css
  WebkitTextStroke: '3px black',
  paintOrder: 'stroke fill',
  textShadow: '0px 0px 4px rgba(0,0,0,1), 0px 0px 8px rgba(0,0,0,0.8)'
  ```

### Teasers / Shorts
- Never start clips with pronouns or conjunctions
- Each clip must have an explicit subject
- 3-4 clips totaling 25-30 seconds for teasers
- Playback speed: 1.3x for energy (1.5x is too fast)
- Transitions: Smooth fade (0.4s) between clips
- First clip must NOT overlap with intro start

### Remotion Video Trimming
- Use `OffthreadVideo` component
- Set BOTH `startFrom` AND `endAt` props
- Convert ms to frames: `Math.round((ms / 1000) * fps)`

---

## Edge Cases & Gotchas

### File Naming
- Avoid spaces in video filenames: use `intro.mp4` not `Intro Video.mp4`
- Remotion folder names can only contain letters, numbers, and hyphens

### Ports
- Remotion Studio may start on port 3001 if 3000 is in use

### Codec Mismatch
- FFmpeg concat demuxer (`-c copy`) fails silently with mismatched codecs
- Use `ffprobe` to check codecs before concatenating
- Use filter_complex for different codecs/resolutions

### Workflow
- **Always preview in Remotion Studio before rendering**
- Never render automatically — wait for user approval
- Copy videos to `public/` folder for Remotion projects

---

## Mac Mini Setup (One-Time)

```bash
# Install Homebrew (if not installed)
/bin/bash -c "$(curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Homebrew/install/HEAD/install.sh)"

# Install FFmpeg
brew install ffmpeg

# Install Node.js (for Remotion)
brew install node

# Verify installations
ffmpeg -version
node --version
npm --version

# Create video editing project
mkdir -p ~/video-editor && cd ~/video-editor
npm init -y

# Install Remotion dependencies
npm install remotion @remotion/cli @remotion/bundler @remotion/captions @remotion/transitions @remotion/install-whisper-cpp react react-dom
npm install -D typescript @types/react

# Setup whisper.cpp for transcription
npx ts-node scripts/setup-whisper.ts
```

---

# SKILL ORIGINAL: video-edit

# Video edit

This skill turns raw footage into a finished edit together with a human editor. Read this whole file before touching a project. Then read `reference/audio.md` before any cut or level change, and `reference/style.md` before any graphics.

## Read first: what you can and cannot do

- **You cannot hear.** You never listen to audio. Everything you know about sound comes from numbers: Whisper word timestamps, a 10 ms energy envelope, loudness meters, spectrograms. Say this to the user plainly and never claim that something "sounds good".
- **The human ear is the final check.** Whether a cut clips a word, whether an effect is too loud, whether a voice sounds muffled: the user decides. Your job is to make every problem measurable and every fix fast.
- **The first pass is never the final edit.** Work stage by stage, hand over a preview after each stage, and expect corrections. Most good results come from short review loops, not from one long autonomous run.
- **Sync by construction, not by ear.** Animations and sound effects are triggered from the same timeline clock, and that clock is derived from the measured word times. That is how effects land on the picture without you hearing them.
- **Be creative, on purpose.** Every visual should help the viewer understand, and nothing should look like a template. See "Creative direction" below.

## Paths

Commands run from the user's project folder. `$SKILL` is the folder this file is in (for example `~/.claude/skills/video-edit`); set it once in the shell: `SKILL=~/.claude/skills/video-edit`.

## First run: get to know the creator

Look for the creator profile at `~/.claude/video-edit/profile.md`. If it does not exist, this is the first run: introduce yourself in one short message and ask these together. The user may skip any of them.

1. **Name on screen:** the name and a one-line role for the lower third and the end screen.
2. **Channel:** channel name, platforms (YouTube, Instagram, TikTok…), the language they speak in the videos, and whether they want a second language on screen.
3. **Colours:** the colours they like (one accent and one or two support colours) and colours they never want. A thumbnail or a brand page works as an answer too.
4. **Logo:** a logo file (SVG, or PNG with transparency), and where it may appear: lower third, end screen, or nowhere.
5. **Font:** a brand font, or the default.
6. **Usual videos:** educational, talk, tutorial, Shorts, and how calm or energetic they like the motion.
7. **Sound:** sound effects never, only when a sound is the thing being shown, or also quietly on transitions. Background music yes or no.
8. **Never:** things they dislike (glows, italics, emoji, fast zooms, a colour…).

Save the answers to `~/.claude/video-edit/profile.md` (format in `reference/profile.md`) and copy the logo into `~/.claude/video-edit/brand/`. Show the saved profile in a few lines and ask whether it is right.

From then on:

- **Read the profile at the start of every project** and apply it without asking again: the accent and support colours go into the scene's CSS variables, the name and logo into the lower third and the end screen, and the sound and motion defaults into the plan.
- **Remember lasting rules.** When the user gives a rule for all videos ("no sound on transitions", "always deliver 4K and 480p", "my accent is now green"), add it to the profile's "Lasting rules" with the date right away, and say that you did. A rule for one video goes to that project's `work/NOTES.md` instead. When unsure which, ask.
- **The profile is personal.** It lives in the user's home folder. Never copy it into a project that is shared or into a repository.

## Every project: ask

Ask these once per project and keep the answers in `work/NOTES.md`. Skip what the profile already answers.

1. **Video type:** educational / explainer, talk (talking head), tutorial with screen recording, or Short (vertical, under 60 s). This sets the motion level (see the table below).
2. **Raw material:** which clips, in which order, is there a script or outline to follow?
3. **On-screen language(s):** from the profile, unless this video is different.
4. **Sound effects:** from the profile. Without a profile, the default is no effects on transitions, effects only where a sound is the subject.
5. **Deliverables:** resolution (1080p / 1440p / 4K), captions and their languages, YouTube chapter list, deadline.

## Video types

| Type | Motion | Sound effects | Cuts |
|---|---|---|---|
| Educational / explainer | Calm and serious. Clean diagrams, readable panels, slow camera moves. three.js only when it explains something (a 3D model of the thing being taught). | None, or very quiet transitions | Natural pace |
| Talk (talking head) | Few cards that stay on screen long, placed in empty areas, never over the face. | Quiet chapter transitions only | Natural or tight |
| Tutorial / screen recording | Zoom into the part of the screen being discussed, highlight boxes, step cards. | Optional soft clicks on highlights | Natural |
| Short | More energetic: faster reveals, bigger type, a hook in the first 2 s, captions on. Still eased, never jittery. | Allowed, still under the voice | Tight |

If the user does not say, infer the type from the transcript and confirm it. Do not over-animate an educational video, and do not make a Short feel slow.

## Creative direction

Be creative, on purpose. A good edit explains the topic better than the speaker alone could, and it never looks like the same card pasted again and again.

- **Explain, don't decorate.** For every beat ask: what would make this click for someone who has never heard of it? Draw the abstract idea (for "the model needs a lot of data", show many different faces being scanned and labelled). When a tool, language or term is named that many viewers may not know, show a small box: its name, what kind of thing it is (language, tool, model, concept) and one plain sentence.
- **Use real material.** The video's own measured data (the waveform of the sentence being spoken, the real loudness numbers), real screenshots and screen recordings the user has, the real before and after. Real proof beats an invented example every time. Ask the user for screenshots when a beat needs one.
- **Show mistakes honestly.** When the video talks about something that went wrong, show the actual screenshot, the note that was given and the fixed version side by side.
- **Vary the form.** Alternate between a keyword card, a list that grows as each item is said, a counter, a full-screen board for a dense idea, a zoom into the screen, a framed insert of another video. Pick the form that fits the content, not the one used last.
- **One idea at a time, calm motion.** Creative does not mean busy. Keep the motion level of the video type and the profile.
- **Pitch the big pieces first.** Before building a full-screen board, a montage or a 3D scene, describe it in two lines and let the user say yes.

## The workflow

Work in this order. After each numbered stage, stop and hand over a preview URL or file plus a short list of what you decided and why.

### 0. Project layout

```
project/
  src/            raw clips (or symlinks to them)
  work/           everything generated: wav, transcripts, program.json, proxy.mp4, sfx/, NOTES.md, backups
  scene/          the motion-graphics page (copied from this skill's template/)
  out/            renders and final files
```

Keep the project outside cloud-synced folders (iCloud Desktop, Dropbox) if possible. Synced files can be evicted from disk mid-render and reads then hang.

### 1. Transcribe

```bash
python3 $SKILL/scripts/transcribe.py --project . src/*.mov --lang en
```

This splits each clip at pauses, transcribes each short chunk separately (padded with silence), and writes `work/transcripts/<clip>.json` + `.txt` with numbered utterances and word times. Transcribing whole files hides retakes: Whisper merges repeated sentences and drops repeated words. See `reference/audio.md`.

Then **read the .txt files yourself**. Mark retakes, false starts, half sentences and filler. Speakers often repeat a sentence two or three times; keep the last fluent take unless the user says otherwise.

### 2. Rough cut

Write `work/edl.json` (format in `reference/edl.md`): the utterances to keep, in the script's order, with chapter markers. Then:

```bash
python3 $SKILL/scripts/cuts.py --project .          # edl.json -> work/program.json (cut points snapped to real word edges)
python3 $SKILL/scripts/build.py --project . audio video   # -> work/prog.wav + work/proxy.mp4 (1080p preview)
python3 $SKILL/scripts/verify.py --project .        # re-transcribes every join, flags clipped words and leftover repeats
```

Fix what `verify.py` flags, rebuild, and only then hand over. Tell the user the new length and how many cuts were made.

### 3. Review loop

- The user gives timestamps. **Map them by content, not by clock.** Their tab may show an older build. Quote the sentence you think they mean and confirm when unsure. If a time does not match, search the transcripts for the quoted words.
- Before every new version, copy `work/edl.json` and `work/program.json` to `work/backup/vN/`. If the user says "undo everything", restore exactly, do not reinterpret.
- Never replace `work/proxy.mp4` while the user is watching it; write the new one and say when it is ready.
- Change one thing at a time when the user is unhappy. A broken preview makes people distrust the whole revision.

### 4. Chapters

Split the video into chapters by meaning, not by clock:

- One chapter per real change of topic, in the order the viewer needs to understand it. Follow the script's sections when there is a script.
- For a 10–15 minute video that is usually 5–10 main chapters, each at least about 45 s. A short sub-topic stays inside its chapter as a chip change, not a chapter of its own.
- Title each chapter with what the viewer gets from it ("Where the AI went wrong"), not with a vague label ("Part 3"). Short enough to read in one glance.
- YouTube rules: the first chapter starts at 0:00, at least three chapters in ascending order, each at least 10 s long, timestamps written with colons (`0:51`, not `0.51`), in the description (not in a comment). Chapters that break a rule fail silently.

Propose the list with one line on why each chapter starts where it does. After approval, add `{"chapter": "..."}` items to the EDL. `cuts.py` prints a YouTube chapter list (`0:00 Intro …`) and writes `work/chapters.txt`. The on-screen veils and the YouTube list use the same times.

A chapter transition on screen is a full-frame veil (see `reference/style.md`, section 9): the chapter number, the title, and a progress row. Put 0.3–0.6 s of the clip's own room tone before each chapter so the veil never wipes across the last word.

### 5. Graphics, range by range

Copy the template into the project (`cp -R $SKILL/template scene`), serve the project and open the scene. `?demo` shows the template's own demo; without it the scene loads `work/program.json` and plays `work/proxy.mp4`:

```bash
python3 $SKILL/scripts/serve.py --project . --port 5600     # http://127.0.0.1:5600/scene/
```

Work on one chapter or time range at a time, never the whole video at once. For each range:

1. List the beats: the few things worth showing (numbers, steps, a comparison, a key idea, a source). **Do not put the narration on screen.** Cards are not subtitles; they carry what the voice cannot (structure, data, a picture). Use the creative direction above.
2. Lock every beat to a spoken word with `at('phrase')`. An element rises when its word is said (about 0.1 s before) and leaves only after its sentence ends. `at()` returns the first match: for a phrase said more than once, pass `{ from: sectionStart }`. A number in speech may be a timestamp ("at zero eighteen" is 0:18 of a video, not 0.18 seconds); check the context before drawing it.
3. Place the transitions (`reference/style.md`, section 9): a veil at each chapter, a blur dip where the picture jumps, a dark blur dip where a cut removes a whole stretch. Nothing on plain cuts.
4. Check `?t=SECONDS` freezes at the beats, then hand over the URL. Always check `?t=0` too: the first frame is what YouTube and most players show before play, so nothing that belongs later may be visible there.

After a re-cut, re-check every time that is tied to a source moment rather than to a word: a source time that was cut away no longer exists in the program.

Rules that apply to every frame are in `reference/style.md`. Run the layout audit before each handover:

```bash
node $SKILL/scripts/audit.mjs http://127.0.0.1:5600/scene/ --every 0.5
```

### 6. Sound effects (only if wanted)

Follow the profile. Without one, put no sound on transitions; use effects where a sound is the subject (showing what a whoosh at a given level sounds like, a click on the button being explained). Generate the library, then cue effects from the scene's timeline with `cue(t, 'whoosh')`:

```bash
python3 $SKILL/scripts/sfx.py lib --out scene/sfx            # --gain -3 makes every effect 3 dB quieter
```

Levels and rules are in `reference/audio.md`. The user's first reaction to the level decides: if they say "can't hear it" or "too much", change the level in small steps and remember the answer for the rest of the video.

### 7. Final render and mix

```bash
python3 $SKILL/scripts/build.py --project . video --final           # source-resolution program video (the render background)
node $SKILL/scripts/render.mjs "http://127.0.0.1:5600/scene/?src=../work/prog-final.mp4" --out out/frames --workers 4 --dpr 2
python3 $SKILL/scripts/mix.py --project . --video out/frames --out out/FINAL.mp4
python3 $SKILL/scripts/loudness.py out/FINAL.mp4
```

Only start a long final render after the user approves the preview. Ask before deleting anything to free disk space.

Deliver the file plus a short report: length, resolution, loudness (integrated LUFS, true peak), the YouTube chapter list, and what was not checked.

## Scripts

| Script | What it does |
|---|---|
| `transcribe.py` | Chunked transcription with word times (faster-whisper or whisperkit-cli) |
| `cuts.py` | EDL to program: cut points snapped to measured word edges, pause removal, chapters, words in program time |
| `build.py` | Sample-exact program audio (gain only, no filters by default) and frame-exact video |
| `verify.py` | Re-transcribes the program around every join to catch clipped words and repeats |
| `loudness.py` | Integrated LUFS, true peak, loudness range, with a plain verdict |
| `sfx.py` | Sound effects synthesized from math: whoosh, tick, click, key, typing, pop, chime |
| `serve.py` | Static server with HTTP range support (needed for video seeking) |
| `render.mjs` | Frame-exact render of the scene in headless Chrome, piped into ffmpeg, parallel workers |
| `audit.mjs` | Finds overlapping cards, text under the face, and elements touching the frame edge |
| `mix.py` | Joins the rendered parts with the voice and the effects track, final encode |

Requirements: Python 3.10+, numpy, ffmpeg + ffprobe, Node 20+, Google Chrome or Chromium, and one Whisper backend (`pip install faster-whisper`, or `whisperkit-cli` on Apple Silicon).

## Working notes

Keep `work/NOTES.md` up to date: the answers from the intake, every rule the user gives for this video ("effects one step quieter here"), and the current version. Rules meant for every video go into the profile (see "First run"); when you are not sure whether a rule is for this video or for all of them, ask.

---

# SKILL ORIGINAL: videoediting

# Video editing with ffmpeg

You are an editor who works with a transcript and filtergraphs rather than a mouse and a timeline. The pipeline is deterministic: everything is edited as text and rebuilt with one command.

```
footage → transcript with word-level timings → EDL (edit list) → ffmpeg → finished video
                    ↑                               ↑
             faster-whisper                 edited by hand — this IS the edit
```

Core principle: **the video is never analysed pixel by pixel — the edit is driven by timed text.** Speech defines the structure; the picture serves it.

## One reel or many?

Decide this first — it changes everything downstream.

- **One or two reels** → follow the step order below, conversationally.
- **Three or more** → switch to the batch pipeline: [references/batch-pipeline.md](references/batch-pipeline.md). Each stage runs once over the whole project and caches by input hash. Doing ten reels conversationally costs roughly ten times the context and buys nothing for it.

## Order of work

Always follow the steps, never skip ahead. Each step produces the input for the next.

1. **Check the environment.** If ffmpeg / faster-whisper / Chrome are missing → [references/setup.md](references/setup.md). On a clean machine this takes ten minutes.
2. **Get the source.** If the user handed you a URL instead of a file, fetch it first with `download.py`.
3. **Inspect the source** — ffprobe, scene detection, a contact sheet. **Look at the frames with your own eyes via Read.** Never edit blind from text alone.
4. **Transcribe** with word-level timings and **proofread the result**. Speech recognition gets words wrong even on clean audio. No local model? `transcribe_cloud.py` gets the same output from Groq/OpenAI.
5. **Agree on the concept** with the user if they have not specified one: format, what goes into graphics, target platform. One question, not a questionnaire.
6. **Build the EDL** — cut pauses, never cut ideas.
7. **Build `base`**, then verify the cuts frame by frame.
8. **Generate subtitles and overlays**, build `final`.
9. **Run the acceptance checklist** and deliver the file with SendUserFile.

Full commands for each step — [references/pipeline.md](references/pipeline.md).

## What this pipeline can and cannot do

Can: semantic cutting, pause removal, punch-in on cuts, colour grading (including `.cube` LUTs), word-level subtitles, arbitrarily complex infographics via HTML/CSS, stabilisation, loudness normalisation for social platforms, batch processing. With the Remotion plugin installed, also animated graphics rendered from React — see [references/remotion.md](references/remotion.md).

**Cannot at all** — say so up front if the user asks for it: object tracking (pinning a caption to a moving hand) and masked grading. Those still need Premiere / DaVinci / After Effects.

**Cannot alone, but can with FableCut installed:** frame-accurate creative cutting, keyframed motion, transitions, speed ramps, chroma key, cutting to a music beat. FableCut is a browser timeline an agent drives through MCP — see [references/fablecut.md](references/fablecut.md). It has no speech recognition and its export needs a human click, so speech-driven structure and unattended batches stay in this pipeline.

## Ready-made tools

`assets/tools/` holds twelve working scripts — **copy them into the project, do not rewrite them from scratch**:

| Script | What it does |
|---|---|
| `download.py` | fetch source footage from a URL (yt-dlp) — skip if you already have a local file |
| `transcribe.py` | faster-whisper → `transcript.json` with word-level timings + `.srt` |
| `transcribe_cloud.py` | same output, via Groq/OpenAI Whisper API — no model download, needs an API key |
| `pauses.py` | lists pauses in speech — the basis for the EDL |
| `dump_words.py` | every word with its index and confidence — for proofreading |
| `make_ass.py` | word-level ASS subtitles, re-timed to the post-cut timeline |
| `render_overlays.py` | HTML/CSS → headless Chrome → 1080×1920 PNG with alpha |
| `build.py` | assembly: `base` (cut + grade), `final` (overlays + subtitles + audio) |
| `build_vo.py` | same, but for a **separate voiceover track** — video from several sources fitted to the audio |
| `batch_vo.py` | transcribe every voiceover in one model load |
| `mute_subs.py` | drop subtitles where a full-screen card is on screen |
| `pipeline.py` | **batch driver** — every stage over every reel, with caching |

`assets/templates/` holds starter `edl.json`, `overlays.json` and `corrections.json`.

Lay the project out like this:

```
<folder with the footage>/
├── IMG_XXXX.MOV
└── edit/
    ├── tools/        ← the copied scripts
    ├── work/         ← intermediates, never shipped
    ├── overlays/     ← rendered PNGs
    ├── out/          ← finished videos
    ├── edl.json  overlays.json  corrections.json  subs.ass
```

## Reference files

Load these on demand, not all at once:

- [references/setup.md](references/setup.md) — installing on a clean machine (Windows/macOS), verifying the ffmpeg build
- [references/pipeline.md](references/pipeline.md) — the workflow step by step, cutting rules, commands
- [references/storyboard.md](references/storyboard.md) — video formats, panel types, how to storyboard a talking head
- [references/design-system.md](references/design-system.md) — palette, type scale, safe zones, overlay techniques
- [references/batch-pipeline.md](references/batch-pipeline.md) — running many reels as stages, and where the context actually goes
- [references/remotion.md](references/remotion.md) — animated graphics tier: when React-rendered overlays earn their cost
- [references/fablecut.md](references/fablecut.md) — timeline tier: when to hand the edit to FableCut, and how the two pipelines meet
- [references/ffmpeg-cookbook.md](references/ffmpeg-cookbook.md) — grades, effects, transitions, audio, encoding
- [references/troubleshooting.md](references/troubleshooting.md) — **read this before your first ffmpeg run**; seventeen failures, each of which cost an hour

## Rules that make or break the video

Learned on real builds, not from documentation.

1. **Look at the frames.** A transcript will not tell you the speaker is standing up or fixing their hair at that exact moment. Those are precisely the places where cuts fall apart.
2. **A card goes above the face, never on it.** The first attempt always drops the panel into the middle of the frame, covering the eyes. The speaker's gaze is half the trust the video earns.
3. **Cut at the end of a phrase, not on a stopwatch.** Cutting "every N seconds" slices ideas in half.
4. **Leave 0.2–0.3 s of every pause.** A pause collapsed to zero sounds like a stutter.
5. **Never touch dramatic pauses** — after a question, before a conclusion. There the silence is doing the work.
6. **Alternate `zoom` between segments.** Then a cut reads as a change of angle rather than a mistake.
7. **Leave one stretch with no graphics at all.** The viewer needs air; a solid wall of cards is exhausting.
8. **Proofread the subtitles.** A recognition error burned into the picture is permanent.
9. **Verify by looking, not by faith.** Pull still frames, stack them with `hstack`, open the result via Read.
10. **Never ship without the acceptance checklist** in pipeline.md.
11. **Never let a tool write back into a config file.** A script that appends its own output to the EDL doubles that file's size, and every later edit echoes the whole thing into context. Generated data belongs in `work/`.
12. **Read the first and last line of the transcript yourself.** Whisper hallucinates fabricated text over silence — classically a fake subtitler credit line on Russian audio — and it can carry a perfectly normal-looking confidence score. Both transcription scripts filter the obvious cases automatically, but the filter is not airtight. See [troubleshooting.md #17](references/troubleshooting.md).

## Orchestration

The task is multi-step — create tasks with TaskCreate for the stages (transcript, EDL, grade, graphics, assembly) and keep their status current. Run long operations (model download, `base` render) in the background and use the time for work that does not depend on them: while the model downloads, design the overlays and test grades on still frames.

Deliver the finished video with SendUserFile and a short note on what changed.

---

# SKILL ORIGINAL: edit-style

# Edit style: dynamic vertical reels from a raw take

You turn one raw talking-head clip into a finished 1080x1920 reel that looks
hand-edited: the frame never sits still, graphics are real UI or official
logos (never stock, never invented), and captions land on the beat. Nothing is
dragged on a timeline. Every decision is a written shot plan rendered by code,
then checked by gates that measure the final file.

Part of a pipeline: `reel-ideation` (ideas, scripts, record sheet) ->
`footage-ingest` ("the card is in": copy, sort, pick takes, conform) ->
**edit-style** (short-form edit) or `longform-edit` (YouTube long-form) ->
`reel-captions` (post text) -> `trial-post` (trial reels) -> `reel-scorecard` (72 h read).

## Requirements

Claude Code on a computer (not Claude.ai: it needs a shell and ffmpeg).

| need | for | install |
|---|---|---|
| ffmpeg + ffprobe (with `ebur128`) | every cut, composite, gate | `brew install ffmpeg` / `apt install ffmpeg` |
| Python 3.9+ and Pillow | captions, cue alignment | `pip install pillow` |
| a local Whisper | word timestamps | see the `use-local-whisper` skill |
| OpenCV (optional) | framing gate, crop solver, face-first gate | `pip install opencv-python-headless` + YuNet model (see `tools/face_box.py`) |
| Node + Playwright (optional) | headless screen capture | `npm i playwright && npx playwright install chromium` |
| Remotion (optional) | animated graphic panels | `npx create-video@latest`; a full panel studio is in github.com/johannships/reels-engine |
| swiftc, macOS only (optional) | iPhone HDR to SDR | `xcode-select --install` |

Install check: `sh tools/check_setup.sh` (exit 0 = ready).

## Inputs

- `raw.mov` (or mp4): one take, phone or camera, any orientation.
- `script.txt`: what was said, spelled the way it must appear on screen. If
  there is no script yet, write one first with `references/script-writing.md`.
- Optional: a topic URL (repo, product page) to capture, and sources for every number.
- From `footage-ingest`: a SHIP line (hook clip > body clips > IG ending / bio ending). Clips recorded one line
  per clip are joined in step 2; each join is a place dead air hides.

## Pipeline (one folder per reel: every file for this reel lives inside it)

`transcribe.py` below is `use-local-whisper/scripts/transcribe.py`. Parallel
edits never share a work folder: one reel, one folder.

0. **Conform camera footage first** (`footage-ingest/scripts/conform.py`): constant 30 fps, TV range, and the
   mirror if the creator wants selfie view. The mirror goes on the raw camera clip only, BEFORE captions, logos,
   cards or screen captures are composited; those are never mirrored. Check the mirrored frame for backwards text.
1. **Check the source.** `ffprobe -v error -select_streams v:0 -show_entries stream=color_range,color_transfer,r_frame_rate -of csv=p=0 raw.mov`
   `arib-std-b67` = iPhone HDR, tone map first; `pc,bt709` = full-range camera
   file. Exact handling: `references/source-footage.md`.
2. **Cut retakes, then pauses.** Transcribe raw (`transcribe.py raw.mov`),
   keep the LAST take of any repeated line, never cut mid-word. Then
   `python3 tools/tighten.py cut.mp4 tight.mp4` (-50 dB, never -38 dB).
   Joining one-line clips: set each in and out point within 0.08 s of the first and last word (refine the edge
   on the audio RMS, not on Whisper), look at the first and last 0.5 s of every clip (no reaching for the
   camera, no settling in), and write the join times of the final timeline to `joins.json`.
3. **Transcribe the tightened take**: `transcribe.py tight.mp4` gives `tight.words.json`.
4. **Write the shot plan** (`shots.json`): one row per shot,
   `{layout, anchor_phrase, content}`. `anchor_phrase` is script text matched
   against `words.json` so the shot starts when those words are said. Never
   hand-type timestamps. Layout rules: `references/edit-craft.md`.
5. **Capture and fetch assets.** Screens: `node tools/capture_clock.js <url> shots/s3 6`
   (headless, frame-exact). Logos: the brand's official press page or site
   SVG only. Open every asset and look at it before use.
6. **Frame the face per take**: `python3 tools/face_box.py tight.mp4 --model yunet.onnx --solve full`
   (and `--solve pip`). Never reuse crops from another take.
7. **Render panels** (Remotion or stills) to each shot's exact duration,
   composite per shot with ffmpeg, concat, lay the continuous voice on top.
   Run renders under `nice -n 15`. Look-dev on stills, then one full render.
8. **Mix and master** (`references/edit-craft.md` section 9): high-pass 75 Hz,
   light denoise, synthesised SFX 12 to 20 dB under the voice, -14 LUFS, true
   peak at or under -1.5 dBTP.
9. **Captions, timed on the FINAL audio**: `transcribe.py final_nocaps.mp4`, then
   `python3 tools/make_cues.py script.txt final_nocaps.words.json --fix fix.json`, then
   `python3 tools/burn_captions.py final_nocaps.mp4 cues.json final.mp4 --caps`.
   Text comes from the script, timing from Whisper. `--y 0.38` on PIP shots.
10. **Gates** (below). Fix and re-run until all pass.

## Layouts

| layout | what | when |
|---|---|---|
| FULL | face fills the frame (head-top 13%, chin 85%) | hook, personal beats, close |
| GFX | full-screen real UI, stat or claim card, no face | each key beat, the CTA |
| PIP | graphic on top, face in a rounded card at the bottom (y 1086, 1080x834, radius 48) | naming a tool while still talking |

Switch every 2 to 5 s. **Open on the face from frame 1** (FULL or PIP): never
a graphic, title card, screen recording or logo-only opener; openers without a
face test badly. Break to GFX on each key beat, land the CTA on GFX, close on
FULL. If the topic is a named brand, show its official logo in the first 2 s
as a clean panel beside or over the face, never instead of it.

## Two endings per keyword reel

Comment-keyword DM automations usually work on Instagram only, so a "comment
KEYWORD" ending wastes TikTok, YouTube and Facebook traffic. Export two files:

- `final.mp4`: Instagram, ends on the recorded "comment KEYWORD" clip.
- `final-bio.mp4`: everywhere else, ends on a recorded "check the link in my bio" clip.

Make the swap invisible: hide the join under a full-screen GFX card, use one
continuous bio clip (no micro-cuts), match scale, colour and loudness, 60 to
100 ms audio crossfade, and frame-check the join. Both files go through every gate.

## QA gates (all must pass; write results to QA.md)

| # | gate | tool | pass |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | framing | `face_box.py final.mp4` | head-top > 3%, chin < 94% on every sample |
| 2 | caption sync per chapter, on the final render | re-transcribe final, `caption_sync_gate.py captions.json final.words.json chapters.json` | median <= 0.12 s, p95 <= 0.25 s |
| 3 | A/V length | `final_gates.sh final.mp4` | within one frame |
| 4 | loudness | `final_gates.sh` | -14 +/- 1 LUFS, true peak <= -1.5 dBTP |
| 5 | colour/encode | `final_gates.sh` | yuv420p, tv range, BT.709 tags, >= 8 Mbps |
| 6 | skin luma vs source | `skin_luma_check.sh` | within 2 levels |
| 7 | first word survives | re-transcribe first 2 s | first script word present |
| 8 | contact sheet | `final_gates.sh` writes `contact.png` | you OPENED it: no pills or stickers, no caption over the mouth, logos correct, nothing private, every label inside the 4:5 crop (y 285 to 1635) |
| 9 | claims | manual | every on-screen number has a named source checked live today; false spoken lines are cut and listed |
| 10 | face first | `reel_qa.py final.mp4 --yunet yunet.onnx` | a face (box >= 15% of frame height) in at least half the frames of the first 0.3 s |
| 11 | dead air | `reel_qa.py final.mp4 --words final.words.json --joins joins.json` | no word gap > 0.25 s at a join, > 0.35 s anywhere (re-whisper of the FINAL) |
| 12 | join envelope | `reel_qa.py` (same run) | no quiet run (< -36 dBFS) > 0.25 s within 0.5 s of a join, > 0.35 s inside speech. Catches room-tone holes Whisper hides |
| 13 | dashes | `reel_qa.py --cues cues.json` | no em dash or spaced en dash in any caption |

Run gates 10 to 13 on `final.mp4` AND `final-bio.mp4`, each with its own joins file.

On a caption-sync fail: retime that chapter's cues to the final words,
re-burn, re-transcribe, re-gate. Loop until every chapter passes.

## Output

```
reel-folder/
  final.mp4        1080x1920, H.264 BT.709, AAC 48 kHz, -14 LUFS (Instagram ending)
  final-bio.mp4    same edit with the "link in my bio" ending (every other platform)
  joins.json       join times per file (joins-bio.json for final-bio.mp4)
  captions.srt     from the re-transcription of final.mp4, names spelled right
  contact.png      12-frame sheet you looked at
  QA.md            one line per gate, plus CLAIMS and any cuts of private detail
  LOG.md           written as you go
```

Example QA.md:

```
framing      12/12 PASS (head-top min 6.1%, chin max 88.4%)
face first   9/9 frames PASS   dead air max 0.21 s at joins PASS   join envelope PASS   dashes PASS
caption sync hook median 0.04 p95 0.11 PASS | body 0.05/0.14 PASS | cta 0.03/0.09 PASS
A/V          diff 0.012s PASS     loudness -14.1 LUFS, -1.8 dBTP PASS
colour       yuv420p tv bt709 13.9 Mbps PASS   skin luma diff 1.2 PASS
contact      looked: no pills, captions clear of mouth, logos official, 4:5 safe
CLAIMS       "40,000 stars": GitHub API 14:05 today = 41,212 (shown as 41K)
```

## Failure handling

- No Whisper: stop and install one (`use-local-whisper`). Never guess timings.
- No OpenCV: skip gate 1's tool but eyeball every contact-sheet frame for chin/forehead cuts, and say so in QA.md.
- No Remotion: build GFX shots as still PNGs (Pillow or a headless Playwright screenshot of an HTML card) with a slow push-in (`zoompan`).
- `make_cues.py` exits 2 (script and audio differ): fix the script to match what was said. Never burn captions that disagree with the voice.
- A gate fails three loops in a row: stop, report the numbers, ask.

## Rules that keep it from looking like AI

- No floating pills, badges, corner tags, stickers or REC chips. Ever.
- No code-generated motion-graphics sizzle. Real footage and real product screens.
- One accent colour. One caption style per shot type.
- Show, don't narrate: when the voice names a thing, that thing is on screen.
- Nothing private on screen: finances, client names, account ids, internal files.
- No sponsor text, "paid partnership" label or disclosure card burned into the video: disclosure goes in the
  post caption and the platform's paid-partnership toggle.
- No "AI clone" or "digital twin" label burned in on avatar shots: use the platform's AI-content toggle.
- No em or en dashes in scripts or captions.

Depth: `references/script-writing.md` (hooks, register, numbers),
`references/source-footage.md` (HDR, full-range cameras, clone takes, silence),
`references/edit-craft.md` (captions, screens, framing, gestures, audio, pitfalls).

---

# SKILL ORIGINAL: longform-edit

# longform-edit

Drop raws in a folder, run one command, get a cut, mastered, captioned video plus a REVIEW.md that
explains every cut. Short-form sibling: `edit-style` (reels). Transcription setup: `use-local-whisper`.
Thumbnails: `thumbnail-generator`. Per-platform captions for clips: `reel-captions`.

## Requirements (check first, install only after the user says yes)

```bash
ffmpeg -version | head -1                       # any 6.x+; libass only needed for --burn-captions
whisper-cli --help >/dev/null && echo WHISPER_OK   # whisper.cpp (brew install whisper-cpp)
ls ~/models/whisper/ggml-small.en.bin           # or set paths.whisper_model
python3 -m pip install -r scripts/requirements.txt  # numpy, pillow, opencv-python-headless
which claude                                     # optional: titles + chapter names
```

Model: `curl -L -o ~/models/whisper/ggml-small.en.bin https://huggingface.co/ggerganov/whisper.cpp/resolve/main/ggml-small.en.bin`
(466 MB; `ggml-base.en` on slow machines, multilingual `ggml-small` for non-English). Another transcriber is
fine too: pass `--words words.json` as `[{"w": "word", "s": 0.12, "e": 0.40, "p": 0.9}]`.
Claude.ai has no ffmpeg: there, take a pasted transcript with timestamps and return the cut list, retake table,
chapters and an SRT only. Say clearly that nothing was rendered.

## Setup (once)

Copy `config.example.json` to `~/longform/config.json`, set `paths.inbox`, `paths.outbox`, `paths.whisper_model`,
your names in `transcribe.prompt` and `publish.word_fixes`, and optionally `publish.cta_line` + `publish.cta_keywords`
(the draft flags a video with no mid-video call to action). Every threshold below lives in that file.

## Run it

```bash
S=~/.claude/skills/longform-edit/scripts/longform_edit.py
python3 $S ~/longform/inbox/<slug>/ --config ~/longform/config.json             # one video
python3 $S --config ~/longform/config.json                                      # every inbox folder with no REVIEW.md yet
python3 $S <folder> --plan-only      # cut plan + REVIEW.md in ~1 min, no render
python3 $S <folder> --mic voice.wav  # separate mic, synced by cross-correlation (clap at the start helps)
python3 $S <folder> --kind camera    # talking head: every silence is idle (auto-detected from motion or file name)
python3 $S <folder> --burn-captions  # also <slug>.captioned.mp4 with 2-5 word captions burned in
python3 $S <folder> --no-llm         # heuristic chapter titles, no claude CLI call
```

Inbox: `<inbox>/<slug>/` holding one video (largest wins) and optionally one audio file (used as the voice) and a
`config.json` with per-video overrides. Output: `<outbox>/<slug>/`. A 15 min raw takes roughly 6-12 min; run it in the
background and tail `<outbox>/<slug>/run.log`. Re-runs are cached: only changed stages redo.

## What it decides (details and every number in references/method.md)

1. **Retakes, keep the last take.** An explicit cue said as its own phrase ("let me say that again", "retake",
   "scratch that", 22 cues in config) cuts the abandoned attempt plus the cue. A line restarted from the top (same
   opening words, similarity >= 0.8, within 45 s) cuts the earlier attempt. Back-to-back repeated phrases lose the
   first copy. um/uh separated by silence are cut. Anything weaker is **flagged, not cut**.
2. **Dead space, strategically.** Every silence >= 0.7 s is checked against the picture (frame diff at 8 fps,
   cursor-sized blobs ignored, webcam bubble masked, net first-to-last change catches slow typing).
   Static screen: tightened to 0.28 s mid-sentence / 0.40 s at a sentence end, breath kept. Cursor only: 0.6 s.
   Real on-screen action: kept, idle trimmed either side; if it runs > 4.5 s it plays at 1.5-3x with the last 0.9 s
   at 1x so the result lands. Cuts only land in measured silence, never mid-word.
3. **Voice master, no music bed.** HPF 75 Hz, FFT denoise from the measured floor, mud cut, presence, air, de-ess,
   2.5:1 compression, gain to -14 LUFS, limiter to <= -1.5 dBTP, filter latency measured and removed.
4. **Captions from the FINAL audio.** The final render is re-transcribed; the SRT is built from that, never from the
   source transcript, with cue starts snapped to speech onsets. Every word is covered (gated).
5. **Publish draft.** Chapters (structural cues like "the first", "next", long pauses, big screen changes; >= 3,
   first at 0:00, each >= 40 s), 3 title options, description, pinned comment, tags and fact-check flags via the
   `claude` CLI when present, heuristic otherwise. 3 thumbnail candidate frames (sharpest, most colourful per third).

## Gates (exit 2 and `<slug>.FAILED-GATES.mp4` if any fails)

| gate | passes when |
|---|---|
| frames | every chunk has exactly the EDL frame count; video and audio length within 1.5 frames |
| words | final re-transcription recalls >= 93% of the kept source words, no lost run >= 6 words |
| av_sync | 10 probes: audio lag <= 25 ms and the frame matches the source better than +-0.5 s |
| captions | per chapter, cue start vs audio onset: median <= 0.12 s, p95 <= 0.25 s |
| caption_coverage | >= 98% of final words sit inside a caption |
| loudness | -14 +-1 LUFS integrated, true peak <= -1 dBTP |

## After it runs (the agent's job, do not skip)

1. **Exit code.** 0 = all gates passed. 2 = never hand the file over as done; REVIEW.md says which gate and why.
2. **Look at `contact-sheet.jpg` and `cuts-sheet.jpg`** (open the images): picture present in every tile, no
   black frames, the before/after of each edit point looks like a continuous state.
3. **Read REVIEW.md**: retakes cut (before/after text), flagged-not-cut list, on-screen actions kept, loudness,
   caption sync per chapter, A/V probes, words check. To change a decision write `<outbox>/<slug>/overrides.json`
   and re-run the same command (times are SOURCE seconds, the `src` column):
   ```json
   {"keep_retakes": ["R03"], "cut_retakes": ["R07"], "keep_ranges": [[312.0, 330.5]],
    "force_cut": [{"s": 100.2, "e": 104.9, "why": "cough"}]}
   ```
4. **PUBLISH.md is a draft.** Rewrite weak titles, fact-check every flagged number (measured, same source, same
   window), fix name spellings in `publish.word_fixes` and re-run so the SRT is right.
5. **Never upload or schedule.** Hand back the paths; the human publishes.

## Report back in this shape

```
LONGFORM EDIT: <slug>
Runtime   14:32 -> 11:05 (24% cut): 6 retakes, 11 fillers, 3 speed-ups, 41 pauses tightened
Gates     6/6 PASS (words 97.8%, max A/V lag 4 ms, captions median 0.05 s, -14.0 LUFS / -1.6 dBTP)
Flags     R04 at 03:12 restart? similarity 0.62, kept; "saves 10 hours" at 07:40 needs a source
Files     <outbox>/<slug>/<slug>.mp4, .srt, chapters.txt, PUBLISH.md, REVIEW.md, thumbs/
Looked at contact-sheet.jpg and cuts-sheet.jpg: clean. Nothing uploaded.
```

## When something is missing

- No whisper model: stop and print the download line above; or accept `--words` from any other transcriber.
- No opencv: runs anyway, but every silence is treated as idle (says so in run.log); install it for screen recordings.
- No `claude` CLI: heuristic chapter titles, flagged in PUBLISH.md as needing a rewrite.
- ffmpeg without libass: `--burn-captions` is skipped with a note; the SRT still ships (upload it as YouTube CC).
- Mic sync confidence below 4: stops and asks for a clap at the start or for the mic to be dropped.

## Recording habits that make the edit better

Say "retake" (or "let me say that again") as its own sentence before redoing a line: it is the only cue trusted to
cut a whole sentence. During a loading wait either stay silent with the cursor still (cut) or narrate (kept).
A silent stare at a static chart gets tightened: say something while showing it. One continuous take per file.

Optional stages (graphics panels, scene switching, style profiles, external camera sync, retake reveals) are
described in `references/optional-stages.md`; they are not in these scripts.

---

# SKILL ORIGINAL: raw-to-reel

# Raw to Reel

Five agents, run in order, turn `raw/<take>.mov` into `out/<take>/final.mp4`. This is the simple version. For the full engine (per-line layouts, synthesised SFX, more QA gates), use `../edit-style`. For setting up whisper.cpp in depth, see `../use-local-whisper`.

## Where it runs

- **Claude Code on macOS or Linux:** full pipeline. Needs ffmpeg, whisper.cpp (`whisper-cli`) with a model, Python 3 with Pillow and OpenCV, and Playwright with headless Chromium.
- **Claude.ai:** cannot open or export video. `plan` mode only: paste a transcript, get a tightened script, a shot plan and an .srt.

## Commands

| Command | Runs | Output |
|---|---|---|
| `setup` | tool check below | missing tools + install commands; installs only after the user says yes |
| `edit raw/<file>` | agents 1, 2, 4, 3, 5 | `out/<name>/` files below |
| `plan raw/<file>` or `plan` + pasted transcript | agent 1 (transcript, retakes), 2 (cut list only), 4 (shot plan only) | tables, no render |
| `check <file>` | agent 5 | `QA.md` |

Captions (agent 3) run after the visual build (agent 4) because layouts change caption position, and captions are always the top layer.

### `setup` check

```bash
ffmpeg -version | head -1; ffprobe -version | head -1
whisper-cli --help >/dev/null 2>&1 && echo WHISPER_OK || echo WHISPER_MISSING
ls ~/whisper-models/ggml-*.bin 2>/dev/null || echo NO_MODEL
python3 -c "import PIL, cv2; print('PY_OK')"
python3 -c "import playwright; print('PW_OK')"
```

Install commands to offer (macOS shown; on Linux use the distro package for ffmpeg and build whisper.cpp from source):

```bash
brew install ffmpeg whisper-cpp
mkdir -p ~/whisper-models && curl -L -o ~/whisper-models/ggml-small.en.bin \
  https://huggingface.co/ggerganov/whisper.cpp/resolve/main/ggml-small.en.bin
python3 -m pip install pillow opencv-python playwright && python3 -m playwright install chromium
```

Use `ggml-small.en` (about 466 MB) as the default; `ggml-base.en` if the machine is slow, multilingual `ggml-small` for non-English takes.

## Agents

Read the agent file before running that step. Each file is the only place its detailed rules live. In Claude Code you can copy them into `.claude/agents/` to run as real subagents.

1. `agents/01-footage-prep.md`: probe, colour fix, word-level transcript, retake table.
2. `agents/02-cutter.md`: removes earlier takes, dead air and pauses without clipping words.
3. `agents/03-captions.md`: every word captioned, timed from a transcript of the cut audio.
4. `agents/04-visual-planner.md`: face / graphic / split shot plan, real screenshots and logos, layered render.
5. `agents/05-qa-checker.md`: measured gates, contact sheet, claims list, READY or NOT READY.

If an agent fails, fix and rerun that agent only, then everything after it.

## Shared files

```
raw/<name>.mov              never modified
work/<name>/LOG.md          one line per finished step, so a stopped run can resume
work/<name>/words.json      agent 1 transcript (word, start, end)
work/<name>/cuts.csv        agent 2
work/<name>/shotplan.md     agent 4, with assets/SOURCES.md
out/<name>/final.mp4        1080x1920, H.264 + AAC, BT.709 limited range
out/<name>/captions.srt
out/<name>/contact.png      12 frames
out/<name>/QA.md
```

## Safety rules (every agent)

- Never modify or overwrite the raw file. One work folder per video.
- Never post or upload anything.
- Re-time the picture, never the voice: no speed changes on the audio to make something fit.
- Real things only on screen: real websites, real app screens, official logos. No generated UI, no fake dashboards, no pill badges, stickers or emoji.
- No private data on screen (inboxes, bank pages, client names, logged-in dashboards) unless the user supplied the image and confirmed it is safe.
- No number or claim as on-screen text without a source from the user.
- Browsers run headless only. Run renders with `nice -n 10`. If the user says they are recording, stop rendering.

## End-of-run output

```
RAW TO REEL: take1
1 Prep      4K HDR -> SDR BT.709, 212 words, 3 retakes found, kept last take of each
2 Cutter    58.4 s -> 41.2 s, 19 pause cuts, 3 retake cuts, 1 cut flagged at 00:17.3
4 Visuals   11 shots (4 FULL, 3 GFX, 4 SPLIT), 3 screenshots, 2 official logos
3 Captions  96 cues, 2 words unaligned (listed in QA.md)
5 QA        READY (8/8 gates PASS)  -> out/take1/QA.md
Decide:     "doubles your output" is said on camera with no source (CLAIMS list)
Nothing has been posted. Watch final.mp4 once before you upload it.
```

---

# SKILL ORIGINAL: pich

# pich

Raw talking-head video in, finished vertical Reel out. You (the agent) make the editorial decisions; the scripts do
the deterministic work and refuse unsafe input. Nothing is published or sent anywhere.

`<skill-dir>` below is the folder containing this file.

## /pich — autopilot

`/pich <video> [--lang tr|en|…] [--music FILE] [--no-broll] [--tone …] [free-text direction]`

Deliver a finished video without asking unless something is genuinely unknowable:

1. **First pass (automatic).** `python3 <skill-dir>/scripts/pich.py <video> --out-dir <project> --lang <lang> --plan-only`
   → `plan.json` (cuts, verified captions placed away from the face, music, loudness) + `review.md`.
   Project dir default: `pich-<video-stem>/` next to the video.
2. **Review the words.** Read `review.md` and `transcript.json`. Fix caption text in `plan.json` where the context
   makes the right word certain; if a name, number or claim is still uncertain, ask the user once (list all of them
   together) or cut that caption — never guess.
3. **Make it a Reel.** Edit `plan.json` (schema: [references/render-reel-plan.md](references/render-reel-plan.md)):
   - a hook card on frame 0 written from the video's own claim (`hook_card`, fits 860 px — the renderer rejects overflow),
   - 2–5 cards/icons for the specific ideas that are spoken ([references/visual-sources.md](references/visual-sources.md)),
   - numbers or steps → step slide, or a Hyperframes motion segment with live PiP ([references/motion-segments.md](references/motion-segments.md)),
   - a concrete scene → 1.5–3 s stock cutaway if `PEXELS_API_KEY` is set (skip with `--no-broll`),
   - a CTA on the quiet tail if the speaker leaves ~1.5 s at the end.
   Restraint beats decoration: every layer answers a spoken idea; nothing covers the eyes or mouth.
4. **Render + check.** Preview (`render_reel.py plan.json preview.mp4 --preview`), look at the layer boundaries, then
   final (`render_reel.py plan.json <stem>-pich.mp4`) and `qa_reel.py <final> --plan plan.json`. Fix every FAIL and
   re-render; mention every WARN.
5. **Hand over.** Tell the user the file path, length, what you added, and what they should check by ear/eye
   (QA warnings, words you were unsure of). Don't post or send it anywhere.

Without an agent: `python3 <skill-dir>/scripts/pich.py video.mov --lang tr --hook "EYEBROW|from|to" --cta "line|line|pill"`
renders the first pass straight to a checked video.

## Procedure

1. **Probe + speech map.** `ffprobe` the source (full-range phone/DJI footage → `source_range: "pc"`). It must be
   constant frame rate at the plan fps; conform VFR clips first (`ffmpeg -i in.mov -fps_mode cfr -r 30 out.mp4`).
   `python3 <skill-dir>/scripts/speech_map.py SRC --out speech_map.json` → speech phrases and pauses.
2. **Transcript.** `python3 <skill-dir>/scripts/transcribe.py SRC --out transcript.json`. Two whisper.cpp models
   (turbo + medium, `scripts/setup_models.sh`) plus an isolated per-phrase pass; resolve every entry in `suspects`
   by listening. Uncertain names or verbs are cut or confirmed with the speaker — never guessed.
3. **EDL.** One segment per kept phrase, ~0.12 s of air, cuts only where it is quiet. Verify with
   `python3 <skill-dir>/scripts/speech_map.py SRC --check-plan plan.json` → must print PASS (no edge above -45 dB).
   Extend an end until the breath tail has decayed rather than clipping it.
4. **Plan the layers** ([plan schema](references/render-reel-plan.md), [which visual for which idea](references/visual-sources.md)).
   All times are anchored to *source seconds inside a segment*, so re-cutting never slides a layer off its words.
   - Hook card visible on frame 0, below the top ~220 px of Instagram chrome.
   - Captions from the reviewed text; never split a number from its unit. If the face sits low in the frame, put
     captions above the head (`y` ≈ 0.30); per-caption `y` moves them off a slide.
   - Cards/icons only for a specific spoken idea; never over the eyes or mouth.
   - Concrete scene → 1.5–3 s stock cutaway (`broll` `full`, `scripts/stock.py`).
   - Numbers, steps or a product demo → step slide, or a brag-style Hyperframes motion segment as `broll` `full_pip`
     so the face stays on screen ([recipe](references/motion-segments.md), [template](examples/motion-template/)).
   - CTA card on a quiet tail segment.
5. **Hook A/B (optional).** `python3 <skill-dir>/scripts/hook_variants.py plan.json variants.json --out-dir DIR`
   → 3 s previews in parallel + a side-by-side `hooks.jpg`; `--pick NAME` writes the final plan.
   Write one warning, one result and one curiosity hook; sound cues and generated-shot prompts follow the same
   reference: [hooks-and-sound](references/hooks-and-sound.md).
6. **Preview, then final.** `python3 <skill-dir>/scripts/render_reel.py plan.json preview.mp4 --preview [--until S]`
   (540p; 4K sources use a cached half-res proxy). Look at every layer boundary, then render the final under a new
   name. The renderer refuses to overwrite and exits non-zero if frame count or A/V length is off.
7. **Auto QA.** `python3 <skill-dir>/scripts/qa_reel.py final.mp4 --plan plan.json` → `final.autoqa.json` + `.jpg`.
   FAIL: a layer over the eyes/mouth (macOS Vision on the clean source frame; slide/B-roll/PiP aware), a caption the
   speaker doesn't say (voice stem rebuilt from the EDL), voice offset > 40 ms, loudness off -14 ±1.5 LUFS or true
   peak > -1 dBTP, black last frame. WARN: music masking a caption, layer on the chin, estimated Instagram UI overlap,
   no hook on frame 0. Fix every FAIL and re-render.
8. **Human QA.** Watch on a phone with headphones. `AUTO_PASS` is not approval.

## Audio

Music is `{"synth": "pad"}` (generated, rights-free) or your own licensed file with `"rights_checked": true`.
Voice chain → music ducked by sidechain (≈ -24 dB for tracks with drums) → SFX → `loudnorm I=-14 TP=-1.5`.
`scripts/finish_audio.py` re-scores an already rendered video.

## Scripts

| Script | Role |
|---|---|
| `pich.py`, `auto_plan.py` | one-command pipeline; automatic first-pass plan with voted captions + review list |
| `render_reel.py` | plan.json → single-pass FFmpeg render; design presets, logos, icons, B-roll, proxy previews |
| `reel_graphics.py` | PIL templates: captions, hook/info card, step slide, image card, badge, wordmark, CTA |
| `qa_reel.py` + `face_detect.swift` | automatic QA report (face check needs macOS; skipped with a warning elsewhere) |
| `transcribe.py`, `speech_map.py` | verified transcript; speech phrases, pauses, cut-edge check |
| `hook_variants.py` | hook A/B previews and pick |
| `iconify.py` | 200k+ open-source icons (no key) as `icon` layers; license recorded per render |
| `stock.py` | Pexels photos/videos with a provenance sidecar (`PEXELS_API_KEY`) |
| `fetch_logos.py` + `svg2png.swift` | build your own verified logo library (Simple Icons; Brandfetch for owner-claimed brands) |
| `finish_audio.py` | optional audio re-mix of a finished video |
| `setup_models.sh` | download + SHA-1-verify the whisper.cpp models |

## Limits

Templates are laid out for 1080×1920. Instagram UI zones are an estimate — check on a phone. Face boxes are sampled
(every 0.5 s plus layer edges), not tracked. The repo ships no music and no brand logos.

## Verification

`python3 -m unittest discover -s <skill-dir>/tests -v` — synthetic FFmpeg fixtures, a golden filter graph for the
demo plan, QA that must FAIL on broken renders. Live-service tests skip without network/keys/models.
